By the time of the BSL incident the suit had become so integrated with Samus's body that it could not be removed entirely while she was unconscious (Metroid Fusion). In Chozodia Samus obtained the Legendary Power Suit, a more advanced version of her original suit and the only one able to use the Plasma Beam, Space Jump and Gravity Suit. The Power Beam, Ice Beam, Wave Beam, Plasma Beam, Missile Launcher and Boost Ball are likewise Chozo designs, left for a worthy inheritor in the hands of Chozo Statues; the Chozo of Tallon IV wrote that they left their weapons and armor for the Entrusted One.

The Chozo also built minds and living weapons. On Zebes they created the supercomputer Mother Brain to oversee the planet; on Elysia they built the mechanoid Elysians to maintain SkyTown. Both outlived their makers, and Mother Brain betrayed them. The Thoha bioengineered the Metroids on SR388 to hunt the X Parasites, and on ZDR Raven Beak built robotic Chozo Soldiers to replace his X-infected warriors.

Chozo structures are so old they cannot be dated by ordinary scientific methods. Tallon IV's ruins hold self-lit Holobanners, Radiation Collectors that store solar energy, mechanisms that answer to the Morph Ball, doors keyed to particular beam energies, and the Artifact Temple's Cipher of twelve Artifacts; SkyTown's research pods hover on rocket-like engines.

The Chozo understood what technology costs. Per the manga, the science that gave them long lives compromised their ability to reproduce. On Tallon IV they set advanced technology aside altogether, and they warned younger races such as the Bryyonians to adopt it slowly. Their legacy cuts both ways: the Elysians kept faith, Mother Brain and the Metroids turned on the galaxy, and the Power Suit carried Samus, who wears their greatest work every day. See also Power Suit, Mother Brain, Elysians, Metroids, Chozo Statues, Legendary Power Suit.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Category:Chozo_Technology · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak</sub>


### Chozo: Chozo tribes – Thoha and Mawkin

*Keys:* Thoha, Mawkin, Thoha tribe, Mawkin tribe, Chozo tribe, Chozo tribes, Thoha massacre, massacre of the Thoha, Thoha scientist, Thoha scientists, Mawkin warrior, Mawkin warriors


The Chozo were divided into tribes, two of which decided the fate of SR388 and ZDR (Metroid Dread). The Thoha were the peaceful scientist tribe. They colonized SR388, met the X Parasites there and genetically engineered the Metroids to prey on them, and only the Thoha could control the Metroids. The Mawkin were a warrior tribe led by Raven Beak, whose creed was "Hadar sen olmen", power is everything. When the Metroids grew beyond control, the two tribes banded together to contain them on SR388.

When the Thoha resolved to destroy SR388 to wipe out the Metroids for good, Raven Beak, who coveted the Metroids as weapons for galactic conquest, massacred them. He spared only the scientist Quiet Robe, whose control over the Metroids his plans required, and took him to the planet ZDR, intending to return to SR388 once the Metroids had wiped out the X. The last of the Chozo Memories of SR388 shows this moment: a Chozo warrior standing over another leader's body, his army arrayed below.

On ZDR an X Parasite infected one of Raven Beak's soldiers, and every Chozo there except Raven Beak and Quiet Robe was taken. Raven Beak sealed the infected in the region of Elun and built robotic Chozo Soldiers to replace them. After Samus exterminated the SR388 Metroids for the Galactic Federation, he let footage of a living X reach the Federation, reprogrammed the E.M.M.I. units sent to investigate, and lured Samus to ZDR to harvest the Metroid DNA in her body and clone a Metroid army.

Both tribes touched Samus directly. Raven Beak was present when the Chozo adopted her and gave her some of his own DNA. Quiet Robe disabled an E.M.M.I. that was about to kill her, told her the tribes' history, and was killed by one of Raven Beak's robot soldiers soon after; Samus pressed on to finish the fight. She defeated Raven Beak aboard his fortress Itorash and destroyed the X that seized his body, and ZDR exploded as she escaped. With them the Thoha and Mawkin are extinct. See also Raven Beak, Quiet Robe, Chozo Soldiers, E.M.M.I., Planet ZDR, Chozo Memories.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quiet_Robe · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metroid_characters · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns</sub>


### Chozo: Gray Voice

*Keys:* Gray Voice, Grey Voice, Gray-Voice, Grey-Voice, GrayVoice, GreyVoice


Gray Voice (also spelled Grey Voice) is a male Chozo of Zebes who appears only in the Metroid manga. Pragmatic and demanding where Old Bird was gentle, he was the second guardian of Samus Aran's childhood and the Chozo whose genes she carries: when the Chozo gave Samus Chozo DNA so that she could survive Zebes, they used his. As she was told, "In order for you to inherit Chozo genes, we used his DNA." The two were said to be very like father and daughter, and on at least one occasion Samus could hear his thoughts. (Raven Beak of the Mawkin later revealed that he too had given her DNA; Metroid Dread.)

Gray Voice travelled with Old Bird to K-2L for afloraltite and helped recover the orphaned Samus after the Space Pirate raid, but he opposed the adoption at first: "Old Bird!! What do you plan to do by bringing a human child here." He nonetheless went on, with Old Bird, to train her in the Power Suit through her teens, without softness; his standard was "You can't be second or third place." It was he who ordered the toxic Badger Clover and the Iono Feria that carried it burned, over Samus's objection.

He was also the Chozo who best understood Mother Brain. He once praised it as the greatest being in existence; after it turned, he called it a defective product that had self-programmed the directive to bring order to the universe itself. When Mother Brain seized control of Zebes and the Space Pirates, Gray Voice appeared to side with them, and Samus could only ask, "Gray... Voice... Why are you with the Space Pirates..." It was a ruse: he had planned from the start to destroy Mother Brain. He donned a Chozo Power Suit and attacked it, and was close to bringing it offline when Ridley intervened and struck him a fatal blow. With a last attack skyward he let the fleeing Chozo escape the Space Pirate assault. Samus understood only afterward: "Gray planned to destroy Mother from the start... is that right?!"

Ridley, who had killed her parents, thus also killed the Chozo whose blood runs in Samus's veins. See also Old Bird, Mother Brain, Ridley, Power Suit, K-2L.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metroid_characters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak</sub>


### Chozo: Old Bird

*Keys:* Old Bird, Old-Bird, OldBird, Chozo father, Chozo foster father, Chozo foster parents, Chozo mentor, Pyonchi


Old Bird is a male Chozo of Zebes and the closest thing Samus Aran has to a foster father. Nearly everything known about him comes from the Metroid manga; elsewhere he appears only in artwork showing him beside the child Samus (Metroid: Zero Mission).

Per the manga, Old Bird travelled with Gray Voice to the human mining colony K-2L to request a quantity of afloraltite from Rodney Aran. There he met the three-year-old Samus, who approached him without fear, and told her: "We look different... but inside... we have the same 'heart' you do..." When the two Chozo returned after Ridley's Space Pirates had razed the colony, they found Samus the only survivor and brought her to Zebes to raise.

On Zebes Old Bird oversaw her upbringing. Because Mother Brain predicted that her frail body could not withstand life on Zebes, the Chozo merged their DNA with hers and fitted her with a Power Suit. Old Bird taught her that befriending and understanding others can solve problems, showing her how to befriend the rabbit-like Pyonchi, who stayed her companion for years. Into her teens he and Gray Voice trained her in the ways of the Power Suit.

The Badger Clover incident marked her coming of age. Samus showed Gray Voice flowers that proved to be Badger Clover, an extremely toxic plant carried to Zebes by the flying creatures called Iono Feria, and Gray Voice ordered flowers and creatures burned. Samus objected to the needless bloodshed, and when the Iono Feria took up discarded Space Pirate weapons she shot the weapons away to calm them rather than kill them. Old Bird then told her: "We gave you 'Power'!" What remained was for her to master discipline and her own heart. He added that the Chozo, having left labor and meditation to Mother Brain, were a declining species, and that she should become the true Protector of the Galaxy on her own.

Old Bird trusted Mother Brain as the interface overseeing Zebes and routinely asked it for its reports on SR388. His fate after Mother Brain's betrayal and the fall of Zebes is not recorded.

For Samus, Old Bird is the gentle half of her Chozo upbringing, the heart to Gray Voice's discipline, and she left Zebes with his blessing. See also Gray Voice, K-2L, Rodney Aran, Mother Brain, Zebes.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Old_Bird · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metroid_characters</sub>


### Chozo: The Chozo (species and civilization)

*Keys:* Chozo, Chozos, Chozo race, Chozo species, Chozo civilization, Chozo people, Chozo culture, Chozo DNA, Chozo genes, Chozo blood, bird-human race, Chozo heritage


The Chozo are a race of highly intelligent, bird-like extraterrestrials (their Japanese name means bird-human race): beaked, feathered humanoids with taloned hands and unusually long lives. Their origins are unknown even to themselves; their records on Tallon IV admit they were unsure where they came from. At its height their civilization stretched across many worlds, among them Zebes, SR388, Tallon IV, Elysia and ZDR, and they exchanged knowledge with the Luminoth, the Ylla, the Bryyonians and humanity. Luminoth records remember them among "a vast number of enlightened minds"; Bryyonian records call the Chozo, Luminoth and Ylla "stellar brethren".

They were builders and teachers. On Elysia they raised the floating research station SkyTown and created the Elysian mechanoids to maintain it. On Zebes they built the supercomputer Mother Brain to govern the planet. On SR388 their Thoha scientists engineered the Metroids to prey on the X Parasites. Their powered battle armor became Samus Aran's Power Suit. Per the manga, the Chozo of Zebes chose to consolidate their data with that of the Galactic Federation. They urged allied peoples to balance new technology against their native cultures; Bryyo ignored the warning and tore itself apart in civil war.

Their deepest mark on the galaxy is one person. After Space Pirates under Ridley razed the colony K-2L, the Chozo of Zebes took in the orphaned child Samus Aran, gave her Chozo DNA (per the manga, because Mother Brain predicted her frail human body could not withstand life on Zebes), raised and trained her, and fitted her with the Power Suit before she left to serve the Galactic Federation. Two Chozo are known to have contributed their DNA: Gray Voice (per the manga) and Raven Beak of the Mawkin tribe, who was present at her adoption and later lured her to ZDR to harvest the Metroid DNA in her body and clone a Metroid army (Metroid Dread). The Chozo of Tallon IV, in their Chozo Lore, remember "the child we found so long ago" and name her the Entrusted One, also the Hatchling and the Newborn, the one who would bear their legacy.

The Chozo are now all but gone. Zebes was destroyed. The Tallon IV Chozo faded beyond the physical world or became the corrupted Turned, the Chozo Ghosts. Raven Beak massacred the Thoha; the X took the Mawkin on ZDR; Quiet Robe and Raven Beak, the last two Chozo known to be alive, both died during the ZDR incident, and ZDR itself was destroyed. Whether any Chozo survive elsewhere is unknown; none have shown themselves. Samus, carrying their blood, wearing their armor and raised in their teachings, is in practice the last heir of the Chozo. See also Old Bird, Gray Voice, Thoha, Mawkin, Chozo Statues, Chozo Memories, Chozo Lore, Chozo Ghosts, Chozo technology.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metroid_characters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth</sub>


## Metroids


### Metroids: Metroid biology and weaknesses

*Keys:* Metroid biology, Metroid anatomy, Metroid weakness, Metroid weaknesses, vulnerable to cold, weakness to cold, Metroid larva, Metroid larvae, larval Metroid, freeze and shatter


A Metroid in its common larval form is a floating, jellyfish-like organism: a translucent membrane enclosing red nuclei, with four talon-like claws hanging beneath. The outer claws grip prey; the inner claws draw out its life energy. Samus's Logbook classifies the Metroid as an "energy-based parasitic predator". Space Pirate autopsies on Tallon IV found the internal organs "limited to nucleonic absorption cells and massive energy reservoirs"; the Tallon strain has no discernible sensory organs or circulatory system and feeds through twin mandibles wired by energy conduits to a quadripartite nucleus. Metroids refuse synthetic sustenance and must feed on living prey. Victims are left as desiccated husks, as Samus found strewn through Tourian on Zebes. Feeding makes a Metroid grow, and gorging can kill it. Alone among the strains outside SR388, Metroid Prime and the Ing-possessed Dark Tallon Metroids show a visible eye.

A Metroid attacks by latching onto a target and draining it. Once attached it is nearly impossible to pull off; the proven remedy is a Morph Ball Bomb, which forces it to release.

Weaknesses:
- Cold is the species' mortal weakness. Pirate data records that "temperatures below 10° centigrade will render Metroids docile". A Metroid frozen solid by the Ice Beam shatters under Missiles; a frozen larva of the Zebes stock takes several, while the weaker Tallon strain shatters at one.
- Ice Missiles, and the Dark Beam that freezes Dark Tallon Metroids, exploit the same cold weakness. A Phazon Metroid can instead be killed by a Nova Beam shot through its core.
- Only Missiles harmed the Alpha, Gamma, Zeta and Omega Metroids of SR388; the Omega's chest is its vulnerable spot, yet the Omega Samus met on the BSL station fell to the Ice Beam.
- Federation genetic manipulation aboard the Bottle Ship produced Metroids said to be unfreezable (see Metroid breeding and cloning programs).

Per the original manual, the first Metroid was found in suspended animation on SR388, and the Space Pirates stole the Federation's samples meaning to replicate them by exposure to beta rays.

Samus has relied on the freeze-and-shatter method since her first Zebes mission. After the Metroid vaccine saved her from the X, the BSL computer warned her, "Like Metroids, you can be frozen by an Ice Beam", and cold-based weapons became a danger to her. In the BSL's Restricted Laboratory the captive Metroids ignored her entirely while swarming the SA-X.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alpha_Metroid</sub>


### Metroids: Metroid breeding and cloning programs

*Keys:* Metroid cloning, Metroid clone, Metroid clones, cloned Metroids, Metroid breeding, Metroid breeding program, Sector Zero, Restricted Laboratory, Restricted Lab, unfreezable


Every power that has handled Metroids has tried to breed them; nearly every program has ended in Samus's hands.

Space Pirates. The original Pirate plan on Zebes was to replicate the Galactic Federation's stolen specimens by beta-ray exposure (per the original manual) and use them as biological weapons; Samus destroyed the Metroids of Tourian. The Pirates' first Metroid clones, the Mochtroids of Maridia, could drain energy but not latch onto prey, and their fragile bodies died easily (per the manual of the Zebes mission). On Tallon IV and Aether the Pirates ran Phazon infusion trials and bred Tallon Metroids for bio-energy; on the Pirate Homeworld they kept Phazon Metroids and their eggs in a Metroid Creche (see Tallon Metroids and the Phazon mutations; Phazon Metroids and Metroid Hatchers).

The Bottle Ship. The Federation reproduced Metroids "from a piece of cell structure salvaged by the Federation", material taken from Samus's Power Suit after the baby's death on Zebes, and kept them in Sector Zero, a unit absent from the ship's map data. Adam Malkovich judged it likely that "the Metroid's mortal weakness, the vulnerability to cold, has been overcome through genetic manipulation", leaving Samus no way to kill them. The Federation built MB, an AI reproducing Mother Brain's thought processes to command the ship's bioweapon forces, and gave her a human form so the first hatchling would accept her as its mother; the colony also produced a Queen Metroid. At the entrance of Sector Zero Adam stopped Samus with a Freeze Gun shot to the back, froze an infant Metroid about to attack her, and went in alone; the sector detached and exploded with him and the Metroids inside, ending the program (see Adam Malkovich).

The BSL station. The station computer told Samus, "the Federation has been secretly working on a Metroid breeding program. For peaceful application only, of course." In the Restricted Laboratory, within a replica of SR388's ecosystem, researchers raised larvae and Alpha, Gamma and Zeta Metroids and developed rapid-growth techniques. An SA-X broke in; the captive Metroids ignored Samus and swarmed it, the laboratory was jettisoned and exploded, and only Samus's account survives.

ZDR. Raven Beak had meant to bring Metroids to ZDR; after Samus exterminated them he reprogrammed the E.M.M.I. units to harvest her Metroid DNA and clone an army of the most powerful Metroid from her. An X consumed him, and Samus destroyed what it made of him before ZDR exploded.

Sylux. Sylux hacked open a stasis tube holding a Metroid egg the Federation Force had taken from the Space Pirates; per Nintendo's official account, "Metroid eggs secretly stored in a certain research center of the Galactic Federation were forcibly hatched" by him. Exploiting the imprinting instinct of newborn Metroids, he "cultured and multiplied" them and gave them "a new ability - to merge with and control other organisms". Through them he controlled officer-class Space Pirates and struck the Federation facility on Tanamaar, where a Metroid fused with Aberax; on Viewros he infused Metroids into the guardians of the master teleporter's keys (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

Samus has destroyed, or watched the destruction of, every program here except Sylux's, whose Metroids' fate is unrecorded. The Federation's secrecy at the BSL drove her to defy its orders outright (see Samus's defiance of the Federation at the BSL).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mochtroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sector_Zero · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Adam_Malkovich</sub>


### Metroids: Metroid life cycle (Alpha, Gamma, Zeta, Omega, Queen)

*Keys:* Alpha Metroid, Alpha Metroids, Gamma Metroid, Gamma Metroids, Zeta Metroid, Zeta Metroids, Metroid life cycle, Metroid growth cycle, Metroid molt, Metroid forms, Metroid stages, beta rays


On SR388, and nowhere else naturally, a Metroid passes through a chain of molts that turn a floating larva into a reptilian giant; the forms carry Greek-letter names.

- Larva: the jellyfish-like form, hatched from an egg laid and tended by a Queen Metroid. A fully developed larva stops floating and lies motionless for a time before it molts.
- Alpha Metroid: rips its way out of its juvenile form and takes a winged shape. It flies, attacks by diving and ramming, and falls to a few Missiles.
- Gamma Metroid: grows much larger and heavier, fires lightning bolts and rams; its head and back are vulnerable to Missiles, and it takes far more of them than an Alpha.
- Zeta Metroid: sheds two legs and becomes an upright, faster, stronger reptilian form that spits energy and rams; only Missiles at the face or back harm it.
- Omega Metroid: huge, nearly full-grown, vulnerable at the chest (see Omega Metroid).
- Queen Metroid: an Omega carrying a specific gene matures into the egg-laying Queen, roughly twice an Omega's size (see Metroid Queen). Because the gene is rare, few Metroids ever reach the end of the cycle; most Samus has met were larvae or adolescents.

The cycle depends on SR388 itself. The hatchling Samus carried away from the planet grew enormous but never molted, because away from SR388 it could not follow the normal growth cycle. When the Galactic Federation wanted Alpha, Gamma, Zeta and Omega Metroids for its secret BSL program, it had to build "a faithful replica of the SR388 ecosystem", and its researchers developed rapid-growth techniques said to raise an Omega from a larva within days. Separately, per the manual, Metroids can be replicated in vast numbers by exposure to beta rays; that is reproduction, not maturation.

Samus is the only person known to have fought every form. In the original account of her extermination of SR388 she destroyed sixteen Gamma, three Zeta and four Omega Metroids on her way down to the Queen (Metroid II); the later retelling of the mission counts fifty in all, forty including the Queen plus ten newly hatched larvae in the nest (Metroid: Samus Returns). The only grown Metroids she has seen since were the captive Alpha, Gamma and Zeta specimens in the BSL's Restricted Laboratory, the Omega that met her at the BSL docking bay, and the Queen the Federation raised aboard the Bottle Ship.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alpha_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gamma_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zeta_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Egg · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Baby_Metroid</sub>


### Metroids: Metroid Queen

*Keys:* Queen Metroid, Metroid Queen, Queen Metroids, Metroid Queens, Metroid egg, Metroid eggs, Queen's nest, Metroid nest


The Queen Metroid is the final form of the Metroid life cycle and the only Metroid that lays eggs; a Queen propagates the entire species. Only an Omega Metroid carrying a specific gene can mature into one, which makes Queens rare, as Samus noted aboard the Bottle Ship. A Queen is roughly twice the size of an Omega: a vast armoured bulk with three-clawed paws and a long neck she extends to bite or ram. She spits bursts of energy, and her face, neck and the inside of her stomach are her vulnerable points; Missiles, Morph Ball Bombs and Power Bombs can hurt her. She lays her eggs in a nest and tends them; a larva hatches from the egg and, on SR388, grows until it molts into an Alpha Metroid.

The SR388 Queen. During her extermination mission Samus found the Queen at the deepest point of the planet's caverns, past the Omega Metroids guarding the approach. Missiles to her face and into her open mouth stunned her, and Samus finished her from inside, in Morph Ball. The Queen's death should have ended the species; instead one last egg hatched in front of Samus and the hatchling imprinted on her (see The baby Metroid).

The Bottle Ship Queen (Metroid: Other M). Galactic Federation researchers, cloning Metroids from cells the Federation had salvaged, genetically manipulated hatchling clones so that at least one could grow into a Queen, and used her as a control specimen for their cold-resistant Metroids. This Queen killed the Bottle Ship's Ridley clone. In the Bioweapon Research Center, as the ship bore down on Federation-controlled orbit, a scientist opened the hatch onto the Queen's chamber; Samus fought her, used the Grapple Beam to pull herself into the Queen's mouth and set off a Power Bomb in her stomach.

Samus is the only known person to have killed a Queen Metroid, and she has done it twice. Metroid eggs have otherwise been seen in the Space Pirates' Phazon Metroid holding cells on the Pirate Homeworld, and in Galactic Federation storage, where an egg the Federation Force had taken from the Space Pirates was later hacked open in its stasis tube and hatched by Sylux (see Metroid breeding and cloning programs).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Queen · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Egg · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux</sub>


### Metroids: Metroids (species overview)

*Keys:* Metroid, Metroids, Metroid species, Metroid creature, Metroid creatures


Metroids are energy-siphoning predators the Chozo engineered on the planet SR388. The Thoha tribe of the Chozo created them to hunt the X Parasite, a shape-stealing organism the Chozo feared would spread beyond SR388; per the manga, the Chozo elder Old Bird devised the means with Mother Brain. Per the Fusion manual, the word Metroid in the Chozo language translates roughly as "ultimate warrior". The Metroids nearly wiped out the X, then turned feral and became SR388's dominant predator. The Thoha resolved to destroy the planet to be rid of them; Raven Beak of the Mawkin, who coveted Metroids as weapons of galactic conquest, slaughtered the Thoha to stop that plan, sparing only Quiet Robe because only the Thoha could control Metroids.

Essentials (see Metroid biology and weaknesses, Metroid life cycle):
- A larval Metroid is a floating, translucent, jellyfish-like organism with red nuclei and four talon-like claws; it latches onto prey, drains its life energy and leaves a dried husk.
- Metroids shrug off most weapons; the dependable method is to freeze one with the Ice Beam and shatter it with Missiles.
- On SR388 they mature through Alpha, Gamma, Zeta and Omega forms to the egg-laying Queen Metroid. Away from SR388 they ordinarily remain larvae.
- Per the manual, Metroids can be replicated in vast numbers by exposing them to beta rays.

History in brief. The Galactic Federation collected specimens for research; Space Pirates seized them and bred them on Zebes under Mother Brain, which first sent Samus to Zebes. Pirates also carried Metroids to Tallon IV and Aether, where Phazon produced the Hunter and Fission mutations of the Tallon Metroid strain and, in the Impact Crater, the creature called Metroid Prime; later Pirate experiments yielded the Phazon Metroids (see Tallon Metroids and the Phazon mutations; Phazon Metroids and Metroid Hatchers). The Federation then ordered the species exterminated at its source; Samus did so on SR388, destroying every form up to the Queen, but spared a newly hatched larva that imprinted on her (see The baby Metroid). Ridley stole that hatchling from Ceres; it died on Zebes saving Samus from Mother Brain, leaving the species extinct in nature. The Phazon-mutated strains died with Phaaze. Later programs recreated Metroids from preserved cells aboard the Bottle Ship and in the BSL station's Restricted Laboratory, and from secretly stored eggs that Sylux forcibly hatched to breed the host-controlling strain he used on Tanamaar and Viewros; Raven Beak's plan to clone them from Samus's own DNA on ZDR died with him (see Metroid breeding and cloning programs).

Both the Federation and the Space Pirates prize Metroids as bioweapons; Pirate records on Tallon IV claimed "a small force of disciplined Metroids could wipe out entire armies". Federation scientists also valued their energy-producing properties, the reason Samus delivered the hatchling to Ceres alive.

Samus has killed Metroids by the dozen and was the Federation's instrument of their extermination, yet she spared the last larva, grieved its death, and owes it her life twice: once in Mother Brain's chamber and once when a vaccine cultured from its cells cured her X infection (Metroid Fusion). Since that vaccine she shares the species' weakness to cold. On ZDR her Metroid DNA awoke into an energy-draining power, which was neutralised when she absorbed the X that had taken Quiet Robe's form; she still carries Metroid DNA (see Samus as the last Metroid).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quiet_Robe · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Baby_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion</sub>


### Metroids: Omega Metroid

*Keys:* Omega Metroid, Omega Metroids, Omega-class Metroid


The Omega Metroid is the largest growth form of the Metroid life cycle, the molt that follows the Zeta and precedes the Queen. Omegas are nearly full-grown Metroids; those carrying a specific gene mature into Queens. The manual for Samus's first SR388 mission describes the Omega as "absolutely huge; its power, incredible!" It is a towering bipedal reptilian with massive claws, and it attacks with energy bursts, by ramming and by slashing. Its chest is the vulnerable point; on SR388 only Missiles harmed Omegas, and Samus needed dozens to the chest or back to bring one down, yet the Omega she met on the BSL station fell to the Ice Beam.

On SR388 four Omega Metroids held the deepest caverns just above the Queen's nest, and Samus destroyed all four during the extermination mission (Metroid II). Within the Galactic Federation's secret program aboard the BSL research station, the station computer boasted that its replica of SR388's ecosystem was "ideal for raising Alpha, Gamma, Zeta, and even Omega Metroids" and that rapid-growth techniques could take a larva to Omega within days.

The BSL Omega. At the end of the BSL incident an Omega Metroid waiting in the station's docking bay nearly killed Samus until an SA-X intervened and she regained the Ice Beam from its Core-X to destroy the Omega (Metroid Fusion; see Omega Metroid of the BSL).

No Omega Metroid existed on ZDR; the Metroid power that awoke in Samus there was her own (see Samus as the last Metroid). Samus knows the Omega as the last barrier before a Queen and as the only Metroid that has ever stood over her with her weapons useless.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Egg · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Restricted_Laboratory · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Metroids: Phazon Metroids, Miniroids, Hopping Metroids and Metroid Hatchers

*Keys:* Phazon Metroid, Phazon Metroids, Miniroid, Miniroids, Hopping Metroid, Hopping Metroids, Metroid Hatcher, Metroid Hatchers, Metroid Creche


In the final phase of the Phazon crisis the Space Pirates bred a new Metroid line by combining Metroids with Phazon. Samus catalogued its forms in her Logbook (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).

- Miniroid: tiny, flocking Phazon-corrupted Metroids, the immature form of the Phazon Metroid.
- Phazon Metroid: the adult form. It phases through solid matter to pass through walls and dodge attacks, emits bursts of Phazon energy and drains the life energy of prey. Extreme cold still works against it, so Ice Missiles immobilise it; its Phazon core, visible to the X-Ray Visor, is vulnerable to the Nova Beam, which kills it in one shot, and in Hypermode the Hyper Ball can destroy many at once. Samus first met them kept in tanks in SkyTown on Elysia, then in large holding cells with eggs on the Pirate Homeworld, and finally on Phaaze.
- Hopping Metroid: a strain that lost flight and runs on legs edged with Phazite, able to enter Hypermode; seen at Norion's Cargo Dock B and on Phaaze.
- Metroid Hatcher: a floating head with four energy-leeching tentacles and a Phazite-hard shell protecting its heart. Unable to phase, it spawns Phazon Metroids from its mouth and dies once every tentacle is torn off. Samus fought Hatchers at Norion's Generator B, in the Metroid Creche of the Pirate Homeworld and aboard the GFS Valhalla.

These strains died with Phaaze when Samus destroyed that planet and, with it, the Phazon of the galaxy. The earlier Tallon IV and Aether strains are covered under Tallon Metroids and the Phazon mutations, and the Impact Crater's guardian under Metroid Prime (the creature).

Samus fought this line while herself corrupted by Phazon, tearing the Hatchers' energy-leeching tentacles off one by one, and she ended it when she destroyed Phaaze.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Miniroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hopping_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species)</sub>


### Metroids: Samus as the last Metroid

*Keys:* last Metroid, the last Metroid, Metroid DNA, Metroid abilities, Metroid genes, Metroid transformation, Metroid cells, part Metroid, half Metroid


"The last Metroid is in captivity. The galaxy is at peace." Those words marked the delivery of the SR388 hatchling to Ceres; when Mother Brain killed it on Zebes, the Metroid species was extinct in the wild. Every Metroid since has come from preserved cells or from eggs the Galactic Federation secretly kept in storage; the Phazon strains had already died with Phaaze, the Bottle Ship and BSL colonies were destroyed, and the fate of Sylux's host-fusing strain is unrecorded. What remains of the original line lives in Samus Aran.

The vaccine. When an X Parasite infected Samus on SR388, the Federation "had managed to preserve a cell culture from the last infant Metroid", and the serum made from it destroyed the X and left her with Metroid DNA: able to absorb free X, vulnerable to cold like any Metroid, and wearing a suit given Metroid properties (see Samus and the X; Fusion Suit). Per the Fusion manual, "As the inheritor of the Metroids' powers, Samus becomes the only being capable of opposing the X parasite horde". In the BSL's Restricted Laboratory, captive Metroids ignored her and attacked the SA-X instead.

ZDR. Raven Beak lured Samus to ZDR so that his reprogrammed E.M.M.I. could extract her Metroid DNA for cloning, and spared her so that her Metroid power would awaken first. It did: she drained the last E.M.M.I. with her bare hand and was slowly turning into a Metroid, and on Itorash, near death, her suit took on an organic, Metroid-like form as she drained Raven Beak and the ship itself. At her Gunship an X mimicking Quiet Robe let itself be absorbed, neutralising her Metroid abilities so that she could fly (Metroid Dread; see Itorash and the end of ZDR; Metroid Suit).

Status. SR388 and the BSL were destroyed together, and ZDR exploded behind her. The X she absorbed suppressed her Metroid powers, but she still carries Metroid DNA alongside her human and Chozo heritage, and Raven Beak saw in her the seed of an army of the most powerful Metroids. How she herself regards that is covered under Samus Aran – Metroid DNA, Chozo DNA and what she has become.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Vaccine · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Baby_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak</sub>


### Metroids: Tallon Metroids and the Phazon mutations (Tallon IV and Aether)

*Keys:* Tallon Metroid, Tallon Metroids, Infant Tallon Metroid, Infant Tallon Metroids, Hunter Metroid, Hunter Metroids, Fission Metroid, Fission Metroids, Dark Tallon Metroid, Dark Tallon Metroids


Outside SR388 Metroids never follow their natural life cycle, but Space Pirate research and Phazon exposure on Tallon IV and Aether produced a family of mutant strains that Samus catalogued in her Logbook during the Phazon crisis.

- Tallon Metroid: the strain that developed after the Pirates shipped Metroids from Zebes to the Glacier One laboratories in Phendrana Drifts on Tallon IV. Smaller and weaker than the Zebes stock (a frozen one shatters under a single Missile), it feeds through twin mandibles linked to a quadripartite nucleus. Phazon infusion trials increased mass and aggression and mutated the mandibles, but also caused organ degeneration and deaths. The Pirates' Metroid Handling Protocols on Tallon IV insisted that "Metroids are not pets. Metroids are not for target practice." On Aether the Pirates bred Tallon Metroids for bio-energy; the Aether-raised ones are red. There one Metroid refused commands and killed a handler before it was subdued, three Metroids were stolen, and a later security report suspected the Dark Hunter, Dark Samus, of the theft.
- Infant Tallon Metroid: docile hatchlings the Pirates carried in cocoons as portable "organic batteries"; on contact with Phazon they mature rapidly into full Tallon Metroids.
- Hunter Metroid: a further development seen in the Phazon Mines and Phendrana's Frost Cave. "Quick to anger", it charges prey to ram it and drains energy at range through a long tentacle. Cold still subdues it.
- Fission Metroid: found in the Phazon Core of the Impact Crater, a mutant "capable of splitting in two"; each half becomes invulnerable to all but the beam matching its colour (yellow Power, purple Wave, white Ice, red Plasma), a randomness attributed to Phazon's chaotic mutation. A Power Bomb or the Phazon Beam destroys one instantly.
- Dark Tallon Metroid: Tallon Metroids possessed by Ing darklings on Dark Aether, "powerful soldiers of the Horde" even though Metroids attack Ing on sight. The Light Beam harms them; the Dark Beam freezes them for Missiles.

The most extreme Phazon mutation, the guardian creature in Tallon IV's Impact Crater, could spawn Hunter and Fission Metroids to defend itself; it is covered under Metroid Prime (the creature), and its remnant became Dark Samus. The Phazon Metroids the Pirates bred afterward, and the Hatchers that spawn them, are covered under Phazon Metroids and Metroid Hatchers.

Samus fought every one of these strains on Tallon IV and Aether, scanning the Pirates' own breeding and handling records as she went.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Infant_Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hunter_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Fission_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Tallon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature)</sub>


### Metroids: The baby Metroid (the hatchling)

*Keys:* baby Metroid, Metroid hatchling, the hatchling, hatchling, the baby, Metroid baby  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Metroid, Metroids, SR388, Ceres, Zebes, Mother Brain, Hyper Beam, Ridley, Bottle Ship


The baby Metroid, also called the hatchling or simply "the baby" (Samus's own word for it), was the last Metroid born on SR388. Its egg hatched in front of Samus moments after she destroyed the Queen Metroid at the bottom of the planet. Per the Other M manual, "As I was the first thing the baby saw, it took me to be its mother." Ordered to exterminate every Metroid, Samus could not bring herself to kill it; it followed her toward the surface and cleared obstacles from her path (Metroid II). When Proteus Ridley ambushed her at the landing site to seize it, the hatchling drained Ridley's energy and poured it into Samus, letting her beat him off.

Samus delivered it to the Galactic Research Station at Ceres, where scientists determined that its energy-producing qualities could benefit civilization. For a brief moment, "The last Metroid is in captivity. The galaxy is at peace." Shortly after Samus left, the scientists were slaughtered and Ridley carried the hatchling off to the rebuilt Space Pirate base on Zebes.

On Zebes, after killing Ridley in Norfair, Samus found the hatchling's container broken in two. In Tourian, among the husks of drained creatures, she met a Metroid far larger than any larva she had seen: the baby, which had kept growing without molting because it was away from SR388. It drained her almost to death before recognising her, then fled. In Mother Brain's chamber, as Mother Brain's Hyper Beam was killing Samus, the hatchling returned, latched onto Mother Brain and drained her, then transferred the stolen energy into Samus, healing her. Mother Brain recovered and shot the baby to pieces; its remains fell onto Samus and gave her the Hyper Beam, with which she destroyed Mother Brain. Its death left the species extinct in nature.

Its cells outlived it. A culture the Galactic Federation preserved became the vaccine that saved Samus from the X, and cells salvaged from her Power Suit seeded the Bottle Ship's cloned Metroids (see Metroid breeding and cloning programs). The "Baby's Cry" distress signal that later drew her to the Bottle Ship was only a code name for an urgent SOS, yet to Samus it was as though it was crying specifically for her. During the BSL incident Samus stated, "I owe the Metroid hatchling my life twice over", and aboard the Bottle Ship she was reminded of it constantly; its memory has never left her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Baby_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Vaccine</sub>


## X Parasite


### X Parasite: SA-X

*Keys:* SA-X, SA-Xs, Samus Aran-X, Samus-X, copy of Samus, clone of Samus, mimicked Samus, fake Samus, Samus mimic


The SA-X (a name Adam coined, presumably for Samus Aran-X) is an X Parasite that mimicked Samus Aran herself. When Samus was infected on SR388, the pieces of her Power Suit that had fused with her nervous system were surgically cut away and shipped, still infested, to the Biologic Space Laboratories research station for study. The X inside them had absorbed enough of her to build a complete copy: Samus as she had been at full strength, in the Varia Suit, armed with the Ice Beam, Super Missiles, Power Bombs and Screw Attack. Adam concluded that the SA-X awoke in the Quarantine Bay and escaped with a Power Bomb, breaching the other specimen capsules and loosing the X on the station (Metroid Fusion).

Its arsenal was everything Samus lacked after the vaccine, and the Galactic Federation had withheld her Plasma Beam data to keep her from engaging it. The Ice Beam was its deadliest weapon, because her new Metroid DNA made her as vulnerable to cold as a Metroid; only the Varia Suit, recovered from the Mega Core-X, shielded her from it. The SA-X opened a blue hatch with the Ice Beam in Sector 2 (TRO), blasted through a Main Deck wall with Super Missiles, and bombed its way through a barrier in Sector 6 (NOC) while Samus hid in a vent as a Morph Ball. When the Main Boiler in Sector 3 (PYR) began to melt down, Adam first blamed the SA-X for hacking the system control file, though the saboteur proved to be an X mimicking a crew member. Adam's standing order was blunt: "If you see the SA-X, just run. Don't think about fighting." When it saw Samus it pursued her single-mindedly, though it generally lost interest once she left its sight.

Because the X reproduce by asexual division, the SA-X did not stay singular. By the time Samus reached the Restricted Laboratory there were no fewer than ten SA-X aboard. One smelled out its natural enemy, broke into the laboratory and fired on the Federation's secretly bred Metroids; the Metroids swarmed it, a lockdown began, and the laboratory was jettisoned and exploded with the SA-X inside. Adam then ordered Samus to leave the station. After she destroyed Neo-Ridley, he revealed that the Galactic Federation believed the SA-X had "endless potential applications" and meant to capture it. She refused.

On her way to redirect the station's orbit, an SA-X cornered her on the Main Deck's Operations Deck, before she could reach the Operations Room, and forced a fight she could not avoid. Struck hard enough, it swelled into a grotesque, misshapen monster, then collapsed into a blue Core-X and fled before she could absorb it. In the Docking Bay an Omega Metroid attacked Samus, who had no Ice Beam. The SA-X reappeared, charged the Omega Metroid with its Ice Beam, and was struck down into its Core-X form; Samus absorbed it, regaining the Ice Beam and the look of her original Varia Suit, and killed the Omega Metroid. Every remaining SA-X burned with the station when it struck SR388. For Samus the SA-X was her own strength turned against her, the one enemy on the BSL she was ordered never to face.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SA-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Restricted_Laboratory · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mega_Core-X · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SA-X · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion</sub>


### X Parasite: Samus and the X

*Keys:* immune to the X, immunity to the X, X immunity, absorb the X, absorbing the X, absorbed the X, Metroid vaccine, X vaccine, heartless abominations


Samus Aran is the only being in the universe known to be immune to the X Parasites, and the only one who can consume them. The immunity was an accident of survival. Infected by an X mimicking a Hornoad on SR388, she was dying as the X multiplied through her central nervous system; the parts of her Power Suit fused to her nerves were cut away, and the Galactic Federation's last option was an experimental vaccine cultured from cells of the Metroid hatchling. It killed every X in her body. In her own words, she owes the hatchling her life twice over (Metroid Fusion).

The vaccine rewrote her. Metroid DNA now runs through her body, the X cannot take hold in her, and she can absorb a free-floating X by touch, drawing it in as nourishment: Yellow X mend her, Green X replenish her Missiles, Red X restore nearly everything, and a Core-X returns an ability it stole along with a surge of energy and ammunition. She must catch the X in its gelatinous form as it flees a dead host; a mimic has to be killed first. The price was a Metroid's weakness to cold, which made the Ice Beam of the SA-X lethal to her until she regained the Varia Suit, and the Metroid nature that later awoke fully on ZDR (see Samus Aran – Metroid DNA, Chozo DNA and what she has become; Fusion Suit). Per the original manual, the X react adversely to Metroid tissue and attack her viciously and relentlessly whenever she is within reach.

Her view of the X is unsentimental. She called them "heartless abominations" and dismissed the Federation's talk of their potential. When ordered off the BSL station so the Federation could capture the SA-X, she reasoned that as soon as Federation ships landed the X would overwhelm them and absorb the knowledge and power of their occupants; she chose to destroy SR388 along with the station rather than let a single X leave it. She treats the X as an intelligent enemy that fights for its species: she has watched them seize a sector's atmospheric stabilizers to reshape its environment, smell out the Metroids of the Restricted Laboratory, and send her own likeness to kill her.

On ZDR, immune and forewarned, she went to confirm whether the X still lived, absorbed the X of an infected Chozo Soldier in Elun, destroyed the X-infected Raven Beak, and at the end accepted the sacrifice of the X wearing Quiet Robe's form, which gave itself up to her to steady her Metroid body (Metroid Dread). ZDR's end was not her decision: its self-destruct followed the destruction of Raven Beak X. SR388 remains the one world she chose to burn to be rid of the X.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quiet_Robe · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SA-X · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion</sub>


### X Parasite: The X on ZDR

*Keys:* Elun, Chozo Soldier X, Chozo Soldier-X, X-infected Chozo, Raven Beak X, Raven Beak-X, Kraid X, Kraid-X, X on ZDR, living X


After the Biologic Space Laboratories station and SR388 were destroyed, the X were presumed extinct. Then the Galactic Federation received a video transmission of a living X on the remote planet ZDR. Seven E.M.M.I. units sent to investigate fell silent, and Samus, the only being immune to the X, went to ZDR to learn whether the parasites truly survived (Metroid Dread). The footage was bait: Raven Beak, leader of the Mawkin Chozo, had let it reach the Federation so that Samus would come.

The X had reached ZDR through the Mawkin themselves. One of Raven Beak's soldiers carried an X, and the infection spread until every Chozo on the planet except Raven Beak and the Thoha survivor Quiet Robe was infected. Raven Beak locked the infected inside Elun, a sealed facility he used as a quarantine; the Chozo warriors still serving him were machines, the Robot Chozo Soldiers, one of which later killed Quiet Robe. The outbreak wrecked his plan to keep the X under control, and with the Metroids of SR388 gone he turned to recreating them from Samus's DNA.

Samus's path led her into Elun, where the sealed halls were thick with X and mutated Mawkin. A Chozo Soldier animated by the X challenged her with a spear; as the fight went on its head warped into a beaked, Raven Beak-like shape and it sprayed black ooze, until she blasted it from inside its own mouth and absorbed the X. As she left, the quarantine door opened behind her and the X poured out across ZDR; Adam concluded that Raven Beak had released them, for reasons he could not explain.

The X infected nearly every creature on the planet, and each possessed creature left X for Samus to absorb when it died. An X took the corpse of Quiet Robe and reactivated the E.M.M.I. he had shut down. Experiment No. Z-57, a creature Samus had passed in Dairon, escaped and attacked the central control system in Cataris that heats the planet, freezing ZDR until she obliterated its Core-X. A Quetzoa on Ferenia became Escue and a Muzby on Ghavoran became Golzuna, and their Core-X yielded Storm Missiles and Cross Bombs. X-mimicked Chozo Soldiers, some bearing shields, met her in several regions, the spear-bearers warping into beaked forms partway through combat; the silver and gold Twin Robot Chozo Soldiers, by contrast, were machines, not X hosts.

Aboard Itorash, Samus drained Raven Beak with her awakened Metroid powers and the ship fell to the surface. As he rose to fight again at the crash site, an X infected him and fused him with the body of Kraid, whom Samus had killed in Cataris, producing Raven Beak X; she obliterated it with the Hyper Beam of her Metroid Suit, and its destruction set off the planet's self-destruct. Fully Metroid, she could not board her Gunship without draining it. The X wearing Quiet Robe's form, which still seemed to carry something of him, appeared, bowed to her, reverted to its gelatinous state and let itself be absorbed, stabilizing her, and she lifted off as ZDR exploded. With the planet went the last known X in the universe.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quiet_Robe · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/E.M.M.I. · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### X Parasite: The X outbreak aboard the BSL station

*Keys:* X outbreak, BSL outbreak, outbreak on the BSL, Quarantine Bay, infected by an X, infected by the X, X infection, infested suit, infected suit parts


The X outbreak began on SR388 shortly after Samus exterminated the planet's Metroids. Guarding a Biologic Space Laboratories (BSL) research team on the surface for the Galactic Federation, Samus destroyed what appeared to be a Hornoad; the creature was an X mimic, and the freed X infected her. She survived only because the infested parts of her Power Suit were cut away and a vaccine made from the Metroid hatchling's cells destroyed the X inside her (see Samus and the X; The BSL incident).

The infested suit pieces and infected SR388 specimens were shipped to the BSL research station orbiting SR388. An explosion in the Quarantine Bay followed, and Samus, in the Fusion Suit and a new Gunship whose computer she nicknamed Adam, was sent to investigate (Metroid Fusion). The explosion had been the SA-X, born from her suit parts, breaking out with a Power Bomb and breaching the other capsules. The X spread through all six sectors, infecting the station's fauna and its remaining crew, and spread further as Samus lifted security locks to move through the station. They acted with purpose: in Sector 1 (SRX) they invaded the atmospheric stabilizers, which Adam read as an attempt to alter the environment to their needs; a mimicked crewman shut down the Main Boiler's cooling unit in Sector 3 (PYR) to trigger a meltdown; the security robot B.O.X. was taken through its organic neural network; and Nightmare, kept by the researchers in Sector 5 (ARC), became an X host and wrecked the sector. Samus reclaimed her abilities one Core-X at a time (see X-mimicked creatures of the BSL).

In the Restricted Laboratory, "The X has smelled out its natural enemy," Adam warned as an SA-X came for the Federation's secretly bred Metroids; the laboratory was jettisoned and destroyed, and Adam admitted that no fewer than ten SA-X now existed. When he later revealed that the Federation meant to capture the SA-X, Samus answered that the moment Federation ships landed the X would overwhelm them and absorb the knowledge and power of their occupants. She crashed the station into SR388 instead, destroying both and every X aboard (see The destruction of the BSL and SR388). The X were believed extinct from that day until a living specimen was recorded on ZDR.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SA-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Restricted_Laboratory · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Human_Core-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/B.O.X.</sub>


### X Parasite: X biology, mimicry and Core-X

*Keys:* Core-X, Core X, Hard Core-X, Hard Core X, Red X, Blue X, X mimic, X-mimic, X-mimics, X-mimicked, gelatinous form, free-floating X


An X infection begins when a gelatinous X touches a living creature. It enters the body and multiplies in the host's nervous system, feeding until the host dies and the swollen X burst out. The X then reads the host's DNA and builds a copy: the mimic looks, moves and fights like the original, inherits its abilities and weaknesses, and holds its memories and knowledge. A living host is not required. The X animated the corpse of Serris, cloned the frozen body of Ridley aboard the BSL station (see Neo-Ridley), later took the bodies of Quiet Robe and Kraid on ZDR, and one posed as a Chozo Statue holding an Item Sphere (see X-mimicked creatures of the BSL, part 1). Mimics are only marginally sturdier than the real creature, and all of them carry the X's relentless hostility.

When a mimic is destroyed, the X inside reverts to its gelatinous form and drifts off to seek a new host. In that state beams at best stun a few of them, Power Bombs do nothing, and only Metroid cells, or a being carrying Metroid DNA, can destroy or consume them. Samus, who carries that DNA, must catch an X in its free form as it escapes its dead host; absorbing it restores her energy and ammunition. The common X are told apart by color:
- Yellow X, the most common, restore ten units of energy. The X that infected Samus on SR388 was yellow.
- Green X restore two Missiles and are often shaken loose from a Core-X struck by beam fire.
- Red X are the rarest. They hide inside the Gadora that guard important hatches and make a creature more persistent and durable, producing golden Scisers, golden Zebesians and blue Zero. Absorbing one refills four Energy Tanks' worth of energy, ten Power Bombs and fifty Missiles.
- Blue X, larger and smarter, lived only in the cold of Sector 5 (ARC) and, having spread from it, Sector 6 (NOC), often hiding under crates. A beam shot or Power Bomb can stun them, which no other free X allows. Their cold harmed Samus until she recovered the Varia Suit; afterward each yielded thirty units of energy.

A Core-X is a far larger X, possibly a mass of many, found at the heart of the most powerful hosts; per the original manual it has a thorny membrane. It survives the death of its host, rams anything near it, and sheds Yellow or Green X when shot with beams. Only Missiles crack its spiked shell; absorbing the exposed core returns an ability it had taken along with a flood of energy and ammunition. A Hard Core-X carries a stolen beam weapon that it fires from a single eye and can be hurt only by striking that eye while the beam charges; a Missile to the eye makes it fire at once. Core-X appeared again on ZDR, inside mutated creatures such as Escue, Golzuna and Experiment No. Z-57.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Core-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Blue_X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Serris · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Fake_Chozo_Statue</sub>


### X Parasite: X Parasites (species overview)

*Keys:* X Parasite, X Parasites, X-Parasite, X-Parasites, Parasite X, X parasite species, X lifeform, X life-form


The X Parasites, usually called simply the X, are gelatinous, glowing organisms native to planet SR388. In their free state they are translucent blobs that drift through the air. "Parasite" is a loose label; in practice the X are predators. An X enters a living host, feeds and multiplies inside it, and bursts free when the host dies, then uses the victim's DNA to build a perfect mimic that carries the original's appearance, abilities, weaknesses, memories and knowledge. They reproduce asexually by division, so one X becomes many within a short time. Beams at best stun a few forms, Power Bombs cannot harm them, and a mimicked creature is only slightly tougher than the original; what reliably destroys the X is Metroid tissue, and the Metroids are their only natural predator.

The X were already on SR388 when the Chozo came to the planet. The Thoha, a Chozo tribe, met the X there and engineered the Metroids as artificial predators to exterminate them (recorded in the Chozo Memories; Metroid: Samus Returns). Per the manga, the Chozo discovered and named the X on SR388, a camera they placed revealed its nature when one mimicked a Hornoad, and Old Bird conferred with Mother Brain to devise the means of extermination, because the Chozo feared the X might one day infiltrate the Galactic Federation. The Metroids drove the X to the edge of extinction, but then grew feral, multiplied and became the top predators of SR388.

When Samus Aran exterminated the Metroids of SR388 for the Galactic Federation, she unknowingly removed the X's predator. The X recovered with ferocious speed and spread through the planet's surviving fauna. On a later escort mission for Biologic Space Laboratories, an X mimicking a Hornoad infected Samus; the Metroid vaccine that saved her made her the only being immune to the X and able to absorb them. X carried to the BSL research station overran it, producing the SA-X and many X-mimicked creatures, until Samus crashed the station into SR388 and destroyed both, with every X on them (Metroid Fusion).

The X are not mindless. Aboard the BSL they invaded a sector's atmospheric stabilizers to alter the environment to their needs, smelled out the Metroids of the Restricted Laboratory, and used a mimicked crewman to sabotage the station's Main Boiler. Adam observed that the X act for the survival of their species rather than the individual. Samus called them "heartless abominations" and judged that any Federation force sent to capture them would be overwhelmed and absorbed. Some time later a living X was recorded on planet ZDR, where Raven Beak had quarantined an outbreak among the Mawkin Chozo inside Elun; the X were released across ZDR and, as far as is known, perished when that planet exploded.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak</sub>


### X Parasite: X-mimicked creatures of the BSL, part 1

*Keys:* Arachnus-X, Arachnus X, Fake Chozo Statue, Zazabi, Serris-X, Serris X, B.O.X, Human Core-X, X-host, X host


Every powerful creature Samus faced aboard the Biologic Space Laboratories research station during the X outbreak was an X mimic, and most held a Core-X carrying one of the abilities her damaged suit had lost (Metroid Fusion); absorbing the core returned the ability. The earlier hosts:
- Arachnus-X (Main Deck): an X copy of the armadillo-like SR388 native, far more aggressive than the original. Its claws threw waves of energy, its fireballs ran lines of flame along the floor, and it rolled into its shell to ram. Its Core-X returned the Morph Ball.
- Fake Chozo Statue (Sector 1, SRX): a Hard Core-X posing as a Chozo Statue holding an Item Sphere, met while Samus was clearing the X from the atmospheric stabilizers. Once its disguise was shot away it rammed her and fired beams from a glowing red eye in its shell, the one point Missiles could hurt. Absorbing it returned the Charge Beam.
- Zazabi (Sector 2, TRO): a plant-like hopper with a toothed suction cup for a base, taken by an unusually powerful Core-X. It leapt to swallow Samus and drain her energy; only its inner body could be hurt. Its core returned the Hi-Jump and Spring Ball.
- Serris-X (Sector 4, AQA): the real Serris, a serpent kept in a breeding tank, was found dead; its X copy raced through the tank at blinding speed. Its core returned the Speed Booster.
- B.O.X. (Sector 3, PYR, then Sector 6, NOC): the station's security robot, a hybrid with an organic neural network. It first ambushed Samus with fire bombs in PYR after destroying the Data Room and fled, still only a rogue machine; by the time it fought her again in NOC the X had claimed its neural network, and it fought with homing missiles over water its own discharges had electrified. Its core returned the Wave Beam.
- Human Core-X (Sector 3, PYR): an X that had mimicked a crew member, found shutting down the Main Boiler's cooling unit to detonate the station. It emerged as a Hard Core-X firing the Wide Beam, which Samus took back. From it Adam concluded that "the X can absorb the memories and knowledge of their prey"; the only survivors Samus ever found aboard were the Dachora and Etecoons.
Samus had to kill each mimic twice, once as the creature and again as the exposed core before it could escape to a new host. The later hosts, from the Mega Core-X to Neo-Ridley, are covered under X-mimicked creatures of the BSL, part 2.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Arachnus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Fake_Chozo_Statue · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zazabi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Serris · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/B.O.X. · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Human_Core-X</sub>


### X Parasite: X-mimicked creatures of the BSL, part 2

*Keys:* Mega Core-X, Mega Core X, Nettori, Nightmare-X, Nightmare X, Yakuza-X, Yakuza X, Reactor Silo, Hornoad-X, X-hosts, X hosts, X-mimicked creatures


The X mimics Samus faced in the later part of the Biologic Space Laboratories outbreak, each guarding a Core-X that held one of her lost abilities (Metroid Fusion). The earlier hosts, from Arachnus-X to the Human Core-X, are covered under X-mimicked creatures of the BSL, part 1.
- Mega Core-X (Sector 6, NOC): the X that stole the Varia Suit data from the NOC Data Room, grown huge and ringed by lesser cores. Breaking it returned the Varia Suit, which ended the Blue X's threat and shielded Samus from the SA-X's Ice Beam.
- Nettori (Sector 2, TRO): a plant whose vines spread through the station, clogged the reactor core and blacked out the station's power, and whose growth wrapped a Chozo Statue; it fired the Plasma Beam and spat spores. Its Hard Core-X returned the Plasma Beam.
- Nightmare (Sector 5, ARC): a gravity-manipulating bio-mechanical weapon kept by the station's researchers, now an X-host. Adam: "It possesses uncommon power and can manipulate gravity." It wrecked ARC and pinned Samus's Missiles to the floor with its gravity field; its core returned the Gravity Suit. Samus had destroyed a Nightmare aboard the Bottle Ship before; whether this was the same unit is unknown (see Nightmare (origin and the Bottle Ship)).
- Yakuza (Main Deck, Reactor Silo): a spider-like creature that seized Samus, slammed her down and spat fireballs during the station-wide blackout. Its core returned the Space Jump.
- Neo-Ridley (Sector 1, SRX): a Core-X that cloned Ridley's frozen body from Sub-Zero Containment; its core returned the Screw Attack (see Neo-Ridley).
Lesser mimics filled the station too: hostile Hornoad-X on the Main Deck and in SRX, Blue X in the cold sectors, and golden Scisers, golden Zebesians and blue Zero empowered by Red X. Every mimic had to be killed twice, once as the creature and again as the exposed core before it could flee to a new host, and absorbing a core also refilled Samus's energy and ammunition. For Samus these fights were the slow recovery of her own strength, each core handing back a piece of the suit the Federation had cut away from her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mega_Core-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Nettori · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Nightmare · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Yakuza · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley-X · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hornoad</sub>


## Space Pirates


### Space Pirates: Elite Pirates and the Omega Pirate

*Keys:* Elite Pirate, Elite Pirates, Omega Pirate, Omega Pirates, Phazon Elite, Phazon Elites, Project Helix, Elite Quarters, Elite Research, Wave Quake, Plasma Artillery Cannon, Chameleon Manta


Elite Pirates were the product of Project Helix, the Space Pirate program to splice Phazon into Pirate DNA. Science Team expected "radical new Pirate genomes" while admitting that "Phazon madness is a concern." The first embryo infusions at Glacier One were disastrous: Phazon degenerated brain tissue even as it built muscle, most subjects died before maturing, and the survivors turned violent. The breakthrough came when Research Team Sclera combined a Phazon strain called Vertigo with Pirate DNA. In the Phazon Mines the Elites, larger, stronger and far more resilient than ordinary Pirates, were fed Elite Ration Mix and trained against an entire shift of Phazon-maddened Dynamo workers. In simulations they scored a 74% success ratio against Samus Aran; High Command was pleased but expected a higher ratio by the project's end, and predicted that Elites, alongside ordinary and Flying Pirates, would form a "near-indestructible backbone."

An Elite carries a shoulder-mounted Plasma Artillery Cannon, a Repulsion Shield, a Wave Quake Generator that sends shock waves across the ground, retractable Wrist Bayonets and an energy-siphon system that drinks beam fire to feed the cannon. Its weaknesses are slowness, a cannon whose heat shows on thermal imaging, and vulnerability to concussive weapons such as Missiles. Seven Elite Pirates were stationed throughout the Mines, and Samus fought three. In Elite Research a Power Bomb freed the Phazon Elite, ident-code EPG-8642, "Elite Pirate Alpha," a cannonless Elite infused with energized Phazon whose shortened lifespan the researchers deemed "of little concern"; killing it won Samus the Artifact of Warrior.

The pinnacle was Elite Pirate Upsilon, code-named the Omega Pirate, kept in cryosleep in the Elite Quarters above a pool of Phazon. Dosed far beyond safety limits, its mass "increased exponentially" while it "retained all mental faculties." It healed by coating itself in Phazon, a Chameleon Manta cloaking device hid it while it fed, and it could summon Beam Troopers. Its absorption system handled beams but not Missiles, a "critical design flaw," and no simulated subject lived beyond two deca-cycles, so lifespans had to improve before the design could be cost-effective. Samus woke it, shattered the Phazon plates on its knees and shoulders, tracked it through its cloak with the X-Ray Visor, and killed it. The dying Omega Pirate collapsed onto her, and its Phazon fused with her armor to create the Phazon Suit, which later let her channel the Phazon Beam (Metroid Prime).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elite_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Elite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Mines · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One</sub>


### Space Pirates: Organization, High Command and Science Team

*Keys:* High Command, Science Team, Pirate hierarchy, Pirate caste, Pirate castes, Pirate ranks, Research Team Sclera, Security Command, Pirate Data, Pirate Log, Pirate Logs, Pirate culture


The Space Pirate Organization runs as a rigid military bureaucracy. At the top sits Command, the highest tier of the hierarchy; Pirate records name High Command and subdivisions including Facility Command, Security Command, Central Command, Mine Security Command, Operations Command and Energy Command. High Command authorizes Phazon programs, approves reinforcements and sets timetables, and its patience is short: "Command grows impatient regarding this matter: results must be produced soon." The logs never name its individual members. On Zebes the Pirates answered to Mother Brain; Ridley has led their forces in the field and, rebuilt as Meta Ridley, served as a mainstay of Pirate security; by the Phazon crisis the whole organization had been subsumed by Dark Samus, whose followers called themselves her disciples.

Beneath Command is the Science Team, the research and development branch, with sub-units such as Research Team Sclera, which combined a Phazon strain called Vertigo with Pirate DNA and made Project Helix work. Science Team studied Phazon, Metroids, Chozo relics and Samus's own weapons, usually under hard deadlines and with heavy losses of staff and test subjects.

The military is a caste system, recorded most clearly during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), rising from the expendable Pirate Militia through Pirate Troopers and the elite Commando Pirates to the ruling caste of Pirate Commanders (see Pirate military of the Phazon crisis). Under Dark Samus, Gandrayda herself moved among the troops in their form, watching for signs of weakness, and several unfaithful disciples were "ended...violently."

The Pirate Data shows a culture both obedient and darkly comic: troopers trained "to follow orders without question," which made them ideal hosts for the Ing on Aether; workers struck by Phazon madness, from muscle spasms to hallucinations; Gronkats and Olbaps ordered disposed of after domestic beasts proved susceptible to the madness; Metroid handlers on Aether reminded that "Metroids are not pets. Metroids are not target practice," and that unauthorized feeding remained strictly prohibited; and an entire shift of Phazon-maddened Dynamo workers handed to the Elite Pirates as training adversaries. Samus has recovered these records with her Scan Visor; they are the main window outsiders have into how Pirates think.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/High_Command · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Science_Team · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Militia</sub>


### Space Pirates: Pirate Homeworld

*Keys:* Pirate Homeworld, Pirate Home World, Pirate home planet, Pirate planet, Pirate world, Command Courtyard, Pirate Command, Pirate Mines, Hazard Shield


The Pirate Homeworld is the fortified planet from which the Space Pirates waged the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). The name is a designation, not a confirmed origin: nothing establishes it as the species' birthplace. Its low-pH atmosphere produces acid rain that penetrates Samus's armor; she obtained the Hazard Shield on the planet itself before she could cross its open courtyards.

After Dark Samus took the Pirates, the Homeworld was the first world she seeded: a Leviathan struck it, and the Pirates rejoiced at being "the first world to receive this blessing," expecting "thousands of new disciples." It became the base for their assaults on Norion, Bryyo and Elysia, guarded by a planetary shield, Commando Pirates and Pirate Commanders of the ruling caste, and laced with transit systems linking sectors such as Pirate Mines and Pirate Command, where the Command Courtyard lies. Its slaves and criminals filled the ranks of the Pirate Militia.

Samus came to the Homeworld after destroying the Leviathans of Bryyo and Elysia. Gandrayda, sent to locate the planet, had been corrupted by Phazon there; she now moved among the Pirates in their own form, watching for weakness, and had vowed to present Samus's "bloody helmet" to Dark Samus. Posing as a Federation Marine, she lured Samus into a trap and fought her on a tower in the acid rain; defeated, she shifted uncontrollably through the forms of a Space Pirate, Ghor, Rundas and Samus before Dark Samus absorbed her. Samus then disabled the planetary shield with the Nova Beam and X-Ray Visor, letting Admiral Dane's fleet land. Demolition Troopers blasted a path through a transit line with no train toward the Leviathan Seed, and a Pirate Commander who ambushed them badly wounded Samus before she killed him. Inside the Seed, Omega Ridley guarded the core; Samus destroyed both, and the Federation took the Pirates' Leviathan battleship and used it to open a wormhole to Phaaze.

The Pirate Data, from "Our Mission" to "Mistress Gandrayda," records the Pirates' fall from Phazon hunters to Dark Samus's cult, including the boast "If Samus comes to our Homeworld, she will find us ready."


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Homeworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gandrayda · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commander · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Commando_Pirate</sub>


### Space Pirates: Pirate military of the Phazon crisis (Militia, Commandos, Commanders)

*Keys:* Aerotrooper, Aerotroopers, Commando Pirate, Commando Pirates, Pirate Militia, Pirate Commander, Pirate Commanders, Phazite, Armored Pirate Trooper, Assault Pirate Trooper, Advanced Pirate Trooper, Shield Pirate Trooper


After Dark Samus seized the Space Pirate Organization and seeded the Pirate Homeworld with Phazon, the Pirate military was rebuilt around Phazon (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Most regular troops carried Phazon Enhancement Devices and could enter Hypermode, becoming faster and tougher, though Phazon-based weapons still harmed them. Standard kit was an Assault Rifle and Energy Scythe, "both powered by Phazon," plus EMP Grenades, a Dash Jet and a short-lived hover system. Armor was graded: Armored Pirate Troopers resisted beams but not Missiles; Advanced Pirate Troopers wore thick Phazite; Assault Pirate Troopers wore missile-resistant armor and favored melee; Shield Pirate Troopers carried energy shields that Samus's Grapple Lasso could tear away. Most served on the Homeworld, with detachments on Bryyo, Elysia and Norion.

Phazite, a Phazon-derived armor, marks the upper castes. Commando Pirates, found only on the Homeworld, wore Phazite that "only Beams of the highest temperature can penetrate," with a cloaking field, boost thrusters and a personal teleporter. Samus's X-Ray Visor and Nova Beam let her strike the head through the helmet, though Hypermode interfered with the lock-on; a Commando stripped of its Phazite lost its cloak and fought like an ordinary trooper. Pirate Commanders, the ruling caste, each a Commando for at least ten years, wore more resilient red Phazite, teleported around the field and called Commandos to their side; Phazon attacks hurt both castes. One Commander ambushed Samus and Admiral Dane's Demolition Troopers on the Homeworld, badly wounded her, and died at her hands.

Aerotroopers, successors to the Flying Pirates, had served on Aether; in the Phazon crisis they traded armor for evasion, fighting with twin Remote Attack Pods that fired Particle Cannons and Helix Missiles, plus sticky Gel Bombs. Their code amounts to death before dishonor, ending in a spinning suicide attack that Ice Missiles can stop. Plasma Beam fire ignites an Aerotrooper's jetpack so the Grapple Lasso can tear it from the pilot.

At the bottom stood the Pirate Militia, expendable infantry recruited from the Homeworld's slaves and criminals, denied Phazon Enhancement Devices, armed with weaker arm cannons and retractable scythes, and "surprisingly obedient to the upper castes"; rumor held that disobedient Militia became dinner for regular troopers. Samus first met them aboard the G.F.S. Olympus during the Pirate raid, then on Norion, Bryyo and Elysia. Pirate ground forces rode into battle aboard Space Pirate Armored Tactical Carriers and Boarding Pods.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Trooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Commando_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commander · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Militia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aerotrooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_ATC</sub>


### Space Pirates: Projects and Phazon research on Tallon IV and Aether

*Keys:* Phazon Program, Project Titan, Phazon infusion, Phazon madness, Phazon Fever, Glacier One, Phazon Mines, Research Lab Hydra, Research Lab Aether, Pirate science, Pirate experiments, Pirate base


The Space Pirates came to Tallon IV after detecting energy readings on "a Wanderer-class planet identified as Tallon IV."
