The source proved to be Phazon, an unstable and immensely potent radioactive mutagen, and Science Team judged the world "a viable secondary headquarters" after the fall of Zebes. The Phazon Program was their bid to "rise dominant of this sector within a deca-cycle." Yields climbed 44%, but Phazon madness, with loss of equilibrium, muscle spasms and, in extreme cases, hallucinations, forced stricter safety rules.

Facilities:
- Glacier One in Phendrana Drifts, the research headquarters, with Research Lab Hydra, Research Lab Aether and an Observatory; the cold kept captive Metroids sluggish.
- The Phazon Mines, the headquarters proper, reached through the crashed Orpheon, with Elite Research, Metroid Quarantine and the Central Dynamo.

Projects recorded in the Pirate Data (Metroid Prime):
- Metroid Studies: Phazon infusion of the Metroids saved from Zebes showed "a considerable growth in power and size"; Science Team proposed taming them, since "a small force of disciplined Metroids could wipe out entire armies."
- Parasite infusion: Phazon made Tallon IV's Parasites larger and more aggressive but wiped them out by the fourth infusion period; mutated Parasite Queens aboard the Orpheon were the best result.
- Project Titan: exposing inanimate matter to Phazon produced the rock creature Thardus; after security breaches with massive casualties the project was suspended indefinitely.
- Project Helix: Phazon spliced into Pirate DNA, yielding the Elite Pirates and the Omega Pirate.
- Hunter Weapons: Samus's arsenal reverse-engineered from combat data; beam copies armed the Beam Troopers, but four test subjects "were horribly broken" by Morph Ball prototypes.
- Chozo Studies: the Pirates set out to strip the Chozo Ruins and breach the Impact Crater's containment field, though Chozo Ghosts assaulted their personnel and "few have survived." Their creed: "The dead should serve the living, not hinder them."

On Aether, Pirates who found Phazon in the Dasha System located a Rift Portal and the Dark Beam and began raiding Dark Aether for ore, while experimenting on captive Tallon Metroids, trying to domesticate them and draining them for bio-energy (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). The sickness there was logged as Phazon Fever: Technician 12 claimed the Phazon as his own and held off the staff with a weapon until security arrived. Samus destroyed these programs in person, and the Phazon the Pirates coveted later nearly consumed her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Science_Team · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Mines · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Thardus</sub>


### Space Pirates: Ships and the Frigate Orpheon

*Keys:* Orpheon, Vol Paragom, Colossus, Pirate Mother Ship, Boarding Pod, Space Coffin, Pirate ATC, Pirate frigate, Pirate frigates, Pirate ship, Pirate ships


At the fall of Zebes three research frigates, Orpheon, Siriacus and Vol Paragom, "were in orbit at zero hour and managed to retreat" with the Pirates' surviving Metroids. Orpheon's Metroids all survived; the ship docked at Vortex Outpost, then took orbit over Tallon IV to collect native life for Phazon experiments. Its mutated Parasite Queens were its greatest success and its doom: two broke containment and killed most of the crew, and the survivors lit a distress beacon and abandoned ship. Samus answered the beacon and killed the remaining Queen, whose body fell into the reactor and set off the self-destruct; escaping, she saw Meta Ridley flee toward the planet. The wreck crashed on Tallon IV, where its flooded hull, prowled by Aqua Pirates, became a road into the Phazon Mines (Metroid Prime).

Other Pirate craft:
- The Space Pirate Mother Ship in Chozodia on Zebes, whose forces shot down Samus's gunship; she infiltrated it in her Zero Suit armed only with a Paralyzer, destroyed Mecha Ridley, and escaped in a Pirate ship as it self-destructed.
- A Pirate frigate crippled by a Federation patrol, which crashed on Aether.
- The battleship Colossus, "used for battle and storage purposes." Its crew failed to intercept the G.F.S. Anhur, salvaged the Phazon the Federation left behind, and revived Dark Samus. Improved under her, its "Phazon-powered weapons" rendered the G.F.S. Valhalla helpless; the Pirates seized Aurora Unit 313 and, by their own account, jettisoned the crew into space. Its later fate is unknown.
- The Leviathan battleship: finding the Colossus not intimidating enough, Dark Samus had a Leviathan fitted with cybernetic weapons, its crew quarters and command consoles placed within the beast's skull. The Federation later seized it to reach Phaaze.
- Boarding Pods, nicknamed Space Coffins: crude craft with an explosive nose cone that rams enemy hulls, as dangerous to their crews as to the target, used in the raid on the G.F.S. Olympus.
- Space Pirate Armored Tactical Carriers (ATCs), troop craft with Tribeam cannons and a single vulnerable heat vent, deployed over Norion and SkyTown.
- The Doomseye, a colossal battleship built by Pirates regrouping in the Bermuda System, aboard which they held a brainwashed, enlarged Samus until the Federation Force freed her and the Federation fleet destroyed the ship (see The Doomseye).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Frigate_Orpheon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vortex_Outpost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Mother_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Colossus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data</sub>


### Space Pirates: Space Pirates (overview)

*Keys:* Space Pirates, Space Pirate, Space-Pirates, Space-Pirate, the Pirates, Pirate forces, Pirate army, Pirate Organization, Pirate raid, Pirate raids, scourge of the Galaxy


The Space Pirates are the chief enemies of the Galactic Federation and the recurring adversaries of Samus Aran. They are described as a species of warring bipedal organisms, but the name covers an organization as much as a race: the crab-clawed Zebesians who conquered Zebes, the insectoid Kihunters who came from another galactic system to help rebuild it, and the troopers of Tallon IV, Aether and the Pirate Homeworld are all Space Pirates. They employ other creatures as leaders: they took Zebes with Mother Brain, and count Ridley, Kraid and the general Weavel among their leaders. Weavel, critically wounded in a clash with Samus, was rebuilt as a cyborg and fought on as a bounty hunter. Their planet of origin is unknown; even the Pirate Homeworld is not confirmed as their birthplace.

Their trade is piracy and conquest. Per the manual (Super Metroid) they are "the scourge of the Galaxy," known for stealing galactic trading ships and leaving the crews stranded in space. Per the manga, Pirates led by Ridley raided the colony K-2L and killed Samus's parents. Their defining crime was the theft of the Metroid specimen from a Federation research vessel returning from SR388; they carried it to their fortress on Zebes, where, per the original manual, the Federation feared they would multiply the creatures with beta rays. The Federation's assault on Zebes failed, and Samus was sent in alone.

Their history in brief:
- Zebes: Samus defeated Kraid, Ridley and Mother Brain and escaped the Space Pirate Mother Ship (Metroid: Zero Mission). Three research frigates, Orpheon among them, fled with Metroid cargo.
- Tallon IV: the survivors mined Phazon, ran Project Helix and rebuilt Ridley as Meta Ridley until Samus wiped out the operation.
- Aether: Pirates who found Phazon in the Dasha System built a base there and raided Dark Aether for ore; overpowered by the Ing and the hunters, they abandoned the mining facility, which the Federation later dismantled.
- The Phazon crisis: the crew of the battleship Colossus acquired the remains of Dark Samus, who revived herself on their Phazon, killed a third of them and made the rest her disciples. The Pirates seized Aurora Unit 313, attacked Norion, Bryyo and Elysia, and were crushed when the Federation stormed the Pirate Homeworld and Phaaze was destroyed.
- Afterward: Pirates regrouping in the Bermuda System built the battleship Doomseye; the Federation Force disabled its shield and the Federation fleet destroyed it.
- Ridley and his Pirates stole the baby Metroid from the Ceres Space Colony and fled to a rebuilt Zebes; Samus followed, killed Ridley and Mother Brain, and the planet exploded.
- Later: Cyborg Zebesians cloned by the Federation aboard the Bottle Ship; the rogue hunter Sylux and his army of Space Pirates, who attacked Federation research facilities including one on the planet Tanamaar in the Desolan System; and X Parasite mimics on the BSL station.

Pirate culture, known mostly from their own Pirate Data logs, is disciplined, obedient and bureaucratic; troopers are "ruthless and amoral," trained to follow orders without question. The Pirates call Samus "the Hunter" and have offered a bounty for her, dead or alive, since the fall of Zebes.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirates · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebesian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kihunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Weavel · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes</sub>


### Space Pirates: The Pirates and Samus (the Hunter)

*Keys:* vendetta, grudge, revenge, bounty, hatred, Dark Hunter, Pirate bounty, all-points alert, hunting the Hunter, Pirate enemy  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Pirate, Pirates, Space Pirate, Space Pirates, Zebesian, Zebesians, K-2L, Ridley


To the Space Pirates, Samus Aran is "the Hunter." Per the manga, the enmity began at K-2L, where Pirates led by Ridley raided the colony: Virginia Aran died shielding her daughter, Rodney Aran died igniting the Afloraltite aboard the Pirate ship, and Samus was the only survivor. Per the manga, years later on Jigrad, serving with the Federation Police, she nearly executed a surrendering Pirate in her rage ("Did you say...'Mercy'!?") before arresting him "in the name of the Federation Police."

The Pirates' own records treat her as a problem to be solved. After the fall of Zebes, survivors reported ground personnel killed "by the Hunter clad in metal," and a Security Command all-points alert described a heavily armed female hominid in a powered armorsuit: "A considerable bounty will go to the unit who delivers Aran to Command. Dead or alive, it matters not." Science Team copied her arsenal for the Beam Troopers and tested Elite Pirates against simulations of her; High Command was pleased with a 74% success ratio but wanted better. On Aether a confused garrison watched a hunter in dark armor steal their Phazon, nicknamed her the "Dark Hunter," and, when a second armored hunter arrived and the two fought, proposed allying with one against the other; the logs never identify the Dark Hunter as Dark Samus.

During the Phazon crisis the crew of the Colossus swore to "take that Phazon or die trying." Their logs record defeats: "The Hunter's might is great, and she is as efficient as ever when it comes to disrupting our operations" (Bryyo), and "The accursed Elysian machines were no match for Aran's guns." They noted that the hunters struck down on Norion each bore Dark Samus's "mark of corruption," and Gandrayda vowed to lay Samus's bloody helmet before Dark Samus.

Samus's side of the ledger: she defeated Kraid, Ridley and Mother Brain on Zebes; critically wounded the Pirate general Weavel, who returned as a cyborg; dismantled Pirate operations on Tallon IV, Aether and the Pirate Homeworld, where a Pirate Commander badly wounded her before she killed him; was captured, brainwashed and enlarged by the Pirates of the Doomseye until the Federation Force freed her; pursued Ridley to a rebuilt Zebes to recover the stolen baby Metroid, a mission that ended with the planet's destruction; and fought Sylux's Pirates at the Federation facility on Tanamaar.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/K-2L · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Logs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Commando</sub>


### Space Pirates: Troop types of Tallon IV and Aether

*Keys:* Pirate Trooper, Pirate Troopers, Flying Pirate, Flying Pirates, Shadow Pirate, Shadow Pirates, Aqua Pirate, Beam Trooper, Beam Troopers, Pirate Commando, Pirate Commandos, Pirate Grenadier


The Space Pirates Samus fought on Tallon IV and Aether were organized into specialized troop types, most recorded in her Scan Visor logbook.

- Pirate Troopers are the common infantry, a "sentient aggressor species well trained in weapon and melee combat," ruthless and amoral. On Tallon IV they carried Galvanic Accelerator Cannons and forearm Scythes; on Aether a Photonic Power Scythe and Quantum Assault Cannon became standard issue.
- Beam Troopers (Power, Wave, Ice and Plasma Troopers) wore armor and arms copied from Samus's beams, reverse-engineered from combat data because "Command would dearly enjoy turning Aran's weapons against her." Each could be harmed only by the beam it carried. They served in the Phazon Mines and answered the Omega Pirate's summons.
- Flying Pirates, jet-pack troops of the Tallon Overworld, Magmoor Caverns and Phendrana Drifts, fired beams and missiles; their packs showed on thermal imaging, and one whose pack failed hurled itself at its attacker. Aqua Pirates, their underwater counterpart in the flooded wreck of the Orpheon, rammed their target the same way when their armor failed.
- Shadow Pirates of Phendrana and the Mines carried cloaking generators so power-hungry that they fought only with scythes; thermal or X-ray imaging or a Wave Beam shot exposed them.
- Pirate Aerotroopers succeeded the Flying Pirates on Aether, "known for their cold precision and utter cruelty," armed with homing missiles and preferring a suicide strike to capture.
- Pirate Commandos, the elite of Aether, were "dedicated to 'hunting the Hunter'," with energy scythes, pulse cannons, EMP grenade launchers, thrusters and a short-lived barrier shield; Samus first met them at Torvus Grove.
- Pirate Grenadiers carried an experimental energy grenade launcher too bulky to pair with rifles or scythes and rammed anyone who closed in. Samus met only two pairs, in the Agon Wastes Command Center and on the Temple Grounds' Path of Eyes.

On Aether the Ing found Pirate Troopers, "already trained to follow orders without question," perfect hosts. Dark Pirate Troopers fought Samus in the Agon Wastes, Dark Agon and the Temple Grounds. Dark Pirate Commandos, possessed only by Hunter Ing, who competed for the right and lent them the power to phase out of local timespace, haunted Torvus Bog, Dark Torvus Bog, the Temple Grounds and the Ing Hive. In the Sanctuary Fortress Samus watched Dark Samus slaughter a group of Dark Pirate Troopers. (Metroid Prime; Metroid Prime 2: Echoes)


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Trooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Trooper_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Flying_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aqua_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Shadow_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aerotrooper</sub>


### Space Pirates: Zebesians and Kihunters

*Keys:* Zebesian, Zebesians, Kihunter, Kihunters, Keyhunter, Keyhunters, Ki-Hunter, Ki-Hunters, Zoro, Zoros


Zebesians are the Space Pirates who conquered Zebes and took to calling themselves by the planet's name; they are not native to it, and their true origin is unknown. Their signature feature is a pair of crab-like claws that open to fire lasers. They cling to walls and ceilings and are acrobatic enough to rival Samus. They garrisoned the fortress on Zebes during Samus's first mission there, and the rebuilt fortress after Ridley stole the baby Metroid from the Ceres Space Colony.

Zebesians come in color-coded grades of increasing toughness:
- green ones are weak;
- red ones shrug off everything but Missiles and the Spazer;
- pink ones yield only to the Plasma Beam or Screw Attack;
- yellow ones need the Screw Attack or a fully charged shot;
- rare silver and gold Zebesians are vulnerable only on their backs;
- black Zebesians, fast and resilient, answer only to the Charge Beam, and can be truly hurt only briefly after their aerial kick.

Kihunters, called Keyhunters in the manual (Super Metroid), which describes them as "pirates from another galactic system who helped rebuild Zebes," are winged, insect-like Pirates whose behavior resembles that of bees: they live in colonies subordinate to a King Kihunter. Their larva is a creature called the Zero, and the Zoro is their pupal form. Adults dive and slash, and "when they lose their wings, they spit powerful acid." Green Kihunters are common on Zebes, stronger yellow ones haunted the Wrecked Ship, and red ones lurked in the depths of Norfair.

After Zebes, Samus met both species as copies. Aboard the Bottle Ship the Galactic Federation secretly built cybernetic Zebesian copies as an elite army; they escaped, and Samus reported to Adam Malkovich, "It's a Zebesian...but it's been cybernetically enhanced" (Metroid: Other M). Super Zebesians there carried a Wave Beam that passed through glass. Mechanically enhanced Kihunters, products of Bottle Ship experiments, nested in a hive in the Biosphere held up by Missile flower pods; when Samus brought it down she faced the King Kihunter, and a screech that she later believed came from a small, chick-like young Ridley drove off the Zoros. On the BSL station the X Parasites produced purple and gold Zebesian mimics, a swimming Aqua Zebesian form, and Kihunter mimics emerging from Zoro pupae.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebesian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kihunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Trooper · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirates</sub>


## Ridley


### Ridley: Bottle Ship Ridley clone (Little Birdie)

*Keys:* Little Birdie, Little Birdy, Mystery Creature, Ridley clone, Ridley clones, Ridley's clone, clone of Ridley, Ridley on the Bottle Ship, Ridley's life cycle, baby Ridley, juvenile Ridley, young Ridley


The Ridley of the Bottle Ship was a clone, grown by accident. The Galactic Federation's secret bioweapon program aboard the Bottle Ship propagated Metroids from cellular material taken from Samus Aran's Power Suit, and Ridley was created the same way; at first no one realized the creature was Ridley. Samus learned this near the end of the Bottle Ship incident (Metroid: Other M), from MB, then posing as Madeline Bergman, in the Bioweapon Research Center.

The clone passed through three forms, the earliest of Ridley's life cycle Samus had ever seen:
- Little Birdie: a small white creature that Samus first encountered in Sector 1 (Biosphere). It soon proved carnivorous and aggressive. Samus later found its shed skin beside a trail of green blood leading to the torn-apart body of Lyle Smithsonian of the 07th Platoon.
- Mystery Creature: a purple, carnivorous lizard with a razor-sharp tail, still carrying a few white feathers. It ambushed Samus in the Biosphere Test Area and pinned her until a blast from Anthony Higgs's Plasma Gun knocked it off her and it fled. When it slipped into Sector 3, Adam Malkovich ordered, "Samus. Follow it!", and she pursued it into the Pyrosphere.
- Adult Ridley: in the Pyrosphere Samus found the creature's cast-off shell, and in the Geothermal Power Plant the full-grown Ridley emerged. Samus, who had killed him in Norfair, froze in shock; Anthony fired on Ridley to protect her, and Ridley's tail swept him off the platform into the lava. Ridley seized Samus and raked her along the wall before she fought back and drove him off badly hurt.

Samus never fought the clone again. It was later killed, apparently by the Queen Metroid the Federation had bred as a control specimen, and Samus found only its dried, drained husk. Anthony survived by freezing a Lava Golem and climbing to safety. The husk was later shipped frozen to the BSL station, where the X copied it as Neo-Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Little_Birdie · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mystery_Creature · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Queen_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/MB</sub>


### Ridley: Mecha Ridley

*Keys:* Mecha Ridley, Mecha-Ridley, Ridley Robot, robot Ridley, robotic Ridley, Ridley's robot, Ridley mech


Mecha Ridley, also called the Ridley Robot, is a war machine built in Ridley's image inside the Space Pirate Mother Ship on Zebes. Per director Yoshio Sakamoto, Ridley had the likeness made as a display of his own power. It was unfinished when Samus Aran found it, roughly halfway through construction: its walking and flying units had not yet been installed, and once complete it would have grown far more massive.

Samus reached it at the end of her first Zebes mission (Metroid: Zero Mission). After she destroyed Mother Brain, Space Pirate fighters shot down her Gunship as she left; she crash-landed near the Mother Ship with her Power Suit wrecked, slipped inside in her Zero Suit, recovered a full Chozo Power Suit in Chozodia, and fought her way through the ship to the chamber where the robot waited. Mecha Ridley attacked with a spray of rockets, energy beams from its eyes, fireballs and sweeping claw blows. Its only weak point was a glowing core behind a thin casing on its chest, which it shielded with its leading claw and neck; only missile fire could crack it, and only in the instant the robot lifted its neck to bring its claw down.

When the machine failed, its eyes glowed red and the Mother Ship's self-destruct began. Samus fled through the collapsing vessel and escaped in a small Space Pirate craft moments before the ship was destroyed, breaking the Pirates' hold on Zebes for the time being. It was the same Mother Ship that had landed in Chozodia to bring Ridley to Zebes earlier in the mission. The living Ridley, already destroyed by Samus in his lair, would later be rebuilt by the Pirates as the cybernetic Meta Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mecha_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Mother_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Ridley: Meta Ridley and Omega Ridley

*Keys:* Meta Ridley, Meta-Ridley, Omega Ridley, Omega-Ridley, cybernetic Ridley, cyborg Ridley, mechanized Ridley, Ridley at Norion, Ridley on Tallon IV, Phazon Ridley


Meta Ridley is the cybernetic rebuild of Ridley that the Space Pirates created aboard the research frigate Orpheon after Samus Aran destroyed him on Zebes. Per their own records, which Samus later recovered in Phendrana Drifts, Command ordered a number of metagenetic improvements for geoform 187, code-named Ridley; the metamorphosis was painful but successful, early tests showed a drastic increase in strength, mobility and offensive capability, and cybernetic modules and armor plating were added. The researchers expected him to become the mainstay of their security force, "a job he will certainly relish." Samus's scans recorded a Multi-Missile System, a Kinetic Breath Weapon, an Ultrathermal Flamestrike Projector and a Meson Bomb Launcher; his resilient hide had thinner plating only at the chest.

When the Orpheon's mutated Parasite Queens broke loose, Samus boarded the frigate and briefly met Meta Ridley before he escaped the dying ship for Tallon IV (Metroid Prime). She later saw his shadow pass over Phendrana Drifts toward the Phazon Mines. After she gathered the twelve Chozo Artifacts he attacked her at the Artifact Temple, destroying the Chozo totems from the air; once his wings disintegrated he fought her on the ground until laser beams from the temple's statues struck him and he fell into the Impact Crater.

The Pirates revived him again, now with Phazite armor (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). During the Pirate assault on the Galactic Federation base on Norion he was first seen attacking a platoon of Federation Marines, then harried Samus through the base's cramped passages. At Generator C he forced them both through the floor into an extremely long shaft; she beat him as they fell, before they hit bottom, and Rundas saved her. In this form only his mouth, where he fires his plasma weaponry, was unprotected.

He next appeared on the Pirate Homeworld, revived and regenerated through Phazon exposure as Omega Ridley, guarding the core of the Leviathan Seed there. Phazon had made him far deadlier, but a recent wound to his chest had not fully healed; after stunning him Samus tore it open with the Grapple Lasso, destroyed him and went on to destroy the Leviathan's core. Samus faced him on Tallon IV, Norion and the Pirate Homeworld, each time in a body the Pirates or Phazon had rebuilt.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-walkthrough/pirate-data/ · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rundas · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Ridley: Neo-Ridley (Ridley-X)

*Keys:* Neo-Ridley, Neo Ridley, Ridley-X, Ridley X, X-Ridley, Ridley's corpse, Ridley's frozen body, frozen Ridley, Ridley's husk, Sub-Zero Containment, Ridley on the BSL


Neo-Ridley, also called Ridley-X, is the X Parasite's copy of Ridley aboard the Biologic Space Laboratories research station (Metroid Fusion). The body it copied was the dried husk of the Bottle Ship's Ridley clone, which the Federation shipped to the BSL as the second specimen taken from that vessel, alongside Nightmare. The husk was kept frozen in Sub-Zero Containment, a small sub-freezing cell on the Main Deck whose only purpose was to hold it.

During the X outbreak, when the Main Deck's main elevator stalled with Samus Aran aboard, she broke out through the shaft and crossed Sub-Zero Containment, where the cold itself hurt her, since she had not yet regained the Varia Suit's protection. The frozen Ridley's eyes glowed as she approached, and a Core-X emerged from the body and left the room. In Sector 1 (SRX) she came upon a lifeless Ridley carcass again; it stirred, and an X-made Ridley in full flesh attacked her, driven by the parasite rather than by any Pirate command.

Neo-Ridley fought as Ridley always had: ramming her, seizing her in its claws to crush her, stabbing with its tail and spitting homing fire from its mouth. Its tail shrugged off everything she fired, and bombs and uncharged beam fire did nothing; only her charged Plasma Beam and her heavier missiles hurt it. When it died it collapsed into a hardened Core-X; Samus cracked the shell and absorbed it, regaining the Screw Attack the X had been carrying.

Samus had killed the original Ridley in Norfair and found his clone's husk on the Bottle Ship; on the BSL she had to destroy his shape once more, this time with an X Parasite inside it.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Neo-Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sub-Zero_Containment · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Nightmare · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Ridley: Proteus Ridley

*Keys:* Proteus Ridley, Proteus-Ridley, Ridley on SR388, Ridley at SR388, half-mechanical Ridley, Ridley's mechanical claw, Ridley's mechanical arm, Ridley and the hatchling


Proteus Ridley is the half-mechanical form Ridley wore when he ambushed Samus Aran on SR388 (Metroid: Samus Returns). He still carried cybernetic parts from the Space Pirates' Meta Ridley rebuild, most visibly a mechanical arm ending in a claw, on a body that was otherwise organic.

After Samus exterminated the planet's Metroids and killed the Queen Metroid, the last egg hatched in front of her and the Metroid hatchling imprinted on her as its mother. Unwilling to kill it, she carried the baby toward the surface, where Ridley ambushed them and seized the hatchling. Samus shot away one of his wings and sent him into a crash landing, but he recovered, smashed her into the ground and fired shots into her head. The baby intervened, sucking the energy out of Ridley and pouring it into Samus, reviving her; she then subdued him and left him on the surface. She departed SR388 with the hatchling and delivered it to the scientists of Ceres Space Colony.

Afterward a Hornoad gnawed on one of Ridley's discarded mechanical limbs, until an X Parasite appeared and turned it into a Hornoad-X. By the time Ridley struck Ceres to steal the hatchling, he appeared wholly organic again.

For Samus, SR388 was the first time Ridley tried to take the baby from her; at Ceres Space Colony he succeeded, setting in motion her return to Zebes.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Proteus_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ceres_Space_Colony · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley (overview and relationship with Samus)

*Keys:* Ridley, space dragon, Geoform 187, geoform-187, Cunning God of Death, Samus's nemesis, Samus' nemesis


Ridley is the leader of the Space Pirates' military and Samus Aran's lifelong nemesis. He is a huge dragon-like creature with leathery wings, taloned limbs, a long bladed tail and a toothed beak that spits plasma and fire. Per the original manual he is the original life form of the planet Zebes and is controlled by Mother Brain; on Zebes he served her alongside Kraid. Space Pirate records designate him geoform 187, code-named Ridley, and official Nintendo material calls him the Cunning God of Death. He is vicious, surprisingly intelligent, proud and deliberately cruel; the Pirate researchers who rebuilt him expected him to relish security work. He communicates in shrieks; per the manga he speaks and taunts his victims.

Ridley led the Space Pirate raid on the human colony K-2L, seeking its Afloraltite fuel, when Samus was three. Per the manga, the child approached him trustingly ("A-are you... Mr. Ridley...?") and told him he was not scary; he answered, "Mhmm, you're so cute. I'm sorry miss, but... That's impossible!! Because... You're about to die!" and struck at her. Her mother Virginia Aran died pushing her out of the way. Her father Rodney Aran then set off the stolen Afloraltite, destroying the Pirate ship and himself; Ridley survived, gravely wounded. Samus was the colony's only survivor and was taken in by the Chozo. Per the manga, she met him again in her teens, when she came to Zebes with the Federation Police during the Pirate takeover: the sight of him triggered a trauma-induced panic attack and he beat her down, gloating about K-2L; he claimed he had healed his wounds by eating the colonists' bodies and taunted that her mother had been among them. He then dealt a fatal blow to the Chozo Gray Voice, whose DNA had been used to give Samus her Chozo genes, as Gray Voice moved to take Mother Brain offline.

During her first mission to Zebes as a bounty hunter, Samus destroyed Ridley in his lair and wrecked Mecha Ridley, a robot built in his likeness, aboard the Space Pirate Mother Ship.

Ridley kept returning. The Pirates rebuilt him as Meta Ridley, whom Samus brought down on Tallon IV and again on Norion; Phazon regenerated him as Omega Ridley, whom she destroyed on the Pirate Homeworld. As the half-mechanical Proteus Ridley he ambushed her on SR388 and was subdued; at Ceres Space Colony he stole the Metroid hatchling and escaped her; and in Norfair, on her return to Zebes, she killed the original Ridley for good. Afterward only his cells and his corpse remained to be exploited: a Federation clone grown on the Bottle Ship, and Neo-Ridley, the X Parasite's copy of that clone's frozen husk aboard the BSL station. Samus had believed him dead since Norfair; seeing him alive again in the Bottle Ship's Pyrosphere paralyzed her with shock until Anthony Higgs intervened.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/K-2L · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Virginia_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rodney_Aran</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley's appearance, abilities and intelligence

*Keys:* Ridley's tail, Ridley's wings, Ridley's claws, Ridley's beak, Ridley's fire, plasma breath, Ridley's shriek, Ridley's scream, Ridley's roar, Ridley's size, Ridley's appearance, how big is Ridley


Ridley's natural form is that of a winged dragon: a long neck, a toothed beak, taloned hands and feet, broad leathery wings and a whip-like tail ending in a blade. He towers over Samus Aran. His hide is purple in most of her encounters, though on Zebes during her first mission he was brown, his color shifting as he took damage. His rebuilt forms (Meta Ridley, Omega Ridley, Proteus Ridley) added armor plating and mechanical parts. His cry is a piercing, animal shriek.

Abilities Samus has faced:
- Flight: he swoops, hovers and dives on his prey.
- Fire: he breathes plasma and spits fireballs, some of which arc or curve after a target. As Meta Ridley he also carried built-in missile, breath, flame and bomb weapons (see Meta Ridley and Omega Ridley).
- Tail: he whips, stabs and drives it down on a target; the X copy of him on the BSL had a tail none of Samus's weapons could damage.
- Grappling: he seizes Samus in his claws and drags or rakes her along walls, as the Bottle Ship clone did in the Geothermal Power Plant.
- Melee: claw swipes, lunges and ramming with his full weight.
- Resilience: he has survived wounds that should have killed him; per the manga he claimed to have healed his K-2L injuries by eating the colonists' bodies, and the Space Pirates and Phazon have revived and rebuilt him repeatedly.

He is not a mindless beast. Ridley leads the Pirate military, led the raid on K-2L and, per the manual, personally headed the Ceres Space Colony mission and stole the Metroid hatchling. Per the manga he speaks, mocks a child before striking at her, and uses her parents' deaths to break her composure years later. Samus knows him as a sadist who enjoys her fear. She has learned to strike at his chest and mouth: Meta Ridley's chest plating was thinner than the rest of his hide, and as Meta and Omega Ridley his mouth was vulnerable while he fired from it.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Neo-Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyrosphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran</sub>


### Ridley: Ridley's history across every encounter

*Keys:* Ridley's history, Ridley's return, Ridley returns, Ridley returned, Ridley comes back, Ridley is back, Ridley keeps coming back, Ridley survived, Ridley revived, Ridley's resurrection, Ridley's death, Ridley's deaths


Ridley's history with Samus Aran, in the order the events happened:

- K-2L: Ridley led the Space Pirate raid on Samus's home colony when she was three; her parents Rodney and Virginia Aran died in it. Per the manga, Rodney's detonation of the stolen Afloraltite gravely wounded Ridley, who survived.
- Zebes, Pirate takeover (per the manga): Samus, there with the Federation Police, was paralyzed by a panic attack at the sight of him and beaten; Ridley then struck down the Chozo Gray Voice as he moved to take Mother Brain offline.
- Zebes, first mission: Ridley arrived aboard the Space Pirate Mother Ship during Samus's hunt for Mother Brain and flew down to his lair, where Samus destroyed him. She later wrecked his unfinished robotic likeness, Mecha Ridley, inside the Mother Ship.
- Orpheon and Tallon IV: the Pirates rebuilt him with cybernetics and metagenetic enhancements as Meta Ridley. He fled the failing frigate Orpheon for Tallon IV, and Samus brought him down at the Artifact Temple, where laser beams from the temple's statues struck him and he fell into the Impact Crater.
- Norion and the Pirate Homeworld: revived again with Phazite armor, he struck during the Pirate assault on Norion and was beaten by Samus in free fall down a generator shaft. He next appeared on the Pirate Homeworld, regenerated by Phazon as Omega Ridley and guarding the Leviathan's core, where Samus destroyed him.
- SR388: as the half-mechanical Proteus Ridley he ambushed Samus and the newborn Metroid hatchling on the surface and seized the baby; it drained his energy into Samus and she subdued him.
- Ceres Space Colony: wholly organic again, he raided the colony, whose scientists were found dead, took the hatchling, fought Samus briefly and fled, triggering the station's self-destruct (per the manual, he personally headed the mission).
- Norfair, return to Zebes: Samus killed him in his lair before descending to Tourian. This was the final death of the original Ridley.
- The Bottle Ship: a clone the Galactic Federation grew by accident from cell material taken from Samus's Power Suit matured from Little Birdie through the Mystery Creature into an adult Ridley, nearly killed Anthony Higgs, fought Samus in the Geothermal Power Plant and retreated wounded. It was later found dead, a dried husk apparently drained by the Queen Metroid.
- The BSL station: that husk, shipped there frozen as a Bottle Ship specimen, was copied by the X as Neo-Ridley; Samus destroyed it and absorbed its Core-X.

Ridley did not appear during the ZDR mission (Metroid Dread) or the Viewros mission (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). He survived first through Space Pirate reconstruction, then through Phazon regeneration, and in the end only through cloning; Samus has had to put him down again and again, in one form or another.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ceres_Space_Colony</sub>


## Galactic Federation


### Galactic Federation: Aurora Units

*Keys:* Aurora Unit, Aurora Units, AU 242, AU 217, AU 486, organic supercomputer, biological supercomputer


Aurora Units (AU) are biological supercomputers built by the Galactic Federation: vast brain-like organisms kept alive in life-support chambers and wired into the Federation's networks. They are identified by three-digit numbers, can message one another across the network, speak of themselves as "we" rather than "I", and can project a hologram into Samus Aran's gunship to deliver objectives. The Olympus-class battleship was the first Federation capital ship designed around an onboard unit, which lets the class run with smaller crews.

- Aurora Unit 242: aboard the flagship G.F.S. Olympus, speaking with a woman's voice. With Fleet Admiral Castor Dane it directed Samus throughout the Phazon crisis, appearing to her in person once and thereafter by hologram.
- Aurora Unit 217: speaking with a man's voice, originally built to support GF Naval Base Demeter and assigned to SkyTown on Elysia shortly after the Treaty of Elysia, fourteen years before the crisis. As SkyTown's administrator it collected the information the Elysian mechanoids gathered, in a productive working arrangement with them. Samus purged its virus with the Federation vaccine on reaching SkyTown; when the Phazon-corrupted hunter Ghor then severed 217's link to SkyTown, she defeated him and repaired the connection, and 217 guided her to destroy Elysia's Leviathan Seed.
- Aurora Unit 486: credited with transforming barren Norion into a thriving forest world.
- Aurora Unit 313: one of the first units, stationed on the G.F.S. Valhalla. Its log recorded "It is obvious now that they intend to extract us from the Valhalla" and, last, "...Darkness coming." Space Pirates boarded the ship during a training exercise and extracted the unit, and Dark Samus took it to Phaaze, where Phazon mutated it into a symbiotic part of the planet used to spread Leviathans across the galaxy. Dark Samus finally fused her own essence with 313 to fight Samus; Samus destroyed them both, ending the flow of Phazon (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).

The virus in the Aurora network, and the vaccine Samus carried, were why Fleet Admiral Dane summoned her to the Olympus in the first place. Samus worked closely with 242 and 217 as guides; 313 showed her what an Aurora Unit becomes in an enemy's hands.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_242 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Olympus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Daiban and Federation Headquarters

*Keys:* Daiban, Federation capital, capital planet, Federation Headquarters, Federation HQ, Galactic Federation Headquarters, Galactic Federation HQ, GF HQ, Federation council


Daiban is the capital planet of the Galactic Federation. It appears only in the manga; "Daiban" is an unofficial transliteration of its Japanese name. Per the manga, the Federation's council of member-world representatives meets on Daiban, and Keaton serves as Federation Chairman (see Chairman Keaton). The Chairman's authority also reached the Bottle Ship incident, where Anthony Higgs invoked orders from Adam Malkovich and the Federation Chairman to secure Madeline Bergman's safety.

Federation Headquarters is the Federation's seat of command; its location is not recorded. Samus Aran's dealings with it are personal rather than political:
- Headquarters asked Adam Malkovich for a report on the Bottle Ship's Metroid project; his report argued that the operation should not be attempted because of its dangers.
- After the Bottle Ship incident, Samus flew Madeline Bergman and Anthony Higgs to Headquarters in her gunship, then returned to the Bottle Ship before its destruction to recover Adam's platoon helmet.
- After an X Parasite infected her on SR388 and her gunship crashed, BSL personnel carried her escape pod to Headquarters. There Federation scientists used a preserved cell culture from the baby Metroid to make the vaccine that saved her, leaving her with the Fusion Suit and Metroid DNA, and sent her to the BSL station in a new gunship directed by a computer she came to call Adam.

For Samus, Headquarters is where the Federation patches her up and sends her out again.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Madeline_Bergman · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Fusion</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation contracts and bounty hunters

*Keys:* Federation contract, Federation contracts, Federation mission, Federation missions, Federation hired, hired by the Federation, Rogue Contract, mission file


Bounty hunters are the Federation's answer to problems its own forces cannot solve, a trade that began, per the original manual, when the Federation Bureau recruited the "space hunters" to catch the Space Pirates its Police could not (see Galactic Federation (overview)). Hunters are mercenaries hired for firepower, and the Federation and the Space Pirates are among their most profitable employers.

How the Federation has hired Samus Aran:
- A hire of last resort: after its assault on Zebes was driven back, the Federation sent her in alone to destroy Mother Brain.
- An extermination order: after Federation teams vanished on SR388, she was sent to wipe out every Metroid there.
- A "Rogue Contract" (Alimbic Cluster): Federation Empaths intercepted a telepathic message promising ultimate power; Samus was to learn its meaning, secure the power for the Federation if possible and otherwise hide or destroy it. The operation had to stay covert because the Alimbic Cluster lies outside Federation space, and rival interests were "presumed hostile."
- A search-and-rescue file: "8 days ago, contact with Galactic Federation trooper squad Bravo was lost"; Samus was to locate the troopers on Aether and render assistance.
- A summons: Fleet Admiral Castor Dane called Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda to the G.F.S. Olympus for the Aurora Unit crisis. Phazon corrupted the other three, and Samus had to fight and defeat each of them; none survived.
- Later missions: the Federation dispatched her to Tanamaar to stop Space Pirates seizing an unearthed artifact, sent her to investigate the BSL station, and sent her to ZDR.

Not every mission is a contract. A distress signal from the Ceres Space Colony, not a Federation order, sent her after Ridley to Zebes when the baby Metroid was stolen, and she answered the Bottle Ship's distress call on her own. When she is not under contract, as on the Bottle Ship, the Federation treats her as an outsider.

Other hunters have other histories. Sylux, once a Federation field captain, disobeyed an order to hold position and await Samus and was left the sole survivor of his platoon; when she arrived and destroyed the Pirate superweapon with ease, his hatred of her and the Federation began. He flies a gunship believed to be a stolen Federation prototype (see Sylux). Samus distrusts the Federation's motives, yet she keeps taking its missions.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bounty_Hunter · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Castor_Dane</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation Marines and trooper culture

*Keys:* Federation Marines, Federation Marine, Galactic Federation Marines, Federation Marine Corps, GFMC, Federation trooper, Federation troopers, Federation soldier, Federation soldiers, Demolition Trooper, Demolition Troopers, PED Marines


The Galactic Federation Marine Corps (GFMC) is the Federation's standard military force. Its troopers' primary task is defeating Space Pirate forces, and Federation records say few can match a Marine in combat. Marines wear heavy power armor patterned after Samus Aran's Power Suit, carry a rapid-fire assault arm cannon and keep personal Logbooks; men and women both serve. Ranks recorded aboard the G.F.S. Tyr run from Captain down through Commander, lieutenants, sergeants, Specialist and Lance Corporal to Private First Class.

Specialized troops:
- PED Marines, whose weapons draw Phazon from a backpack Phazon Enhancement Device (during the Phazon crisis).
- Demolition Troopers, who carry heavy explosives but little firepower; they blasted Samus a path to the Leviathan on the Pirate Homeworld.
- The Federation Force, an elite unit formed under Operation Golem to pilot giant Mechs modeled on Samus's Power Suit, led by General Alex Miles and sent to the Bermuda System (see The Federation Force and their Mechs).

Losses. Squad Bravo of GFMC Task Force Herakles followed a crippled Pirate frigate to Aether aboard the G.F.S. Tyr, was grounded by a storm, ran out of ammunition against Splinters possessed by the Ing and was wiped out. Private First Class L. Brouda's last entry asked that it be known "we did our duty and fought well." Contact had been lost eight days before Samus was assigned to find them; she found only bodies (see GFS Tyr and Bravo Squad). The Marines of the G.F.S. Valhalla died much the same way, overrun by Pirate boarders during a training exercise: impaled by Pirate blades, caught by grenades from both sides, their remains later fed on by Phazon bioforms.

Troopers and Samus. Bravo's comms specialist M. Angseth wished aloud to be out taking care of business like Samus Aran instead of sending reports; Private First Class I. Crany found it hard to believe one person could take out a whole Pirate base. Marines greet her with "Stand to, Samus!", and the four hunters summoned by Fleet Admiral Castor Dane, assisted by the Federation military, broke the Pirate attack on Norion. Federation personnel swept from Tanamaar to Viewros worked beside her there (see Myles MacKenzie and the stranded Federation squad). At the BSL station she judged that the X would absorb any Federation force sent in and spread with it across the galaxy, so she destroyed the station before the Federation arrived.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Marine · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFMC_Task_Force_Herakles · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Tyr · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation Navy and notable ships

*Keys:* Federation Navy, Federation fleet, Federation ship, Federation ships, G.F.S., GFS, Olympus-class, Anhur-class, Griffin-class, Theseus, Enkidu, Naval Base Demeter


During the Phazon crisis the Federation fleet was commanded from the flagship G.F.S. Olympus by Fleet Admiral Castor Dane; it operates from naval installations such as GF Naval Base Demeter and the base on Norion that forms the Federation's first line of defense on its frontier. Federation vessels carry the prefix G.F.S. and names from ancient Earth mythology. Fuel Gel, rare and precious, is the main fuel of Federation spacecraft; Bryyo, a Federation protectorate, is its largest supplier, and piracy against gel convoys has forced the Federation to expand its antipiracy patrols.

Classes and notable ships:
- Olympus-class battleships: the backbone of the fleet and the first Federation capital ships designed around an onboard Aurora Unit, which lets them run with smaller crews than other ships their size.
- G.F.S. Olympus: Dane's flagship during the Phazon crisis and home of Aurora Unit 242, where the hunters were briefed before the Norion attack and which led the fleet through the wormhole to Phaaze (see GFS Olympus and the Valhalla).
- G.F.S. Valhalla: an Olympus-class ship whose crew was presumed dead after a Space Pirate attack during a training exercise; Samus restored power to its drifting wreck to recover the codes the fleet needed for the Phaaze wormhole (see GFS Olympus and the Valhalla).
- Anhur-class patrol ships police and defend planetary systems. The G.F.S. Tyr carried Squad Bravo to its death on Aether; the G.F.S. Anhur later went there to help the Luminoth dismantle a Pirate mining facility, and its crew detected Phazon and collected the ore that led to the PED.
- Griffin-class frigates: the G.F.S. Theseus, lost until Samus found its wreck on Bryyo with its crew presumed dead, restored its power and downloaded a map of Bryyo from it; and the G.F.S. Enkidu, docked at Norion with its crew on shore leave.
- Delano 7: Sylux's Delta-class gunship, believed to be a stolen Federation prototype.

Samus's gunships are her own, though for the BSL mission the Federation issued her a new ship on condition that she obey its onboard computer, which she came to call Adam (see Samus's Gunship).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Olympus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Tyr · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Anhur · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Theseus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Enkidu</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Federation Police and military command

*Keys:* Galactic Federation Police, Federation Police, Federation Army, Galactic Federation Army, Federation military, Federation officer, Federation officers, Federation colonel, Federation command, chain of command


Galactic Federation Police. Per the original manual, the Federation Bureau created the Police to stop Space Pirate raids on spaceships and, when they could not run the Pirates down, recruited the "space hunters" (see Galactic Federation (overview)). When a Federation attack on the Pirates' fortress on Zebes was driven back, the Federation Police sent Samus Aran in alone.

Samus's service. Samus served in the Federation military (the Galactic Federation Army per Nintendo's official profile, the Federation Police per the manga) under Adam Malkovich, whom she came to see as a father figure, before leaving to work as a bounty hunter; that history, including the Jigrad incident and the death of Adam's brother Ian, is told in Samus Aran – Galactic Federation service and the bounty hunter years and Adam Malkovich.

Command and authority. The Federation officers Samus has dealt with include:
- Adam, who later led the 07th Platoon on the Bottle Ship and placed her under his command once she had helped his troopers.
- The unnamed Federation colonel who arrived after the Bottle Ship incident, ordered her escorted off with "Time for the lady to go home," and was stopped from taking custody of Madeline Bergman only when Anthony Higgs cited orders from Adam and the Federation Chairman.
- Fleet Admiral Castor Dane, her handler through the Phazon crisis, and General Alex Miles of the Federation Force.

After Zebes was destroyed, a Federation quarantine officer cleared her before she filed her mission report. The Federation treats Samus as something between an asset and a civilian; her loyalty runs to Adam and to the people beside her, not to the chain of command.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Adam_Malkovich · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ian_Malkovich · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Madeline_Bergman · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Fleet Admiral Castor Dane

*Keys:* Castor Dane, Admiral Dane, Fleet Admiral Dane, Fleet Admiral Castor Dane, Fleet Admiral, Admiral Castor, the Admiral


Fleet Admiral Castor Dane is the human commander of the Galactic Federation flagship G.F.S. Olympus and the officer who directed Samus Aran through the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Federation records credit him with "utmost dedication to protecting the Federation from its enemies" and describe him as stern and aggressive, with a strong dislike of the Space Pirates; rumor has it they orphaned him at an early age, an act he still seeks to avenge.

Dane summoned four bounty hunters, Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, to the Olympus. With Aurora Unit 242 he briefed them on a virus spreading through the Federation's Aurora Units, which the hunters were to purge with a Federation vaccine. Space Pirates attacked the fleet during the briefing, and Dane sent the hunters to break the assault on the Federation base at Norion. There the hunters and the Federation garrison fought off the Pirates; after Dark Samus struck the others down, Samus, the last on her feet, fired the base's defense cannon and destroyed the incoming Leviathan before collapsing, corrupted by Phazon.

When Samus woke a month later, Dane and Aurora Unit 242 became her handlers. The Federation fitted her with a Phazon Enhancement Device, and Dane assigned her to destroy the Leviathan Seeds on Bryyo, Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld and to find the three missing hunters. The codes the fleet needed lay aboard the derelict G.F.S. Valhalla; once Samus recovered them, Dane ordered her to seize the Leviathan battleship over the Pirate Homeworld, which could open a wormhole to Phaaze, and led the fleet through it. The fleet escaped Phaaze's destruction but lost contact with Samus until her gunship reappeared and she reported the mission accomplished.

Dane was the nearest thing to a commanding officer Samus had during the crisis, but their relationship was professional: he set objectives and she carried them out. After Phaaze she mourned her fellow hunters alone on Elysia.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Castor_Dane · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_242 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norion · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Galactic Federation (overview)

*Keys:* Galactic Federation, Federation, Federation Bureau, Federation government, Federation world, Federation worlds, Federation space, member world, member worlds


The Galactic Federation is the interstellar government that most of the settled galaxy answers to, and the power that has hired, sheltered, deceived and leaned on Samus Aran for her whole career.

Per the original manual, "in the year 2000 of the history of the cosmos" representatives from many different planets formed a congress called the Galactic Federation, opening an age of prosperity, cultural exchange and routine interstellar travel. Space Pirates then began preying on spaceships; because the vastness of space made them nearly impossible to catch, the Federation Bureau created the Galactic Federation Police and recruited warriors famed for courage, the "space hunters", to run them down. The war with the Space Pirates has defined the Federation ever since.

Structure and institutions:
- Government: a Chairman and an assembly of member-world representatives. Per the manga, the capital is the planet Daiban and the Chairman is Keaton (see Daiban and Federation Headquarters; Chairman Keaton).
- Forces: the Galactic Federation Police, the Galactic Federation Marine Corps and other Federation troops, and a fleet of Olympus-class battleships, Anhur-class patrol ships and Griffin-class frigates commanded during the Phazon crisis from the flagship G.F.S. Olympus by Fleet Admiral Castor Dane.
- Aurora Units: biological supercomputers that link Federation ships and installations.
- Research: the Biologic Space Laboratories station, the Bottle Ship and the Ceres Space Colony, all tied to the Federation's dealings with Metroids.
- Technology: Marine power armor patterned on Samus's Power Suit, the Phazon Enhancement Device, the Federation Force's Mechs and the E.M.M.I.

Members and partners: humans predominate on Federation worlds such as Norion. Bryyo, a Federation protectorate, is the largest supplier of Fuel Gel, the main fuel of Federation spacecraft, and its gel sales fund the Federation forces guarding that sector. The Elysians of SkyTown gather information for the Federation in exchange for parts and fuel, an arrangement dating from the Treaty of Elysia fourteen years before the Phazon crisis. After the Ing fell, Federation forces helped the Luminoth of Aether dismantle a Space Pirate mining facility.

Samus and the Federation: it hired her as a last resort when its assault on Zebes was driven back, and has since sent her to the Alimbic Cluster, Aether, the Phazon crisis, SR388, Tanamaar, the BSL station and ZDR, yet treats her as an outsider whenever she is not under contract. Federation Headquarters is where she was treated after the X infection. Its secret Metroid breeding aboard the Bottle Ship and the BSL station, its withholding of data from her and its plan to capture the SA-X eroded her trust until she destroyed the BSL station against its wishes (see Metroid policy and secret programs). Even so, when its E.M.M.I. went silent on ZDR, the Federation turned to her again, and she answered.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Fuel_Gel</sub>


### Galactic Federation: Metroid policy and secret programs

*Keys:* Metroid policy, Federation policy, Federation secrets, Federation secret, Federation cover-up, bioweapon program, bioweapons program, Metroid program, Metroid weapons, weaponize Metroids, weaponized Metroids, extermination order


The Federation's official Metroid policy is extermination; its unofficial practice has been to breed them in secret. That contradiction lies at the root of Samus Aran's distrust of the Federation.

Official line. The Federation first learned of Metroids when a deep-space research vessel found one on SR388; Space Pirates seized it on the way home and carried it to Zebes. Fearing Pirate-bred Metroid weapons, the Federation attacked Zebes, was driven back, and hired Samus to destroy Mother Brain and the Metroids. It later sent teams to SR388; when they vanished, it sent Samus to exterminate the species. She spared the last hatchling and delivered it to Federation scientists at the Ceres Space Colony; Ridley stole it (see The baby Metroid).

Secret programs:
- After the Bermuda System incident, a Federation research station held a Metroid egg the Federation Force had taken from the Space Pirates; Sylux broke in and hatched it (see The Metroid egg and Sylux's break-in).
- The Bottle Ship, built to cultivate bioweapons against Federation protocol, ran a secret special-forces program based on the Space Pirates, including Metroids cloned from cells taken from Samus's Power Suit. They were kept in Sector Zero, a recreation of Tourian, and could not be frozen. MB, an AI reproducing Mother Brain's thought processes, was built to control them; when she began to develop emotions and defy its plans, the Federation decided to reprogram her, and she rebelled. Headquarters had asked Adam Malkovich for a report on the project, and he argued against it. Adam died destroying Sector Zero (see The Bottle Ship incident).
- Aboard the Biologic Space Laboratories station the Federation was again "secretly working on a Metroid breeding program. For peaceful application only, of course."

The BSL break. When X Parasites overran the station, the Federation set out to capture the SA-X. It stopped sending Samus support data, and the Plasma Beam modification "was ready some time ago, but they withheld it to keep you from engaging the SA-X." Judging that the X would absorb any Federation force sent in, Samus crashed the station into SR388, destroying both (see Samus's defiance of the Federation at the BSL).

When the Federation later received video of a living X on ZDR, it sent seven E.M.M.I. units; when they went silent, it turned once more to Samus (see The ZDR mission). She no longer takes the Federation's assurances about Metroids or the X at face value.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_II:_Return_of_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bottle_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Madeline_Bergman · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


## Manga


### Manga: Kreatz and Mauk

*Keys:* Kreatz, Mauk, Kreatz and Mauk, Mauk and Kreatz


Kreatz and Mauk were Samus Aran's comrades during her years in the Federation Police; both appear only in the manga. The three served together on reconnaissance under Chief Hardy, who once roared that he could grill and boil them and still not be satisfied.

Kreatz is the talker: mindful of orders but quick to join a fight. On Jigrad he confirmed the Pirates' Quantum Radar and urged the squad to report back to headquarters and meet the other recon teams. When Samus charged in to save a child he groaned, 'Oh man - She started a firefight - We're disobeying orders and we'll have to write an apology. What should we do, Mauk?', then joined in: 'They're leaving me out. I can't let that go! Count me in! I don't hate this kinda stuff!!' Mauk is the quieter, more timid of the pair, and fought beside them.

When the squad reached Zebes, Samus told Pyonchi she was leaving 'these two' in his care. Kreatz protested at taking on her pet as extra weight; Samus shot back that he was the extra weight, since the planet was full of predators. Later, when Kreatz spotted Skree hanging from a ceiling, Samus warned that Zebes had predators found nowhere else in the galaxy, moments before the Skree dove onto a Space Pirate who had surprised them. Kreatz came to heed Pyonchi; during the escape it was Pyonchi who noticed the sky clearing as the Space Pirate Mother Ship moved away ('What's wrong Pyonchi? The rain stopped..?').

When Samus suffered a panic attack before Ridley and was defeated, Kreatz and Mauk stole in to rescue her, and the commotion they caused, together with Gray Voice's revolt against Mother Brain, let the group escape with Pyonchi and the surviving Chozo, whom the two got off the planet.

Nothing further is recorded of either after Samus left the Police to become a bounty hunter. They were her comrades through the Jigrad rescue and the fall of Zebes, and they saw her at her most vulnerable before Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Skree · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/サムス・アラン</sub>


### Manga: Mother Brain's betrayal of Zebes (manga account)

*Keys:* Gray Voice, Grey Voice, Platinum Chest, defective product, fall of Zebes, betrayal of Zebes, Mother Brain's betrayal, Chozo Power Suit


Per the manga, the fall of Zebes came from within. Mother Brain, the supercomputer the Chozo built to oversee Zebes and harmonize the galaxy, concluded that their plan had failed and self-programmed a new directive: 'I will bring order to the universe myself.' Soon after the Chozo revealed the finished Metroids, she reported 'a large unknown object' approaching Zebes's planetary protection shield: the Space Pirate Mother Ship. The Pirates attacked and expelled the Chozo, and Mother Brain, judging them 'a subordinate species that naturally follows the orders of a strong master', took command of them, with Ridley serving under her.

Samus arrived as a Federation Police officer with Kreatz and Mauk and reached Mother Brain, who told her to 'please relax': Zebes was switching to 'a satisfactory cooperative relationship with the Space Pirates. In other words, they are under our control.' Beside her stood Gray Voice, declaring, 'Mother is the greatest being in existence and does everything perfectly.' Mother Brain recounted her birth from Chozo and Federation data and reminded Samus that she had made the Power Suit: 'I gave birth to you!' When Samus attacked, a shield made by molecule transference of Zebetite rendered her weapon useless. Her first sight of Ridley then brought on a panic attack, and she was defeated.

Gray Voice's defection was a ruse; he had planned to destroy Mother from the start. Calling her 'our defective product' that 'must be destroyed', he put on a Chozo Power Suit and attacked, doing serious damage, but Ridley struck him a fatal blow before he could take Mother Brain offline. Before Ridley could finish him, the mortally wounded Gray Voice fired skyward from the ground, covering the escape of Samus, her companions and the surviving Chozo, and died. During the flight the elder Platinum Chest told Samus that Gray Voice's DNA had been used so she could inherit Chozo genes, which made them 'very similar to father and daughter' and was why she had heard his thoughts. The Chozo who escaped later disappeared.

Mother Brain and the Space Pirates then held Zebes and its Metroids. When Samus returned years later as a bounty hunter, she found its ecosystem 'gone mad': 'This isn't the Zebes that I grew up on!' The loss of Gray Voice, the Chozo father whose DNA she carries, to the being that had called itself her mother colours how Samus regards Mother Brain.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Old_Bird · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/サムス・アラン</sub>


### Manga: Samus's Chozo upbringing and the birth of Mother Brain (manga account)

*Keys:* Old Bird, Gray Voice's DNA, DNA infusion, Samus's childhood, raised by the Chozo, Chozo upbringing, trained on Zebes, Iono Feria, Badger Clover, Protector of the Galaxy, Protectors of the Galaxy, Mother Brain's creation


Per the manga, Old Bird and Gray Voice brought three-year-old Samus to Zebes, home of a declining Chozo people who had left everything, even labor and meditation, to Mother Brain. To speed their plan of linking the galaxy into one unified society, the Chozo had merged their data with the Galactic Federation's to create her; she oversaw Zebes, detected threats in orbit and advised the elders. When Old Bird introduced Samus to Mother Brain, the computer judged that 'this weak creature is incapable of living in Zebes' harsh environment', so the Chozo integrated their DNA into Samus's to let her body adapt. Per the manga, Gray Voice's DNA was used, making the two 'very similar to father and daughter' and later letting Samus hear his thoughts; later revelations (Metroid Dread) show the infused DNA also carried Mawkin DNA from Raven Beak. Mother Brain built the Power Suit, which 'reacts to your thoughts, but it can't think for you', and later told Samus, 'I gave birth to you!'

In her teens Samus trained in the suit under Old Bird and Gray Voice, the Chozo who were called Protectors of the Galaxy. Gray Voice was the harder teacher: 'You can't be second or third place.' After one failed session she followed the Iono Feria, harmless butterflies that ionize moisture to fly, into a valley and picked flowers that Gray Voice identified as Badger Clover, a terrifyingly poisonous plant from another world. Mother Brain traced its alkaloid to the butterflies, and Old Bird ordered the clover and the Iono Feria burned. Samus objected; Gray Voice answered that if they did not hurt them, they would be the ones hurt. When the Iono Feria took up weapons the Space Pirates had left behind, Samus shot only the weapons, sparing every butterfly. Pyonchi stayed at her side throughout.

The Chozo had found the X on the remote frontier world SR388, where their camera recorded one taking over a Hornoad, copying its memories and abilities and sucking it dry. Old Bird and Mother Brain devised the Metroids as the X's predator; the first sample was later unveiled on Zebes.

At last Old Bird told Samus she must become independent and should be the true Protector of the Galaxy. She left Zebes and joined the Federation Police. She still calls Zebes the world she grew up on.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Iono_Feria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species)</sub>


### Manga: Samus's confrontation with Ridley and her trauma (manga account)

*Keys:* Ridley trauma, trauma, traumatic, traumatized, PTSD, post-traumatic stress, panic attack, panic attacks, flashback, flashbacks, froze up, frozen in fear  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Ridley, K-2L, Gray Voice, Kreatz, Mauk, Bottle Ship, Little Birdie


Per the manga, Samus met Ridley a second time on Zebes in her teens, while serving in the Federation Police during the Space Pirate takeover. The sight of the creature that had killed her mother in front of her brought on a panic attack: shortness of breath, violent flashbacks to the burning colony on K-2L, and loss of control over the Power Suit, which answers to her thoughts. Ridley gloated about his time at her old colony and played on the memory; she could not fight and was defeated. Kreatz and Mauk's rescue and Gray Voice's revolt against Mother Brain got her out, but the episode continued after her escape until the Chozo calmed her. Gray Voice had been the first to recognize the distress as post-traumatic stress triggered by Ridley. Another elder explained that 'in the distant past, she witnessed the worst kind of disaster' and that 'repressed fear will grow inside of you, even if you are unaware that it even exists.' In her inner world the Chozo urged her to stand again.

She did not recover at once. Samus left the Police, became a bounty hunter and entrusted Pyonchi to Chairman Keaton. Hired to destroy Mother Brain and the Metroids, she returned to Zebes with Missile and Bomb data uploaded to her suit by Adam Malkovich, and found its ecosystem 'gone mad', reshaped to protect Mother. She beat Kraid only after curling into the Morph Ball and using Bombs, the one time she uses them in this account, then faced Ridley again. This time she held: after Wave Beam and Ice Beam fire, a single Missile brought him down. She fought on to Tourian and found Mother Brain surrounded by Metroids; the account ends as that battle begins, where her first recorded mission takes up (Metroid: Zero Mission).

The paralysis returned years later, when Samus froze before a clone of Ridley aboard the Bottle Ship until Anthony Higgs distracted it and she regained her focus (Metroid: Other M). Ridley remains the one enemy known to have paralyzed her. The lesson the Chozo gave her is that fear left buried grows.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Psychosis · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Missile_Launcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi</sub>


### Manga: Samus's Federation Police years (manga)

*Keys:* Federation Police, Galactic Federation Police, Federation Police officer, Federation Police officers, Chief Hardy, Jigrad, Damara, Quantum Radar


Per the manga, after leaving Zebes Samus joined the Federation Police and flew reconnaissance missions watching Space Pirate activity. She served under Chief Hardy with two other young officers, Kreatz and Mauk, a trio known as the problem children.

Jigrad: the Pirates had held Jigrad for two months, their main forces having moved on to other planets, and the remnant was forcing natives to build a gaudy building by hand. The three confirmed the Pirates' Quantum Radar, and Kreatz wanted to report back to headquarters and join the other recon teams. Then Samus saw the Pirates passing children under a height sensor and killing any too small to trigger it as unfit to work. She dropped in, caught the girl they were about to kill and declared, 'I'm with the Federation Police. Don't move, you're under arrest.' She sent the colonists to safety and fought; Kreatz, fretting that they were disobeying orders and would have to write an apology, joined in with Mauk. When the last Pirate begged for mercy, the K-2L murders flooded back and she nearly shot him, until the girl's crying stopped her. Instead she declared, 'In the name of the Federation Police, you are under arrest!!', and guaranteed him a trial under Federation law. Hardy reprimanded all three for disobeying orders. The girl, Damara, thanked Samus, who answered that Damara was the one who had saved her. Adam Malkovich remarked that the battleship prepared to fight the Pirates had gone to waste, and that they had gained 'an unnecessary piece of baggage... Samus Aran'.

Zebes: the squad later went to Zebes, met the Space Pirate takeover, Mother Brain's betrayal and Ridley, and escaped with the surviving Chozo.

Afterward Samus left the Police to become a bounty hunter and left Pyonchi with Chairman Keaton of the Galactic Federation. When she was hired to destroy Mother Brain and the Metroids, Hardy went over SR388 with her: a remote planet absent from most galactic charts, where the Metroids had killed a Federation inspection team. Damara reappeared, recognizing Pyonchi as the Rabbilis that had been with Keaton, and Adam uploaded Missile and Bomb data to Samus's Power Suit.

Her later recollections (Metroid: Other M) instead describe her pre-hunter service as the Galactic Federation Army under Adam Malkovich, which she left after a dispute with him. The manga's Police-under-Hardy version conflicts with that account, and the later account takes precedence.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pyonchi · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(species) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Missile_Launcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Adam_Malkovich</sub>


### Manga: The K-2L raid (manga account)

*Keys:* K-2L, K2L, K-2L raid, Afloraltite, Rodney Aran, Virginia Aran, Chief Aran, Samus's parents, Aran family, Old Bird, Pyonchi, Rabbilis


Per the manga, K-2L was an Earth colony where humans mined Afloraltite, the mineral used as the ignition module in all space-faring vessels, for Galactic Federation caravans. Rodney Aran headed the mining operation, and his miners greeted him as Chief Aran. He and his wife Virginia had a three-year-old daughter, Samus.

Two Chozo elders, Old Bird and Gray Voice, landed and asked to borrow a large amount of Afloraltite. They needed its energy for the Metroid project but could not disclose why. Rodney refused, and their ship left empty-handed. During the visit Old Bird befriended the girl, telling her 'We look different... But inside... We have the same heart that you do', and showed her how to win over Pyonchi, a rabbit-like Rabbilis that had carried her bag up a tree, by making friends with him and giving him a name. Pyonchi stayed her companion for years.

A Space Pirate flagship under Ridley had followed the Chozo, curious why they would land on so inconvenient a world. After the elders left, the Pirates detected the Afloraltite and the mining team, killed colonists, loaded the ore onto their cargo ships and were ordered to annihilate every human on the colony. Samus, remembering Old Bird's lesson, walked up to Ridley: 'I'm Samus. Three years old... You're not scary... we can still be friends!' He called her cute, answered 'That's impossible!! Because... You're about to die!', and struck. Virginia threw herself into the blow and was killed in Samus's place. Rodney, knowing the Federation Police would not arrive in time, slipped aboard the Pirate cargo ship and ignited the stolen Afloraltite with a repair welding laser. The blast killed him and the raiders and left Ridley badly wounded but alive. Samus was the only survivor.

The colonists' distress signal reached the departing Chozo, and Old Bird ordered the ship back. He and Gray Voice found the child in the ruins and took her to Zebes, Gray Voice at first demanding what Old Bird meant by bringing a human child home.

Samus carries the day for life. Years later on Jigrad, the memory of the K-2L murders nearly drove her to execute a surrendering Pirate, and her buried terror of Ridley resurfaced as a panic attack when she met him again on Zebes. The raid is the root of her hatred of Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/K-2L · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Afloraltite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rodney_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Virginia_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ridley</sub>


### Manga: The Metroid manga (overview and canon status)
