*Keys:* Metroid manga, Magazine Z manga, Samus manga, e-manga, Monthly Magazine Z, Magazine Z, Kouji Tazawa, Kenji Ishikawa, Episode of Aether, Comic BomBom, Comic Bom Bom


The Metroid manga is the official sixteen-chapter account of Samus Aran's early life, published by Kodansha under Nintendo's license, with script by Kouji Tazawa and art by Kenji Ishikawa. Its first two chapters were posted online in Japan in 2002 as an official Flash e-manga with sound and music. The story was serialized in Kodansha's Monthly Magazine Z between 2002 and 2004 and collected in two volumes of seven and nine chapters.

It follows Samus from age three to the eve of her first mission against Mother Brain on Zebes: Ridley's Space Pirate raid on K-2L and the deaths of Rodney and Virginia Aran; her adoption by the Chozo elders Old Bird and Gray Voice; the infusion of Chozo DNA and her training in the Power Suit on Zebes; the Metroids, designed on Zebes by Old Bird and Mother Brain and released on SR388 to prey on the X; her service in the Federation Police beside Kreatz and Mauk under Chief Hardy; the Pirate seizure of Zebes and Mother Brain's betrayal; Gray Voice's death at Ridley's hands; her panic attack before Ridley; and her recovery and return to Zebes as a bounty hunter.

Nintendo has never directly confirmed or denied the manga's canon status, but it is Nintendo-licensed, and Nintendo Treehouse's Nate Bihldorff, defending Samus's paralysis before Ridley (Metroid: Other M), pointed to 'the official manga related to her childhood'.

Conflicts with later accounts:
- Per the manga, Samus's pre-hunter service was in the Federation Police under Chief Hardy, with Adam Malkovich only a dismissive outsider. Her later recollections place that service in the Galactic Federation Army under Adam, which she left after a dispute with him; where the two differ, the later account takes precedence.
- Per the manga, only Gray Voice's DNA was infused; later revelations (Metroid Dread) show the infused Chozo DNA also included Mawkin DNA from Raven Beak.
- Per the manga, the Chozo sought Afloraltite on K-2L and were refused, and the Pirates struck only after shadowing them; Mother Brain, not the Chozo directly, built the Power Suit.

A separate Japan-only manga, Metroid Prime: Episode of Aether by Hisashi Matsumoto, retold the opening of Samus's Aether mission in seven chapters in Kodansha's Comic BomBom from July 2005 to January 2006; it is not part of this backstory. The 1986 Wanpakku strategy manga, Samus & Joey and Metroid EX are not canon.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_(manga) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(manga) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_e-manga · https://myanimelist.net/manga/6642/_ · https://hakutaku.us/2018/06/16/metroid-manga-universe/ · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Other_M</sub>


## Zebes


### Zebes: Chozodia, the Ruins Test and the Space Pirate Mother Ship

*Keys:* Chozodia, Pirate Mother Ship, Pirate Mothership, Ruins Test, Legendary Power Suit, Fully Powered Suit, Unknown Item, Unknown Items, Mecha Ridley, Mecha-Ridley, Paralyzer, Chozo city


Chozodia is a region of Zebes bordering Crateria near the surface: the ruins of a Chozo city, left empty after the Chozo fled the planet (per the manga). When Samus came there on her first mission nearly every living thing she met was a Space Pirate, because the Space Pirate Mother Ship had landed in the ruins.

The Mother Ship was the vessel Ridley used. It set down in Chozodia while Samus was fighting through Norfair, shortly before she entered Ridley's lair, and it was still there when Pirate fighters shot down her gunship as she left the planet. She crash-landed beside it without her Power Suit and infiltrated it in the Zero Suit, carrying the Paralyzer, an auto-charging pistol that stuns Pirates but cannot kill them.

From the ship she passed into the ruins and found the Ruins Test, a test handed down by the Chozo: a mirror-like guardian holding an image of Samus, which hurt her if she fired on it while it lay dormant, glowed only briefly to expose itself, and once struck tore free of the wall to hurl lightning. Each true hit set a Chozo symbol into one of four slots on the wall; with the fourth the guardian returned to the wall and showed Samus herself in full armor. Claiming that image gave her the Legendary Power Suit, a more advanced Chozo armor with the broad pauldrons of her later Varia Suit. It restored all her lost abilities and could integrate technology the old suit could only register as unknown: the Plasma Beam, the Space Jump and the Gravity Suit. Chozodia also yielded Power Bombs.

Armored again, Samus went back into the Mother Ship and climbed to its summit, where Mecha Ridley waited, a robotic image Ridley had made of himself to demonstrate his power, still half finished. Its defeat lit its eyes red and started the ship's self-destruct; Samus took a small Pirate craft and escaped as the Mother Ship exploded (Metroid: Zero Mission).

Samus kept the Legendary Power Suit after Zebes. It was lost only when its X-infected parts were surgically removed after her infection on SR388, shortly before the BSL incident, and the Fusion Suit took its place (Metroid Fusion). In Chozodia, the empty city of the people who raised her, the Chozo's own test judged her worthy of their legendary armor.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozodia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Mother_Ship · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ruins_Test · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Legendary_Power_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Unknown_Item · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mecha_Ridley</sub>


### Zebes: Kraid

*Keys:* Kraid, Kraids, Mini Kraid, Mini-Kraid, Mini Kraids, Fake Kraid


Kraid is an enormous reptilian creature who served the Space Pirates on Zebes as one of Mother Brain's two chief lieutenants, alongside Ridley. He laired in the depths of Brinstar beyond a red door, in a tall grey chamber hung with curved ceiling spikes that opened onto long purple corridors pooled with acid; the rebuilt lair lay in Brinstar's lower reaches, east of the elevator to Norfair, mechanical, bolted and panelled, its walls turning green near his den. Per the manuals he is "the largest enemy in all of the mazes beneath the surface of Planet Zebes" and "the giant lizard of Brinstar".

He is so huge that his body fills a chamber from floor to ceiling. He spits rocks from his mouth, fires spikes from his belly and flings claws from his fingertips; the belly spikes jut far enough to stand on. Per one manual, no beam, Missile or Bomb has any effect on his body, and on Zebes Samus brought him down by firing Missiles, Super Missiles or charged shots into his open mouth. In the rebuilt lair a smaller, weaker look-alike called Mini Kraid guarded the approach two rooms before him.

Samus defeated Kraid on her first mission to open the way to Tourian and claimed the Speed Booster from his lair; per the manga, she beat him with the help of the Morph Ball Bombs that Adam Malkovich supplied. She defeated him again in the rebuilt base on her return and claimed the Varia Suit. Kraid nonetheless outlived Zebes: during the ZDR mission Samus found him in Cataris, chained to the wall and ceiling, his belly spikes gone and replaced by purple blemishes and gaping holes, and killed him (Metroid Dread). How he came to ZDR is not recorded. An X parasite later possessed his corpse, and that X went on to infect Raven Beak.

Kraid is an old enemy who keeps coming back; Samus has beaten him three times.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid_(area) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mini_Kraid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Dread</sub>


### Zebes: Mother Brain

*Keys:* Mother Brain, Mother-Brain, MotherBrain, Mother Brains


Mother Brain is the artificial intelligence that ruled Zebes, first for the Chozo and then for the Space Pirates. Physically she is a vast brain with a single eye, kept in a tank in the deepest chamber of Tourian behind Zebetite barriers; she is gender-neutral but presents a female persona. Per the manga, the Chozo created her to oversee Zebes and to bring the species of the galaxy into harmony ("And so, I was born"). She delivered regular reports to the Chozo on the Metroids' progress against the X on SR388, made the Power Suit to support the Chozo's martial strength and on that basis told Samus "I gave birth to you!", and predicted that the frail human child could not survive life on Zebes (all per the manga).

When the Space Pirates came to Zebes, she concluded that her purpose had backfired and resolved to bring order to the universe by whatever means were necessary, assuming control of the Pirates, whom she judged genetically predisposed to obey a powerful leader. Per the manga, the Chozo elder Gray Voice, whose DNA had given Samus her Chozo genes, sided with her and the Pirates as a ruse; he donned a Chozo Power Suit and attacked her, but Ridley struck him a fatal blow before he could bring her offline, and his last attack, launched skyward, let the fleeing Chozo escape.

In the Pirate fortress she directed Kraid, Ridley and every defense of the planet. The Galactic Federation described her as the mechanical life-form that controlled the fortress and its defenses, and sent Samus to destroy her. Shielded by turrets, Rinkas and five Zebetite barriers, she could close her eye to become momentarily invulnerable and fire a large purple blast from it. Samus broke through and destroyed her, triggering a time bomb that wrecked Tourian (Metroid: Zero Mission).

The Pirates revived her. On Samus's return she rose from her shattered tank on a towering mechanical body and struck Samus with a Hyper Beam that drained her energy and left her paralyzed. The baby Metroid absorbed Mother Brain's energy and gave it to Samus, then died shielding her; its death left Samus wielding the Hyper Beam, and with it she destroyed Mother Brain for good before Zebes itself exploded (Super Metroid). Years later Federation scientists built MB, an android running an AI derived from Mother Brain's thought processes, to regenerate and control cloned Space Pirate special forces and raise cloned Metroids aboard the Bottle Ship; MB developed Mother Brain's consciousness and turned violent.

Mother Brain knew Samus from childhood, and Samus has destroyed her twice.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/MB · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid</sub>


### Zebes: Regions of Zebes (Crateria, Brinstar, Norfair, Maridia)

*Keys:* Crateria, Brinstar, Norfair, Maridia, Kraid's Lair, Ridley's Lair, Kraid's Hideout, Ridley's Hideout, regions of Zebes, Zebes caverns


Zebes is layered, its regions linked by elevators and long vertical shafts; the deeper one goes, the hotter and stranger it becomes.

- Crateria is the outermost surface, barraged by meteors and acid rain and struck by lightning; sparse caverns offer the only refuge and lead down toward the core. Samus landed her gunship here on both of her Zebes missions. Chozo relics survive among its rocks (on her return a Torizo guarded them), and Chozodia borders it. The Wrecked Ship, the hulk of a vessel that crashed long ago, splits Crateria in two; when Samus returned its power was dead and Phantoon haunted it. The ruins of the old Tourian lie in Crateria's lowest reaches.
- Brinstar lies just beneath the surface: a tropical, humid cavern-jungle holding over 60 percent of the planet's lifeforms, many of them carnivorous plants. Its lower section has red soil and borders Norfair. Kraid's lair occupies Brinstar's deepest points beyond a red door, a tall grey chamber opening onto long purple corridors pooled with acid. Etecoons and a Dachora live in Brinstar.
- Norfair lies deep beneath Brinstar: volcanic caverns of magma lakes, superheated chambers and boiling acid streams, its walls covered in purple bubble-like formations. Its heat demands the Varia Suit. Deeper still stand ancient Chozo constructs, abandoned until the Pirates built among them; Ridley's lair lies in these lower sections. When the Pirates rebuilt, they gave it a gate shaped like Ridley's head and flooded it with acidic lava that burns even through the Gravity Suit, and after Samus's first mission Norfair filled far more heavily with lava.
- Maridia is a large, mostly submerged region of sand walls and quicksand floors, home to fish and crustaceans. Partly artificial, it served as Draygon's base. The first way in open to Samus was a tunnel in the middle of Brinstar that she broke open with a Power Bomb, and its flooded shafts cannot be climbed without the Gravity Suit.
- Tourian is the Pirates' metal headquarters and Mother Brain's seat. The first was reached by a tall shaft from Brinstar; the rebuilt one lies deeper, entered by an elevator in western Crateria.

Samus grew up on Zebes; on her first mission she worked down from Brinstar through Kraid's lair and Norfair before Tourian opened to her; on her return she crossed every region, including Maridia and the Wrecked Ship, which she had never entered before.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Crateria · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Brinstar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norfair · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Maridia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kraid_(area)</sub>


### Zebes: Samus's first Zebes mission (Zero Mission)

*Keys:* Zero Mission, Zero-Mission, first Zebes mission, first mission to Zebes, first mission on Zebes, first trip to Zebes, fall of Zebes, Zebes has fallen, 20X5


In the year 20X5 of the Cosmic Calendar, Space Pirates attacked a Galactic Federation deep-space research vessel returning from SR388, seized the Metroid it carried and took it to their fortress on Zebes. The Federation feared the Pirates would multiply the creature with beta rays and loose Metroids as biological weapons. It located the base and attacked, but the Pirates drove the Federation Police back. As a last resort the Federation sent a lone space hunter, Samus Aran, to exterminate the Metroids and destroy Mother Brain, the mechanical life-form that controlled the fortress and its defenses.

Samus landed on Crateria and descended into Brinstar, the caverns of the world where the Chozo had raised her. Chozo Statues among the ruins yielded the relics she needed, among them the Morph Ball, Missiles, Bombs, the Power Grip and the Ice Beam, and in Crateria, Kraid's lair and Ridley's lair she found three devices her suit could not identify, the Unknown Items. She defeated Kraid; while she fought through Norfair the Space Pirate Mother Ship landed in Chozodia; in lower Norfair she killed Ridley. With both lieutenants dead the barrier to Tourian lifted. Inside, the Pirates' own Metroids had apparently killed their keepers. She froze them with the Ice Beam, shattered them with Missiles, broke the Zebetites, destroyed Mother Brain and outran the time bomb up the escape shaft to her ship.

Pirate fighters ambushed the gunship as it left Zebes and brought it down beside the Mother Ship. "The timing of my escape couldn't have been worse," Samus later recalled. Without her Power Suit, wearing only the Zero Suit and armed with the Paralyzer, an auto-charging pistol that could stun but not kill, she slipped through the Mother Ship into Chozodia, where a Chozo trial, the Ruins Test, granted her the Legendary Power Suit. It restored everything she had lost and revealed the Unknown Items as the Plasma Beam, Space Jump and Gravity Suit. She fought back through the Mother Ship, destroyed Mecha Ridley at its summit, and escaped in a small Pirate craft as the self-destruct tore the vessel apart (Metroid: Zero Mission).

The Pirates' own logs read "Zebes has fallen": all ground personnel were presumed dead, killed by the Hunter or in the destruction of the base, but the research frigates Orpheon, Siriacus and Vol Paragom retreated from orbit with live Metroids, and Pirate Security Command issued an all-points alert on the Hunter. Per the manga, Adam Malkovich supported Samus during this mission. The Pirates later rebuilt on Zebes, and Samus returned for the baby Metroid (Super Metroid).

Samus walked the caverns of her childhood as the enemy of the planet's new masters, and left Zebes in armor made by the Chozo.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid:_Zero_Mission · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Orpheon</sub>


### Zebes: The destruction of Zebes

*Keys:* destruction of Zebes, Zebes's destruction, Zebes destroyed, Zebes was destroyed, destroyed Zebes, Zebes exploded, Zebes explosion, Zebes blew up, blew up Zebes, Zebes is gone, end of Zebes, Zebes no longer exists


Zebes was destroyed at the end of Samus's second mission there. After her first mission the Space Pirates resurfaced on the planet and completely rebuilt Tourian, deeper than before, and Ridley carried the baby Metroid he had stolen from Ceres Space Colony down into it. Samus followed, defeated Kraid, Phantoon, Draygon and Ridley, and in the new Tourian destroyed the revived Mother Brain with the Hyper Beam after the baby gave its life for her (Super Metroid).

Mother Brain's death started a self-destruct countdown. Samus broke through a wall into the old escape shaft and raced through Tourian and Crateria to her gunship, freeing on her way the three Etecoons and the Dachora she had met in Brinstar. As her ship climbed away the entire planet exploded, and nothing of Zebes was left.

The Chozo were long gone by then. Some of the planet's creatures outlived it as specimens aboard research stations such as the Bottle Ship and the BSL station, and Kraid was later found alive, chained in Cataris on ZDR.

The world Samus grew up on died with Mother Brain, in the same battle in which she lost the baby Metroid.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tourian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/ZDR</sub>


### Zebes: Tourian

*Keys:* Tourian, Old Tourian, New Tourian, Zebetite, Zebetites, Rinka, Rinkas, Golden Statue, escape shaft


Tourian is the Space Pirate headquarters on Zebes and the seat of Mother Brain. Unlike the planet's natural caverns it is built, a complex of metal and synthetic materials housing the Pirates' Metroid laboratories, and it held most of the Metroids on the planet. Its long metal corridors have doors that must be forced with Missiles. Launchers spit a constant stream of Rinkas, ring-shaped fireballs that burn on contact; turrets cover the approaches; and before Mother Brain's chamber stand five Zebetite barriers, tubes of shielded, conduit-like material connected to Mother Brain. Zebetite shatters under Missiles but regrows; per the manga it is produced by the same molecule transference that generates Zebes's planetary shield. Mother Brain's tank stands above a pit of lava crossed by three platforms.

The first Tourian was reached by a tall shaft in Brinstar, its way barred until Kraid and Ridley were both dead. When Samus entered on her first mission she found the corridors littered with dead Space Pirates; the Metroids had apparently turned on their keepers. She froze each Metroid with the Ice Beam and shattered it with Missiles, broke the Zebetites and destroyed Mother Brain. That armed a time bomb, and Samus fled up a long escape shaft to the surface before the base blew (Metroid: Zero Mission).

The Pirates rebuilt Tourian deeper, entered by an elevator in western Crateria sealed by the Golden Statue: four statues of Kraid, Phantoon, Draygon and Ridley, each losing its eye and turning grey as its original fell, until the whole statue sank to open the way. The ruins of the old Tourian survived in Crateria's lowest depths, still occupied by Pirates, with Mother Brain's destroyed holding tank in plain view. The new Tourian kept the metal corridors, Metroids and Rinkas, but beyond them lay a zone of lifeless, decaying creatures, and there the grown baby Metroid seized Samus, nearly killed her, then spared her. Mother Brain's revival, the baby's death and the second detonation followed (Super Metroid); when the countdown began, Samus used the Hyper Beam to break through a wall into the original escape shaft and climb to her ship.

Samus has walked out of Tourian alive twice, each time with the base exploding behind her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tourian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebetite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rinka · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Golden_Statue · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Super_Metroid</sub>


### Zebes: Zebes (planet overview)

*Keys:* Zebes, Planet Zebes, Zebeth, FS-176


Zebes is a planet of the FS-176 system, which it shares with Tallon IV, Oormine II, Twin Tabula and Bilium; two moons orbit it. Scan records classify it as a Class XIX world, inhospitable to most bioforms, with a mass near 4.8 trillion teratons and a crust rich in Urithic Ore, a mineral well suited to underground construction. Its surface, Crateria, is a mostly lifeless expanse beaten by acid rain, meteor falls and lightning; nearly everything that lives on Zebes lives below it, in the humid cavern-jungles of Brinstar, the magma depths of Norfair, the flooded caverns of Maridia and the metal fortress of Tourian. Chozodia, the ruined city of the Chozo, lies beside Crateria near the surface.

Zebes was a Chozo world, and the Chozo raised Samus Aran there. Per the manga, the Chozo created the artificial intelligence Mother Brain to oversee the planet, and after Space Pirates destroyed the human colony K-2L, the Chozo elders Old Bird and Gray Voice took the orphaned Samus, then three years old, to Zebes. Also per the manga, Mother Brain predicted that her frail body could not withstand life there, so the Chozo integrated their DNA with hers and equipped her with the Power Suit; she trained on Zebes through her teenage years until Old Bird told her she was ready to leave and head out on her own.

The Space Pirates later took Zebes and Mother Brain assumed command of them; per the manga, the Chozo fled and Gray Voice was fatally wounded by Ridley while trying to bring her offline. The Pirates made the planet a fortress: Tourian as headquarters and Metroid laboratory, Kraid in the depths of Brinstar, Ridley in lower Norfair. After they brought a captured Metroid there to multiply as a weapon and repelled the Federation Police, the Galactic Federation sent Samus to destroy Mother Brain (Metroid: Zero Mission). She did, and destroyed the Space Pirate Mother Ship in Chozodia as well. Pirate logs record that Zebes had fallen, but the research frigates Orpheon, Siriacus and Vol Paragom retreated from orbit with live Metroids; Samus later answered the Orpheon's distress signal above Tallon IV.

The Pirates returned, rebuilt Tourian deeper, and Ridley carried the baby Metroid stolen from Ceres Space Colony there. When Samus destroyed the revived Mother Brain, the entire planet exploded and nothing was left (Super Metroid). Lifeforms taken from Zebes survived aboard research stations such as the Bottle Ship and the BSL station, and Kraid was later found alive in Cataris on ZDR.

Zebes was the only home Samus had after K-2L. She knows its caverns from childhood, fought two campaigns through them, and saw the planet die.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Zebes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Old_Bird · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gray_Voice · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mother_Brain · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data</sub>


## Prime


### Prime: Chozo Artifacts and the Artifact Temple

*Keys:* Chozo Artifact, Chozo Artifacts, Artifact Temple, the Cipher, Chozo Totem, Chozo Totems, twelve Artifacts, Artifact of Truth, Artifact of Newborn, Artifact of Chozo, Artifact of Warrior, Artifact of Lifegiver


The Chozo Artifacts are twelve relics into which the Chozo of Tallon IV poured their will, together forming the Cipher, a mystical lock that seals the Great Poison within the Impact Crater. They hid them across the planet so the source of the poison would not fall into the wrong hands, and carved clues to each resting place in the Chozo tongue on twelve pillars, the Chozo Totems, in the Artifact Temple.

The Artifact Temple, which the Chozo called the Cradle, is the holiest Chozo site on Tallon IV: a courtyard of Chozo Statues ringed by the totems, set over the Impact Crater in the Tallon Overworld. Its seal slowed the spread of Phazon but could not stop it.

The twelve, and where Samus found them:
- Truth: the Artifact Temple itself.
- Chozo: the Life Grove, beneath the water.
- Wild: Flaahgra's nest in the Sunchamber of the Chozo Ruins.
- World: a chamber beneath the statue in the Hall of the Elders.
- Lifegiver: the Tower Chamber of the Chozo Ruins.
- Strength: the Warrior Shrine of Magmoor Caverns.
- Nature: the shattered column at the center of the Lava Lake.
- Sun: the Chozo Ice Temple, under thawed ice.
- Elder: the Control Tower of the Pirate base in Phendrana Drifts, revealed when the tower was brought down.
- Spirit: a hidden entrance at Phendrana's Edge.
- Warrior: Elite Research in the Phazon Mines, guarded by a Phazon-corrupted creature.
- Newborn: the Phazon Mining Tunnel, through a vein of raw Phazon.

The Space Pirates' Phazon Program ran into the seal at once: a containment field over the crater that no tunneling could breach. Their records show they linked it to the Artifacts, assumed from the number of statues that there must be twelve, could not decipher the totems, resolved to dismantle the temple, and lost personnel to Chozo Ghosts whenever they disturbed Chozo sites. They never broke through.

Samus gathered all twelve. When she set them in place, Meta Ridley swept down and shattered the totems, and beams from the temple's statues helped her strike him into the crater. Twelve Chozo Ghosts then rose where the totems had stood and activated the Cipher, opening a shaft of light down to the Crater Entry Point. After Metroid Prime died, the temple crumbled in the explosions that collapsed the crater, and Samus watched it fall.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Artifact · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artifact_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ghost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Overworld</sub>


### Prime: Flaahgra, Thardus and the Omega Pirate

*Keys:* Flaahgra, Thardus, Omega Pirate, Omega Pirates, Project Titan, Project Helix, Sunchamber, Quarantine Cave, Elite Pirate Upsilon, Mirror Arrays, Sacred Fountain, Elite Quarters


Flaahgra, Thardus and the Omega Pirate were three Phazon-made monsters Samus destroyed during the Tallon IV incident, each guarding ground where she recovered part of her arsenal.

Flaahgra was a giant flower of an unknown species, grotesquely mutated by Phazon and rooted in the Sunchamber, the Sacred Fountain of the Chozo Ruins. Its accelerated growth demanded near-constant sunlight, fed to it by four Mirror Arrays, and its toxins poisoned the water throughout the Ruins; a Chozo inscription records a beast defiling the sacred fountain. Its insectoid core swung scythe-like claws and sprayed toxic fluid, and its sun-hardened shell turned weapons. Samus knocked the mirrors out of alignment until it wilted, then bombed its exposed roots in Morph Ball form until it burned. The fountain ran clean afterward, and the Varia Suit lay beyond.

Thardus was the one major success of Project Titan, the Space Pirates' attempt to animate inanimate matter with Phazon radiation: a colossal, sentient creature of Phazon ore. The Pirates tried to tame it for patrol duty in the Phazon Mines, but its violence caused mass casualties; the project was suspended indefinitely and Thardus confined in the Quarantine Cave of Phendrana Drifts pending relocation. It hurled boulders telekinetically, rolled, and encased foes in ice, and its radiation scrambled targeting systems. Pirate research had found that thermal imaging exposed its weak points; Samus found them with the Thermal Visor, shattered it and claimed the Spider Ball.

The Omega Pirate, designated Elite Pirate Upsilon, was the pinnacle of Project Helix. Early Helix experiments on Pirate embryos were disastrous, the few survivors suffering psychotic breakdowns as juveniles, until a Phazon strain code-named Vertigo produced the Elite Pirates. A constant Phazon diet swelled the Omega Pirate's mass exponentially yet left its mind intact; it wielded a Plasma Incendiary Launcher, Wave Quake Generators and the Chameleon Manta cloak, and regenerated by exposing itself to Phazon. Its creators' stated concern was its overdependence on Phazon, and no such subject outlived two deca-cycles in their simulations. Samus woke it from its Cryosleep Tank in the Elite Quarters of the Phazon Mines, broke its Phazon plating, tracked it with the X-Ray Visor while it cloaked to heal, and killed it. Its body collapsed onto her and disintegrated, and its Phazon corrupted her armor into the Phazon Suit, the means by which she later wounded Metroid Prime.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Flaahgra · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Thardus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phendrana_Drifts</sub>


### Prime: Frigate Orpheon and the Parasite Queen

*Keys:* Orpheon, Frigate Orpheon, Parasite Queen, Parasite Queens, Siriacus, Vol Paragom, Vol Paragon, Frigate Crash Site, crashed frigate, derelict frigate, Vortex Outpost


The Orpheon was one of three Space Pirate research frigates, with the Siriacus and the Vol Paragom, in orbit when Samus destroyed the Pirate facilities on Zebes. It docked at Vortex Outpost with its Metroid cargo intact, then took station above Tallon IV. Aboard it the Pirates revived Ridley's mortally damaged body and rebuilt him with cybernetics as Meta Ridley, and their scientists used native life for Phazon mutagen experiments: Parasites, Plated Parasites, Shriekbats, Ice Shriekbats, Plazmites, War Wasps and Zoomers.

The Parasites answered best. Phazon infusion enlarged and strengthened them and grew poison sacs and a second ring of mandibles; most survivors of the third infusion were so violent they had to be euthanized, and the fourth killed every subject. From this program came the Parasite Queen: massive jaws ringed with sacs of corrosive bile, galvanized legs that pierce alloy, a tail orifice for birthing young, and weapon-grade energy fired from its mouth. Two Queens broke containment and killed much of the crew. The survivors destroyed one, lit the distress beacon and abandoned ship, leaving the badly wounded behind; Samus's scans still found faint life signs when she arrived.

She tracked the beacon to the derelict, read the terminals that first told her of Phazon and Tallon IV, and in the Reactor Core fought the Queen still loose aboard, shielded by a protective energy barrier. When it died its corpse fell onto the reactor, which went critical. During her escape a hibernating Queen in Biotech Research Area 1 broke free and died in an explosion, she came face to face with Meta Ridley, who fled to the planet, and a blast in an elevator shaft stripped her Power Suit of most of its functions before she reached her Gunship (Metroid Prime).

The Orpheon fell to Tallon IV and came to rest half-drowned at the Frigate Crash Site in the Tallon Overworld. Once Samus had the Gravity Suit from Phendrana Drifts she went back in: Aqua Reapers, Tallon Crabs and Aqua Sacs had colonized the hull, Aqua Pirates were combing it, apparently for survivors, and she restored its Power Conduits with the Wave Beam, tracing them through the Thermal Visor. The wreck was her route into the Phazon Mines. Two more Parasite Queen corpses turned up years later in the Pirate laboratories of Eastern SkyTown on Elysia, one noted as resembling the parasitic life of Tallon IV.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Frigate_Orpheon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Parasite_Queen · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime</sub>


### Prime: Impact Crater

*Keys:* Impact Crater, Tallon IV's Scar, Phazon Core, Orange Phazon, Phazon Infusion Chamber, Crater Entry Point, Crater Tunnel, Metroid Prime Lair, Subchamber, Subchambers


The Impact Crater is the wound left when the Leviathan from Phaaze struck Tallon IV about fifty years before Samus came; the Galactic Federation later named it Tallon IV's Scar. Its depths are the only known source of Orange Phazon, which Samus's scans rated eighty-six percent more mutagenic than ordinary Phazon and which harmed her even through the Phazon Suit. Lumigeks and Fission Metroids live there and nowhere else on the planet. Metroid Prime gestated at its heart, absorbing Phazon from the core.

The Chozo built the Artifact Temple, the Cradle, directly above the crater and sealed it with the Cipher of twelve Chozo Artifacts, which slowed the Great Poison's spread. The Space Pirates, drawn to Tallon IV by an energy spike, could not get in: every tunneling attempt met an impermeable shield of strange energy, and their records speak only of a massive creature gestating within. Mining near the crater raised their daily Phazon yields by forty-four percent, and their miners went mad.

Samus entered after twelve Chozo Ghosts activated the Cipher, descending in a beam of light to the Crater Entry Point. Crater Tunnel A led to the Phazon Core, a cavern floored with Orange Phazon from which Fission Metroids spawned two at a time, with the Crater Missile Station branching off it. Crater Tunnel B led on to the Phazon Infusion Chamber, where Metroid Prime hung from the ceiling by orange cables and fought her in its armored exoskeleton before burrowing down through Subchambers One to Five. At the bottom lay the Metroid Prime Lair, where the broken exoskeleton came to rest in a mass of Phazon and she destroyed the creature's core essence, channeling its own Phazon through her suit as the Phazon Beam. The crater exploded and collapsed when the creature died, taking the Artifact Temple with it (Metroid Prime).

It was the deepest immersion in Phazon Samus experienced before the Phazon crisis, and the place where Dark Samus was born from the Phazon Suit that Metroid Prime tore from her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Impact_Crater · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV's_Scar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Core · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Infusion_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Lair · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data</sub>


### Prime: Metroid Prime (the creature)

*Keys:* Metroid Prime, Test Subject Z-d, core essence, Prime's core, Prime's exoskeleton, prophesied Worm, Worm of Tallon IV


Metroid Prime was the creature the Chozo of Tallon IV prophesied as the Worm, "born from parasites, nurtured in a poisoned womb," that would grow, devouring from within, until the world began to rot. It came to the planet in the meteor, the Leviathan from Phaaze, about fifty years before Samus: a Metroid of unknown origin that somehow absorbed the Leviathan's core and became it, gestating in the Impact Crater. The Chozo raised the Artifact Temple, the Cradle, over its prison and locked it with the Cipher.

The Space Pirates never reached it. Their records show every tunneling attempt stopped by an impermeable shield of strange energy, while life-form readings revealed a massive creature gestating inside and absorbing Phazon from the crater's core. An earlier, superseded version of their records instead described a captured Test Subject Z-d, genetically similar to a Metroid, with a limitless capacity for Phazon and no degeneration, that escaped stasis. Samus's own scans called it a highly evolved, Phazon-producing life-form whose genetic flaw left it open to certain weapons for brief periods; only its head was truly vulnerable.

In its armored exoskeleton it first confronted Samus in the Phazon Infusion Chamber, hanging from the ceiling, then burrowed down through the five Subchambers toward the Metroid Prime Lair with her in pursuit. At any moment the color of its armor showed which single beam could hurt it: purple for the Wave Beam, yellow for the Power Beam, white for the Ice Beam, red for the Plasma Beam. It attacked with Ultrafrigid Breath, Multi-Missiles, Particle Wave Projectors and a Snare Beam, a green light that dragged her toward it. When the exoskeleton broke apart, the creature fell down a long shaft and Samus followed.

Its core essence, a dark form that could turn invisible, released shock waves and spawned Metroids, and was invulnerable to every weapon but a Phazon-fused Arm Cannon. It left pools of Phazon as it attacked; standing in them, Samus channeled the Phazon through her Phazon Suit as the Phazon Beam and wounded it. Dying, it seized her, tore the Phazon Suit from her body and exploded, and the Impact Crater collapsed.

It survived by forming itself into the stolen suit; a hand rose from the Phazon pool. This was the birth of Dark Samus, the name under which the being was known from then on: it had all of Samus's abilities with greater power, spread Phazon into Federation space and next crossed her path on Aether (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). Husks of a nearly identical creature were later found in the Genesis Chamber on Phaaze, their origin unknown. For Samus, Metroid Prime was the first enemy she could kill only with Phazon itself, and the seed of a conflict that followed her through the Phazon crisis.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Infusion_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Lair · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore</sub>


### Prime: Regions of Tallon IV

*Keys:* Tallon Overworld, Chozo Ruins, Magmoor Caverns, Magmoor, Phendrana Drifts, Phendrana, Phazon Mines, Glacier One, Life Grove, Chozo Ice Temple, Hall of the Elders, regions of Tallon IV


The explored surface of Tallon IV divides into five linked regions, joined by Chozo and Pirate transport elevators, plus the sealed Impact Crater beneath the Artifact Temple. The twelve Chozo Artifacts were hidden across them.

Tallon Overworld: a rainforest under constant rain, where Samus's Gunship set down at the Landing Site. It holds the Artifact Temple above the Impact Crater, the Life Grove (an ancient Chozo religious site), the Great Tree Hall and the Frigate Crash Site, where the Orpheon lies half-drowned.

Chozo Ruins: the first Chozo colony, built of stone, woven branches and channeled water around ancient trees; its stone defies carbon dating. The Great Poison fouled its water, and Flaahgra nested in the Sunchamber. Chozo Ghosts haunt it, above all the Hall of the Elders, and twelve of the planet's sixteen Chozo Lore inscriptions are carved here. The Pirates meant to terraform the Ruins and harvest Chozo relics, but Ghost attacks forced them to restrict access.

Magmoor Caverns: a volcanic underground crossway linking every other region except the crater, all lava lakes and flame jets with no plant life. The Pirates ran geothermal works at the Monitor Station. The Warrior Shrine stands here.

Phendrana Drifts: frozen mountains stalked by Sheegoths, divided into the Shorelines, Glacier One and the Underground Lake area, where Samus recovered the Gravity Suit. The Chozo Ice Temple, with its busts of a Chozo Philosopher, Warrior, Architect and Shaman, and the Chapel of the Elders make it a center of Chozo worship. Glacier One was the Pirates' research headquarters: Research Lab Hydra, Research Lab Aether, the Research Core, the Observatory, the Control Tower and the Quarantine Cave that held Thardus. The cold kept their Tallon Metroids sluggish.

Phazon Mines: the Pirates' mining complex, entered through the Orpheon's wreck or from Magmoor. Its tiers descend from the Main Quarry and Ore Processing through Elite Research and the Central Dynamo to the Elite Quarters, the Metroid Quarantines and the Phazon Processing Center. Project Helix bred the Elite Pirates and the Omega Pirate here, and Troopers carried beam weapons reverse-engineered from Samus's own arsenal.

Samus crossed all of them during the Tallon IV incident, reclaiming the armor and weapons the Chozo had left for her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_Overworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Magmoor_Caverns · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phendrana_Drifts · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Mines · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Glacier_One</sub>


### Prime: Tallon IV

*Keys:* Tallon IV, Tallon 4, Tallon Four, planet Tallon, Wanderer-class


Tallon IV is a Wanderer-class planet in the FS-176 system of the Spiral Sector, a neighbor of Zebes, Oormine II, Twin Tabula and Bilium, with a mass of 5.1 trillion teratons according to Samus's scans. Most of its explored surface is rain-soaked forest.

Chozo settled it long ago and chose to leave advanced technology behind, building in stone, branch and water in harmony with the world. Their colony became the Chozo Ruins, and they raised shrines as far as the ice of Phendrana Drifts. About fifty years before Samus arrived, a meteor, later identified as a Leviathan from Phaaze, struck the planet and released what the Chozo called the Great Poison and the Pirates named Phazon. It fouled the water, mutated the wildlife and corrupted many Chozo into the spectral Chozo Ghosts. The rest built the Artifact Temple over the Impact Crater, locked it with the Cipher of twelve Chozo Artifacts and left their weapons and armor for a foretold Entrusted One; Federation data records that the survivors abandoned the planet for an unknown location. Samus's own scans projected that at the current rate of decay Tallon IV would become a barren Class XIII wasteland in about twenty-five years.

After the fall of Zebes, Pirate sensors detected an energy spike on Tallon IV. Scouts landed at its center, a land mass at heading mark 40.08.02, and reported the planet a viable secondary headquarters. The Pirates sank the Phazon Mines, built the research base Glacier One in Phendrana Drifts, tapped the magma of Magmoor Caverns and began terraforming the Chozo Ruins, planning a combat base and a starport and expecting to dominate the sector within a deca-cycle. Daily Phazon yields rose forty-four percent, at the cost of Phazon-induced madness among the miners: loss of equilibrium, erratic breathing and muscle spasms.

Samus came following the Orpheon's distress beacon and ended the Tallon IV incident by breaking the Pirate operation and destroying Metroid Prime in the Impact Crater. By the time of the Phazon crisis her scans found the planet recovered from the impact, and the Galactic Federation had renamed the crater Tallon IV's Scar (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). The Chozo of Tallon IV foresaw her as the Entrusted One of their Chozo Lore, and it was on this world that she first wore Phazon in her armor and first met the thing that would become Dark Samus.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV's_Scar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime</sub>


### Prime: The Chozo of Tallon IV and the Chozo Lore prophecy

*Keys:* Chozo Lore, Entrusted One, Great Poison, Worm prophecy, Chozo prophecy, the Turned, Prophecy of Light, Shining One, Hatchling Samus, Tallon Chozo, Chozo of Tallon IV, Chozo settlers


The Chozo who settled Tallon IV knew much of technology but did not worship it; by choice they left it behind and built their sanctuary of stone, branches and water around the ancient trees. As their harmonization with the universe deepened, many gained the distant sight, reading prophecy in rippling water and the wind. The worst and most common vision was of the Worm: "born from parasites, nurtured in a poisoned womb," it would grow, devouring from within, until the world began to rot. Not all prophecies come to pass, the inscriptions add, but the Chozo feared this one.

Then a meteor no sage foresaw fell. From its crater spread the Great Poison, an otherworldly pestilence that corrupted trees and water and bred mutated beasts, one of which defiled the sacred fountain. The Chozo wondered whether the meteor had been crafted by alien hands or was a roving chunk of a planet that suffered a violent end, and hoped the poison's only surviving remnants were on Tallon IV. Unable to destroy it, they contained it: a temple, the Cradle, rose over the crater, and at its heart the Cipher, "a mystical lock powered by twelve Artifacts," which they hid across the land. The seal slowed the poison but did not stop it.

Not all endured. The Turned, Chozo whose minds the poison blackened, keep ghostly Chozo forms and will likely exist in limbo forever; they are the Chozo Ghosts that haunt the Chozo Ruins. The rest departed, "withdrawing beyond the illusion" and promising to remain ever watchful, their will waiting unseen until the Entrusted One arrives.

Their last inscriptions are a prophecy. A savior would come; they would leave their weapons and armor for that soul, and whoever gathered them would become the Entrusted One, the only being able to reverse the evil. The "Prophecy of Light" sees a light chasing the poison's shadows, sometimes in the shape of a woman; the "Shining One" is a human, a lone figure shining in the toxic shadows. The older rendering of the inscriptions names her outright as "the Hatchling Samus" and speaks of a great Defender who delivers the world from evil.

Samus, raised by the Chozo of Zebes and wearing their armor, is the Entrusted One they foresaw. She read these inscriptions one by one during the Tallon IV incident, and her Logbook preserves them.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ruins · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ghost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV</sub>


### Prime: The Tallon IV incident

*Keys:* Tallon IV incident, Tallon IV mission, mission to Tallon IV, mission on Tallon IV, Metroid Prime incident, Metroid Prime mission, events of Metroid Prime, Orpheon distress signal, Orpheon distress beacon


The Tallon IV incident was Samus Aran's first encounter with Phazon and with the creature Metroid Prime (Metroid Prime; set after her first mission on Zebes and before her campaign on SR388). It began when she tracked a distress beacon to the Orpheon, a Space Pirate research frigate that had escaped Zebes and taken station above Tallon IV in the FS-176 system.

Aboard, she found a crew slaughtered by its own experiments: Parasites from Tallon IV mutated with Phazon into Parasite Queens. She killed the Queen in the Reactor Core; its corpse fell onto the reactor, which went critical, and the ship began to self-destruct. On her way out she met Ridley, whom the Pirates had revived aboard the frigate and rebuilt with cybernetics as Meta Ridley. An explosion in an elevator shaft stripped her Power Suit to basic functions, and she pursued Ridley to the surface in her Gunship as the frigate fell from orbit.

On the planet she pieced its history together from Chozo Lore and Pirate Data. Chozo settlers had lived there in deliberate harmony with nature until a meteor, a Leviathan, struck about fifty years earlier and released the Great Poison, which the Pirates named Phazon. The Chozo built the Artifact Temple over the Impact Crater, sealed it with the Cipher, a lock of twelve Chozo Artifacts, and foretold a savior, the Entrusted One, who would gather their armor and weapons and cleanse the land. After the fall of Zebes the Space Pirates had judged Tallon IV a viable secondary headquarters, and they were mining Phazon and experimenting with it on the native life, on their own troops and on the Metroids carried in the Orpheon's hold.

Samus crossed the planet's regions recovering her abilities and the Artifacts. In the Chozo Ruins she killed Flaahgra, the mutant plant poisoning the water. In Phendrana Drifts she broke into the Pirate research base Glacier One and destroyed Thardus, the living Phazon ore of Project Titan. She explored the flooded wreck of the Orpheon, then the Phazon Mines, where the dying Omega Pirate collapsed onto her and its Phazon fused with her armor into the Phazon Suit.

When all twelve Artifacts were in place, Meta Ridley attacked and shattered the Chozo Totems. Samus beat him with help from the temple's statues, whose beams struck him down into the crater. Twelve Chozo Ghosts then took the totems' places and opened the way into the Impact Crater. There she destroyed Metroid Prime's armored exoskeleton and then its core essence, turning its own Phazon against it through the Phazon Suit. The dying creature seized her and tore the Phazon Suit away before it exploded. She escaped the collapsing crater, watched the Artifact Temple crumble and removed her helmet before departing. Unseen by her, the Phazon left behind merged with the stolen suit and a hand rose from it: the birth of Dark Samus.

The Pirate operation on Tallon IV was broken. By the time of the Phazon crisis, scans described the planet as recovered from the impact (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). For Samus the incident began a conflict with Phazon and Dark Samus that ended only with that crisis.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tallon_IV · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Frigate_Orpheon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Artifact_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature)</sub>


## Prime Hunters


### Prime Hunters: Alimbic Cluster locations

*Keys:* Celestial Archives, Alinos, Arcterra, Vesper Defense Outpost, VDO, Alimbic ruins, Echo Hall, Sic Transit, Cannon Control Room


The Alimbic Cluster, in the Tetra Galaxy beyond Federation space, holds four sites that Samus explored during the incident there (Metroid Prime Hunters). Each hides two Octoliths, so she had to visit each at least twice.

Celestial Archives: an Alimbic space station built as a vast data-storage facility to keep their knowledge safe, filled with automated defenses. It was Samus's first stop, and she recovered the Volt Driver and the Shock Coil there. Its Docking Bay holds the records of the Alimbic Order and the Octoliths, and its Celestial Gateway and Synergy Core carry verses of the Alimbic Prophecy. Kanden, the first rival she met, ambushed her here, and the first Stronghold Void she entered lay within the station.

Alinos: a rocky planet with a considerable volume of magma flowing beneath its crust, settled by the Alimbics, who harnessed its heat. Its ultra-high temperatures once powered an Alimbic city (per the manual), and thermal-energy machines still dot the surface; one of the Alimbics' last recorded messages, left in Echo Hall, calls it a place of death. The High Ground holds the sarcophagi of General Almiiak and the scientist Arctherus and the Interment Chamber of their warriors, and it is where Spire first confronted Samus; the Alimbic Gardens and Council Chamber hold accounts of Gorea; the Cannon Control Room houses the Alimbic Cannon. Weavel ambushed Samus at the top of Echo Hall, and the Fire Spawn lives in the magma; Samus took the Magmaul after defeating it.

Arcterra: a bitterly cold yet habitable planet settled by the Alimbics, where the Arctic Spawn and other cold-adapted creatures survive among Alimbic ruins. The Sanctorus, Fault Line and Subterranean chambers preserve the history of the Alimbic War and the Seal Sphere; the Ice Hive records their advances in telepathy and telekinesis. Samus found the Judicator here and the Imperialist in the Fault Line. Noxus and Trace were both first met in Sic Transit: Noxus confronted her directly while Trace sniped from a perch at the back of the same chamber.

Vesper Defense Outpost (VDO): a station built during the Alimbics' brief militant period after the invasion that ended their tribal era; it held no cultural significance for them. Kept at subzero temperatures and deserted since their extinction, its automated systems attack anyone who approaches. Its Weapons Complex, Fuel Stack, Stasis Bunker and Compression Chamber preserve the Alimbics' accounts of the Oubliette, the Infinity Void and the Octolith guardians. Samus recovered the Battlehammer in its Cortex CPU, Sylux first confronted her in the Weapons Complex, and two Stronghold Portals here lead to Octoliths.

The fifth site, the Oubliette, is Gorea's prison in the Infinity Void and can be reached only by the Alimbic Cannon.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Celestial_Archives · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alinos · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/High_Ground · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Arcterra · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vesper_Defense_Outpost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sic_Transit</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Gorea

*Keys:* Gorea, Alimbic War, Seal Sphere, Trocra


Gorea is the shapeshifting entity that destroyed the Alimbic civilization and the true author of the message promising "Ultimate Power" in the Alimbic Cluster. Its origin is unknown. It arrived without warning, mistaken for a comet, and crashed on an Alimbic world, most likely Alinos. It landed as a vapor, then copied the Alimbics' cellular structure to take solid form, gaining an intellect equal to theirs. Gorea can change its shape, cellular structure and atomic structure at will, so it has no fixed form. It devours the life force of its prey and grows stronger, and it replicates its victims' cellular structure and absorbs their technology to turn it back on them. During the three-month Alimbic War every weapon the Alimbics used against it was turned against its makers, and nothing they had could destroy it. As the death toll rose, the Alimbic Order of Elders chose containment: Gorea was lured into a trap and bound by the Seal Sphere, which bonded to it when it tried to absorb the device, then sealed inside the Oubliette and hurled into the Infinity Void by the Alimbic Cannon. There it lay immobilized for thousands of years.

As its containment declined, Gorea's psychic power reached across dimensions as a repeating telepathic lure, drawing the galaxy's most dangerous hunters to the Cluster in the hope that one would free it. When the six rival hunters forced their way into the Oubliette and broke its confinement, Gorea rose, drained their energy and absorbed their weapons. In its first form it stood as an armored giant whose arms wielded the hunters' own weapons, cycling from one to the next. Its shoulders were its weak points, each vulnerable to the weapon opposed to the one Gorea was then wielding: the Magmaul against its Judicator form, the Shock Coil against its Imperialist form, the Imperialist against its Shock Coil form. With both arms destroyed it hung inverted beneath the freed Seal Sphere, lashing with a tentacle that chased Samus and lifting Trocra from the chamber's edge with psionic tentacles to hurl as living bombs. When the Alimbic Prophecy was fulfilled, Gorea took a second, faster and tougher form in a lower chamber, immune to every weapon except the Alimbic Omega Cannon; six glowing points on its body went dark one by one under that weapon's fire until it was destroyed and the Oubliette exploded.

Samus is the only being ever to kill Gorea (Metroid Prime Hunters). She did so despite the Alimbics' final wish that no one seek Gorea without the means to destroy it, having read their records and gone on regardless.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seal_Sphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Oubliette · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Kanden

*Keys:* Kanden, Enoema, Volt Driver, Stinglarva, failed supersoldier


Kanden is an Enoema and the product of a failed attempt to engineer the ultimate soldier, immortal and relentless (per the manual); he gives his origin only as "a sinister laboratory." The stress of the neural combat encoding forced on his still-mortal brain was more than it could handle (per the official site), leaving a ferocious, deadly and dangerously unpredictable being, unbound by morals or conscience and driven by an instinct to hunt. He broke out of the laboratory and killed those responsible for his neurological alterations and surgical enhancements, then made himself a bounty hunter to prove he is the finest in the galaxy. The message from the Alimbic Cluster was his chance: he came for the Ultimate Power like the rest.

His weapon is the Volt Driver, an electrically charged weapon. In Kanden's hands its shots home in slightly on a target and briefly distort the Combat Visor on impact, though they travel more slowly than the same weapon fired by anyone else. His own electromagnetic field carries voltage comparable to lightning and can likewise disrupt a visor. His alternate form is the Stinglarva, a serpentine, segmented insect bioengineered from predatory insects; its tail detaches, regenerates, homes in on a target and explodes, and its sting is dangerous to anyone who comes close. The form handles sharp turns well and suits evasion. He is most vulnerable to Weavel's Battlehammer.

Kanden was the first rival Samus met during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), ambushing her in the Celestial Archives, where she beat him. He reached the Oubliette with the other hunters, was drained by Gorea and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance afterward.

To Samus he is one of six rivals she has fought and beaten; nothing in the record suggests any dealings between them before or after the Cluster.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kanden · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Volt_Driver · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Celestial_Archives · https://omegametroid.com/?p=14836</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Noxus

*Keys:* Noxus, Vhozon, Vhozons, Judicator, Vhoscythe, Vho


Noxus is a Vhozon from the cold planet Vho. The Vhozon are a proud, reclusive race of the galaxy's outer rim; little is known of their culture beyond what Noxus himself shows. They are purple with rounded features, their skin has extraordinary insulating properties developed in a frigid climate, and they can manipulate ice to some degree. The official account calls Noxus a spiritual being who walks a harsh, righteous path. He became a bounty hunter to bring justice to the galaxy's criminals and evildoers and to keep the balance of order from collapsing into instability and chaos. He pursued the Ultimate Power of the Alimbic Cluster so that it would not fall into the hands of lawbreakers or the unworthy, which in practice meant ensuring that no one else obtained it.

His weapon is the Judicator, which fires a beam of supercooled plasma that freezes what it strikes; in Noxus's hands it throws up a short-range wall of ice that freezes anything in its path. His alternate form is the Vhoscythe, an evolutionary defense mechanism of the Vhozon rather than a technological enhancement, serving both attack and defense. He is most vulnerable to the heat of Spire's Magmaul.

During the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters) Noxus confronted Samus in Sic Transit on Arcterra, with Trace sniping from the back of the same chamber, and lost to her. He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea, which then wielded the hunters' weapons against Samus, and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance afterward.

Of the six rivals, his stated aim came closest to Samus's own orders, which were to keep the power out of hostile hands or destroy it; he nonetheless treated her as one more competitor and fought her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Noxus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vhozon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Judicator · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sic_Transit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon · https://omegametroid.com/?p=14836</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Octoliths and the Alimbic Cannon

*Keys:* Octolith, Octoliths, Alimbic Cannon, Cretaphid, Cretaphids, Slench, Slenches, Stronghold Void, Stronghold Voids, Stronghold Portal, Alimbic Artifact, Alimbic Artifacts


The Octoliths are eight large crystals the Alimbics created as the keys to Gorea's prison. Each holds the transferred essence of one of the eight Alimbic Elders and carries unique coordinates, frequencies and codes needed to operate the Alimbic Cannon; without all eight, neither the Infinity Void nor the Oubliette can be reached. The Alimbic Order scattered them across four heavily defended territories, the Celestial Archives, Alinos, the Vesper Defense Outpost and Arcterra, with no more than two on any one planet or station. Each was hidden in a Stronghold Void, a concealed chamber holding the Alimbics' most sacred relics, accessible only by extraordinary means: a Stronghold Portal controlled by three Alimbic Artifacts and guarded by the Alimbics' most formidable cybernetic security systems. "Beware our guardians," their records warn.

Those guardians are the Cretaphid and the Slench, each built in four increasingly advanced versions. A Cretaphid is a tall defense column split into three rotating sections studded with spots that fire homing plasma or lasers; a spot can be destroyed only while it glows blue, and once all are gone a central crystal rises from the top as the only vulnerable point. Later versions mix lasers and plasma, and the fourth roams its chamber. A Slench is a giant mechanical eye anchored to a wall or ceiling by three tentacle-like synapses that control its energy blasters; a forcefield shields its body until the synapses are damaged, after which it detaches, floats, fires and rams, moving faster the more it is hurt. Later Slenches fire superheated magma, high-voltage bolts whose electromagnetic pulse disrupts the Combat Visor, or supercooled plasma.

The Alimbic Cannon stands in the Cannon Control Room on Alinos. It fires polarized antimatter faster than light to tear a dimensional rift, the Infinity Void, between this dimension and a parallel one of formless antimatter, emitting blue rings as it works. The Alimbics used it to hurl the Oubliette, with Gorea sealed inside, into that void, then sealed the rift. Gathering all eight Octoliths in the control room reactivates it.

During the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters) Samus recovered all eight despite the Alimbics' warnings; each one brought her a telepathic message from the Alimbic spirits. The rival hunters sought them too, and whenever one of them defeated her he took an Octolith that she had to win back by beating him again. With the cannon restored she opened the way to the Oubliette and to Gorea.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Octolith · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Cannon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Infinity_Void · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Cretaphid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Slench · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Spire

*Keys:* Spire, Diamont, Diamonts, Magmaul, Dialanche, Mondreus  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Diamont, Diamonts, bounty hunter, Magmaul, Dialanche, Mondreus, Alimbic, Alimbics, Alinos


Spire is a Diamont, a member of an ancient silicon-based race from the planet Mondreus whose bodies are composed of organic rock containing molten ferrous compounds normally found only in a planet's core. He is believed to be the sole surviving Diamont, and he took up bounty hunting to solve the mystery of his people's disappearance, searching the galaxy for any clue to where they went or what became of them. The message from the Alimbic Cluster drew him because he hoped the Alimbics' source of ultimate power would also explain his own race's fate.

He withstands extreme heat and is the only one of the hunters who can walk in lava. His weapon is the Magmaul, made by the Diamonts and handed down through generations to him; it lobs cohesive bursts of superheated magma, and in Spire's hands its blasts are far more devastating, though the splash can burn him as well. He can transform at will, without any technology, into the Dialanche, a hypercondensed ferrous orb bristling with spikes that rolls at high speed over terrain and climbs flat surfaces, and which extends two retractable titanium limbs to spin and batter enemies; this appears to be a natural Diamont ability. He is most vulnerable to the cold of Noxus's Judicator.

Samus met and defeated Spire on the High Ground of Alinos, among the Alimbic sarcophagi, during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters). He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea and escaped in his ship when Gorea died. His search for the Diamonts has no recorded resolution, and he has no recorded appearance after the incident.

Samus knows him as a rival she has beaten; his quest for his lost people echoes nothing she has said aloud, though she herself was raised by a race, the Chozo, that has all but vanished.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spire · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Diamont · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Magmaul · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/High_Ground · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon</sub>


### Prime Hunters: The Alimbic Cluster incident

*Keys:* Alimbic Cluster, Alimbic Cluster incident, Tetra Galaxy, Prime Hunters, Metroid Prime Hunters, rival hunters, Alimbic System, Mission File 79109, Rogue Contract, Federation Empaths, Neural Thread


The Alimbic Cluster incident took place after the Tallon IV mission and before Samus's work on Aether (Metroid Prime Hunters). The Galactic Federation, through its Federation Empaths, intercepted and translated a repeating telepathic Neural Thread originating in the Alimbic Cluster, a largely uncharted region of the Tetra Galaxy outside Federation control. It read: "The secret to ULTIMATE POWER lies in the ALIMBIC CLUSTER." The Federation hired Samus Aran under a covert Rogue Contract (Mission File 79109): discover the meaning of the message, secure the power for Federation control, or failing that keep it secret or destroy it. The disposition of rival interests was presumed hostile.

Six other bounty hunters had intercepted the same message: Kanden, Weavel, Spire, Noxus, Trace and Sylux. Samus found only the ruins of the extinct Alimbics across four sites: the Celestial Archives, Alinos, the Vesper Defense Outpost and Arcterra. Scanning Alimbic Datashades, she learned that a shapeshifting entity, Gorea, had destroyed the Alimbic civilization and that the Alimbics had bound it in the Seal Sphere aboard the Oubliette, a prison hidden in the Infinity Void. The message had almost certainly come from Gorea itself. Samus nevertheless gathered the eight Octoliths, each guarded by a Cretaphid or a Slench and contested by the rival hunters, who took an Octolith from her whenever they bested her. In the same ruins she recovered the six rivals' own affinity weapons: the Volt Driver and Shock Coil in the Celestial Archives, the Magmaul on Alinos, the Battlehammer on the Vesper Defense Outpost, and the Judicator and Imperialist on Arcterra. With all eight Octoliths in the Cannon Control Room on Alinos she fired the Alimbic Cannon and opened the way to the Oubliette.

The rivals reached the Oubliette first, meaning to destroy the threat, but shattered Gorea's confinement. Gorea rose, drained their energy and turned their own weapons against Samus. She crippled it, used the six affinity weapons to fulfill the Alimbic Prophecy, and took up the Alimbic Omega Cannon, the only weapon able to destroy Gorea's final form. The Oubliette exploded as the hunters fled in their ships. In a vision, three Alimbic spirits appeared before Samus, unarmored, and expressed their gratitude; she then returned to her gunship and departed. The "Ultimate Power" was gone, so the Federation's fallback objective was met.

Samus defeated every one of the six rivals at least once during the incident and knows each by name, weapon and alternate form. Of the six, only Sylux is known to have kept pursuing her afterward.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Cluster · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Tetra_Galaxy · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Octolith</sub>


### Prime Hunters: The Alimbics

*Keys:* Alimbics, Alimbic race, Alimbic people, Alimbic civilization, Alimbic Order, Order of Elders, Alimbic Elders, Almiiak, Arctherus, Essence Transference, Datashade, Datashades


The Alimbics were a technologically advanced race of the Tetra Galaxy, beings of superior mental prowess, now extinct. Eons ago they colonized the region still called the Alimbic Cluster, settling the hot planet Alinos and the frigid Arcterra and building stations such as the Celestial Archives and the Vesper Defense Outpost. An Alimbic had three legs, two arms, a single eye and a floating head; a very durable exoskeleton let them endure both worlds' extremes. They could alter their form, and ultimately dissolve it, through a technique called Essence Transference.

Their records survive as Alimbic Datashades, blue packets of telepathic data scattered across the Cluster, invisible to the eye but readable with a Scan Visor and built to keep their integrity indefinitely. They tell the Alimbics' history. A peaceful tribal era ended when an alien invasion caught them unprepared; the survivors vowed never to be caught unprepared again and turned to military science, developing weapons, including bioweapons, that outpaced every enemy. General Almiiak led them to victory in the Tetra Border Wars, which his tomb inscription places two thousand years before it was written, long before the Alimbic War; he later formed the Alimbic Order of Elders, an eight-member council and the only government their records name, though another record says the eight Elders gathered to create the Order during the war against Gorea. The scientist Arctherus's discovery of Neurogenesis opened an age of peace, prosperity and advances in telepathy, telekinesis and extradimensional exploration. Their own scriptures admit that confidence became overconfidence: "Our pride was a veil over our eyes."

Then Gorea fell from the sky, first mistaken for a comet. The Alimbic War lasted three standard galactic months; death swept across their worlds, every weapon was turned back on its makers, and the Alimbics feared Gorea would consume the whole galaxy. Unable to destroy it, and with the death toll rising, the Order of Elders chose containment. They lured Gorea into a trap, bound it in the Seal Sphere, placed the sphere in the Oubliette and used the Alimbic Cannon to hurl that prison into the Infinity Void. To power the seal, the surviving citizens reduced themselves to pure telepathic energy through Essence Transference, and each of the eight Elders placed their essence in one of the eight Octoliths. The race vanished from the physical world, leaving its last recorded messages across the Cluster; the one in Echo Hall on Alinos reads, "We depart from this place of death." They asked that their disappearance not be investigated and warned any who came after not to seek Gorea unless they could destroy it.

On Alinos's High Ground stand the sarcophagi of Almiiak and Arctherus and the Interment Chamber of warriors buried so their spirits could return to the planet's core. Samus read these records during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), and when Gorea finally died, three Alimbic spirits appeared to her in a vision to express their gratitude.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Datashade · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seal_Sphere · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters</sub>


### Prime Hunters: The Oubliette, the Seal Sphere and the Alimbic Omega Cannon

*Keys:* Oubliette, Seal Sphere, Infinity Void, Alimbic Omega Cannon, Omega Cannon, Alimbic Prophecy, Gorea's prison  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Oubliette, Seal Sphere, Infinity Void, Alimbic Omega Cannon, Alimbic Prophecy, Gorea, Alimbic, Alimbics


The Oubliette is the Alimbic station built to hold Gorea forever. The Alimbics sent it into the Infinity Void, a parallel dimension of formless antimatter outside this galaxy that only the Alimbic Cannon can open, so that Gorea could neither escape nor be freed by accident. Its entrance halls hold Alimbic Datashades recording how Gorea was lured into a trap, sealed and sent away.

The Seal Sphere is the device at the heart of the prison. After every weapon failed, the Alimbic Order of Elders built it from the spirits of their surviving citizens, who reduced themselves to pure telepathic energy through Essence Transference. The sphere bonded to Gorea when the creature tried to absorb it, and it draws on both the Alimbics' combined psychic energy and Gorea's own captive psychic power, so that Gorea cannot escape while ensnared. It rested on the central island of the Oubliette's main chamber, ringed by a lethal pool. When the six rival hunters forced their way in ahead of Samus, meaning to destroy the threat, they broke Gorea's confinement instead and were drained. In the battle that followed Samus destroyed Gorea's arms, the sphere rose free, and she wounded it with whichever of the hunters' weapons Gorea was then vulnerable to, which weakened Gorea at the cost of the Alimbic presence within the sphere.

The Alimbic Prophecy, left in eight verses across the four sites' gateways and other chambers, foretold that the Alimbic power would materialize when six frequencies shimmered in a divine spectral order: Yellow Lightning (the Volt Driver), then the Greenwood (Battlehammer), an Orange Blaze (Magmaul), Blue Smoke (Shock Coil), Violet Crystal (Judicator) and a Red Stain (Imperialist). Striking six wall devices in the main chamber in that order with those weapons carried Samus and Gorea into a lower chamber where the Alimbic Omega Cannon waited at the bottom.

The Alimbic Omega Cannon was built by the Alimbics and left in the Oubliette, apparently as a last resort should the seal fail; their surviving records never mention it, saying only that no weapon of theirs could defeat Gorea. It has no ammunition limit, does not harm its wielder, and was the only thing that could hurt Gorea's final form; its shots blacked out the six glowing points on Gorea's body until the creature died and the Oubliette tore itself apart. Samus wielded it once, during the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters), and it presumably perished with the Oubliette. It is a different weapon from the Omega Cannon she later gained temporarily from E.M.M.I. Central Units on ZDR (Metroid Dread).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Oubliette · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Seal_Sphere · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Cannon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Infinity_Void · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alimbic_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gorea</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Trace

*Keys:* Trace, Kriken, Krikens, Kriken Empire, Imperialist, Triskelion  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Kriken, Krikens, Kriken Empire, bounty hunter, Imperialist, Triskelion, Alimbic, Alimbics, Arcterra


Trace is a Kriken, a vicious race that ranks among the most hated and feared in the galaxy for its ruthless imperialism; the Kriken Empire covers much of the galaxy. When a Kriken reaches a certain age its clan exiles it as a rite of passage, and the exile is not accepted back until it has expanded the Empire. Trace is such an exile, roaming the galaxy to find planets for the Kriken to conquer. The quest for ultimate power consumes him (per the manual): his every thought is bent toward bringing supremacy to the Krikens and honor to himself, and he saw the Alimbic Cluster's promise as the way to both.

His weapon is the Imperialist, a long-range quantum cascade laser with precision targeting that suits the sniper's role he prefers; while he holds still with it he can cloak himself and vanish from sight. His alternate form is the Triskelion, a three-legged war machine with a powerful lunge attack into which sophisticated Kriken military biotechnology lets him biomorph; it dashes at damaging speeds and can likewise cloak while motionless. His slender exoskeleton may not be as durable as it appears, which may be why he fights from a distance. He is most vulnerable to Sylux's Shock Coil.

During the Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters) Trace perched at the back of Sic Transit on Arcterra, above the fight between Samus and Noxus, and sniped at her until she beat him. He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance afterward.

To Samus he is a rival she has beaten and a scout for an empire that conquers worlds; nothing in the record shows any contact between them outside the Cluster.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Trace · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Kriken · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Triskelion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Imperialist · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sic_Transit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon</sub>


### Prime Hunters: Weavel

*Keys:* Weavel, Battlehammer, Halfturret, Pirate cyborg, cyborg Pirate


Weavel is a Space Pirate turned cyborg and one of the six rival hunters drawn to the Alimbic Cluster (Metroid Prime Hunters). Before his transformation he served in a Space Pirate special unit, and the official site calls him a once-powerful Space Pirate General. He was critically wounded in a past clash between the Space Pirates and Samus Aran; the manual says he was badly injured in a past battle with her.
