Only his brain and spinal cord remained functional, and they were fused to a mobile life-support system (per the official site); the manual describes his body as reconstructed and fused with a Power Suit. The result is a formidable and ferocious warrior. Like the other hunters he came for the Ultimate Power; whether he would have delivered it to the Space Pirates or kept it for himself is unknown.

His weapon of choice is the Battlehammer, a heavy-duty repeating weapon powered by a miniature nuclear reactor whose rounds have a lethal blast radius; the records of the incident advise keeping one's distance from him. In Weavel's hands its rounds explode on impact for additional damage, with markedly larger blasts than when anyone else fires it. His cybernetic body can split into the Halfturret: the upper half walks on its arms while the detached legs remain as a turret carrying powerful projectile weapons, and both halves attack independently. He is most vulnerable to Kanden's Volt Driver.

In the Cluster, Weavel ambushed Samus at the top of Echo Hall on Alinos and lost to her. He was among the six who broke into the Oubliette ahead of her, was drained by Gorea, and fled in his ship when Gorea died. He has no recorded appearance after the incident.

Samus is the cause of his condition: she is the reason there is more machine than Pirate left of him, and the Alimbic Cluster was at least their second clash. He knows her as the hunter who nearly killed him; she knows him as a Pirate she once critically wounded.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Weavel · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Battlehammer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Affinity_Weapon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_Hunters · https://omegametroid.com/?p=14836</sub>


## Prime 2


### Prime 2: Aether and Dark Aether

*Keys:* Aether, Dark Aether, Transdimensional Flux, Dark Vapor, Safe Zone, Safe Zones, Light Crystal, Light Crystals, Light Beacon, Light Beacons, Ingclaw, Ingclaws


Aether is a rogue planet without a sun, drifting in the Dasha Region on the edge of Galactic Federation space. The star-born Luminoth, led there by the scout X-Qar, settled it in an age their lore calls the Golden Age: a fertile, aged world of mountains, the Agon plains and the Torvus forest, with a Great Temple raised between the three domains. Because the planet's energy was failing, the Luminoth built Energy Controllers to preserve and regulate it; this stored energy, the Light of Aether, also powered a planet-wide weather control system and a light-based teleportation network.

About fifty years before Samus's arrival a Leviathan from Phaaze struck Aether. The Luminoth saw it coming and could not divert it. Agon was scorched into desert, Torvus was swallowed by the sea, and the planet entered Transdimensional Flux: half of its energy and part of the impacted matter were pulled into a newly born twin dimension occupying the same space, Dark Aether. The Ing were born of that dark world. Aether itself grew unstable; extreme climate shifts left most of the planet uninhabitable, and only the northern regions still support life.

Dark Aether is a twisted, poisonous mirror of Aether: dim, toxic, rich in Phazon, each region echoing a Luminoth domain (the Sky Temple Grounds, Dark Agon Wastes, Dark Torvus Bog, the Ing Hive and the floating Sky Temple). Its air kills any being not native to it, rooted Ingclaws thicken it further with Dark Vapor waste, and the surface waters of Dark Torvus are venomous. Luminoth scouts could not survive long there, so the Luminoth made Light Crystals, and when crystals ran short, cheaper and unstable Light Beacons, which pool Aether's light into spherical Safe Zones that cancel the atmosphere, heal those inside and burn the Ing. Dark energy can nullify a crystal for a time; the Light Beam or Annihilator Beam restores it, and a crystal charged by the Light Beam kills any Ing that steps into its glow. Travel between the worlds runs through portals, some natural and most opened by Luminoth machines; how the Ing open their own rifts is unknown. The Space Pirates forced temporary rifts open with a strange artifact found near the dimensional anomaly, which their science team dubbed the Dark Beam.

Samus's Varia Suit could not withstand Dark Aether's air; she lived from Safe Zone to Safe Zone until the Luminoth's Dark Suit, and later the Light Suit, shielded her. When she drew the last planetary energy from the Emperor Ing, Dark Aether collapsed and vanished with the entire Ing Horde. Aether is slowly recovering: its energy supply, once torn in two, is stable again, the Luminoth are mending their world under Federation supervision, and the G.F.S. Anhur later helped them dismantle the abandoned Pirate mining facility and carried off its Phazon ore.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_Crystal · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Controller</sub>


### Prime 2: Amorbis, Chykka and Quadraxis

*Keys:* Amorbis, Chykka, Quadraxis, Dark Agon Temple, Dark Torvus Temple, Hive Temple, Chykling, Chyklings, Dark Sphere, Head Module


Three guardians held the Energy Controllers of Dark Aether's temples, and Samus had to kill each before she could drain the stolen Light of Aether back to the Luminoth.

Amorbis (Dark Agon Temple, Dark Agon Wastes). Gigantic tunneling worms native to Dark Aether, immensely strong and nearly indifferent to pain, that sense prey on the surface from deep underground. In the temple three of them rose in turn from the sand, ramming Samus and fusing themselves to the Dark Sphere at the chamber's heart; joined to it, a Dark Amorbis grows armor, regenerates, sweeps the room with beams and fires dark energy that nullifies the Safe Zones, and a crystal it darkens cannot be recharged. Samus shattered each head's armor, let the dying worm inhale her in Morph Ball form and detonated bombs in the soft flesh of its mouth. When the last Amorbis died the Dark Sphere burst, and inside lay the Dark Suit.

Chykka (Dark Torvus Temple, Dark Torvus Bog). A single rapidly aging insect of both dark and light nature. Samus freed its larva from a cocoon above the temple's poisoned water; it circled her, spat acid and tried to drag her into its mouth with its tongue. Stunned, it slid under and surfaced moments later as a winged adult that rammed her and fired bursts of dark water, then, when its four wings were torn, fell back and rose as Dark Chykka, its body sheathed in dark energy with only its eggsac exposed, spawning Chykling swarms to defend it. Samus killed it; her scans found no trace of Ing possession in the corpse and no likelihood of resurrection. Afterward she claimed the Dark Visor.

Quadraxis (Hive Temple, Ing Hive). A four-legged Luminoth war machine of Sanctuary's making, several times the size of the Quads it resembles; an unfinished twin still lies in parts in the Sanctuary Temple. An Ing possessed it as Samus entered the Hive Temple and it went rogue in the Horde's service. The walker fired Annihilator-type blasts, homing missiles, turret fire and leg shock waves; Samus boosted into the orbs beneath its feet to cripple its knees until the body collapsed. Its Head Module then detached and fought on, commanded through the body's antenna. With the Echo Visor she targeted the antenna to drop the head's shield, destroyed its three sonic receivers (each loosing a Dark Quad), stunned the head and rode its hull by Spider Ball to bomb its weak points. The head crashed and exploded, and she took the Annihilator Beam from the wreck.

Six lesser Ing Guardians, the Warrior Ing and Darklings that held her stolen abilities, stood between Samus and these temples.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Amorbis · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chykka · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quadraxis · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_Crystal · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctuary_Fortress</sub>


### Prime 2: Energy Controllers and the Light of Aether

*Keys:* Energy Controller, Energy Controllers, Light of Aether, Transfer Module, planetary energy, Sky Temple Key, Sky Temple Keys, Dark Temple Key, Dark Agon Key, Dark Torvus Key, Ing Hive Key, Alpha Splinter


The Light of Aether is the planetary energy of Aether, the collected solar, bio and geothermal power that the Luminoth gathered when their sunless rogue world began to die. Their greatest minds built the Energy Controllers to preserve and regulate it: a Main Energy Controller in the Great Temple and three sub-controllers in the temples of Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary. Each region's share is bound to its own land. The Energy Controllers fed a planet-wide weather control system and a light-based teleportation network, and the Light brought peace and prosperity. The Luminoth venerate it; their farewells invoke it ("May the Light of Aether serve you well," "may the Light of Aether guide your aim").

The Leviathan impact split the energy in two. Dark Aether formed with Energy Controllers of its own, mirrors of the Luminoth temples, holding the stolen half: the Dark Agon, Dark Torvus and Hive Energy Controllers, and the Sky Temple Energy Controller beneath the Emperor Ing. As U-Mos put it, "Should one world gain control of this energy, the other will perish." To recover their half, the Luminoth built the Energy Transfer Module, a device whose core runs on light energy and can siphon power from one Controller and release it into another. They gave it to the Keybearers for an assault on the Sky Temple. Only A-Kul returned, and she too died; the Ing seized the Module and turned it on Aether, draining Agon, then Torvus, then Sanctuary until only the Great Temple's Controller still shone.

An Ing bearing the Module came for that last light inside the Alpha Splinter, the giant of a Splinter war pack hatched in the Temple Sanctuary. Samus killed the Dark Alpha Splinter and the Module bonded to her Varia Suit with no loss of performance; its light core also made her immune to possession. She drained each Dark Aether Controller in turn, after Amorbis, Chykka and Quadraxis, and returned the energy to the Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary Controllers, where the holograms of the fallen Sentinels I-Sha, A-Voq and O-Lir spoke to her and updated her Translator Module. Drawing the final energy from the Sky Temple destroyed Dark Aether. The Light Suit U-Mos gave her is itself energized by the Light of Aether.

Each Dark Temple was held fast by three locks; the Dark Agon Keys, Dark Torvus Keys and Ing Hive Keys were hidden across its region of Dark Aether. The Sky Temple required ten Sky Temple Keys, hidden by the Ing in Flying Ing Caches visible only to the Dark Visor; A-Kul set one in the Sky Temple Gateway before she died, and Samus recovered the other nine where the Keybearers fell: three in the Sky Temple Grounds, two each in the Dark Agon Wastes, Dark Torvus Bog and the Ing Hive.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Controller · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Light_of_Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Energy_Transfer_Module · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sky_Temple_Key · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Temple_Key · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Alpha_Splinter</sub>


### Prime 2: GFS Tyr and Bravo Squad

*Keys:* Tyr, G.F.S. Tyr, GFS Tyr, Bravo Squad, Squad Bravo, Exeter, Captain Exeter, Task Force Herakles, GFMC Compound, Dark Trooper, Dark Troopers, Angseth


The G.F.S. Tyr was an Anhur-class Galactic Federation warship, one of a line built to police and defend planetary systems and named, like other Federation vessels, for an Earth myth: Tyr, the Norse god of single combat. Its crew of Federation Marines was Squad Bravo of GFMC Task Force Herakles, a roster of twenty-two under Captain A.C. Exeter.

On patrol in the Dasha system the Tyr engaged an unknown Space Pirate frigate and crippled it; the frigate crashed on Aether and the Tyr followed, shooting down a Pirate transport full of reinforcements in orbit on the way. A storm on atmospheric entry damaged the ship and magnetic interference killed communications. Exeter made an emergency landing in the Temple Grounds and split his people into two forces: one to guard and repair the ship, the other to set up an operations base. They scavenged the Tyr for makeshift defenses, set a GM-42 "Growler" turret, a dated but effective point-defense design, at their security station, and raised the GFMC Compound beside a Splinter hive. Against science specialist B. Reevs's warning that the hive was a small part of a larger network, they attacked it; Exeter later wrote, "It's stupid to stir a hornet's nest, especially if you plan on sleeping under it." The Splinters, possessed by the Ing, came in waves. The engines could not be restored, the distress beacon was jammed by atmospheric interference, ammunition ran out. "Pretty soon we'll have to go to bayonets," gunner F. Triplette recorded. PFC L. Brouda: "If anyone reads this, know that we did our duty and fought well." PFC E. Denys, the last alive, found everyone at the ship dead and set out alone for the alien building nearby. Exeter's final report breaks off with "Each unit was doing fine, no problems... ...until they appeared."

Contact was lost eight days before Samus Aran accepted the contract to locate the troopers and render assistance. She found only bodies. In the hive's command chamber the Marines who had pushed into the Splinter nest rose before her as Dark Troopers, clumsy corpses driven by an unknown parasitic biomass, their armor and weapons still working; the rest, Exeter among them, lay dead in the compound near the terminal that held his final report, their logs intact. Comms specialist M. Angseth had wished aloud to be out taking care of business like Samus Aran instead of pushing buttons and sending reports, and PFC I. Crany had found it hard to believe that one person could take out an entire Space Pirate base. The Ing that killed them did not outlive her mission, and the Federation later sent another Anhur-class ship, the G.F.S. Anhur, back to Aether to dismantle the Pirates' mining facility with the Luminoth.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFS_Tyr · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFMC_Task_Force_Herakles · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/GFMC_Compound · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Command_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Trooper_Logs · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Captain_A._Exeter</sub>


### Prime 2: Regions of Aether (Temple Grounds, Agon Wastes, Torvus Bog, Sanctuary Fortress)

*Keys:* Temple Grounds, Great Temple, Agon Wastes, Agon, Torvus Bog, Torvus, Sanctuary Fortress, Sky Temple, Ing Hive, Dark Agon, Dark Torvus, Sky Temple Grounds


Aether's habitable north holds four Luminoth domains, each with a dark reflection on Dark Aether.

Temple Grounds and the Great Temple. The crossroads of the Luminoth colonies lies beneath the Great Temple, built between the three domains as a place of peace and a monument to Luminoth achievement; it houses the Main Energy Controller, the Temple Sanctuary and the stasis chambers where the Luminoth slept. The Grounds hold the Hall of Honored Dead, a vast Splinter hive where the Marines of the G.F.S. Tyr died and rose as Dark Troopers, and the GFMC Compound they raised around their downed ship; Samus's gunship came down here too. Its reflection, the Sky Temple Grounds, is four broken, Phazon-soaked sections where Samus, having followed Dark Samus through a portal from the Marines' positions in the hive, lost her equipment; it holds the Sky Temple Gateway, whose shaft of light is the only road to the floating, upside-down Sky Temple where the Emperor Ing kept the last stolen energy.

Agon Wastes. Once green farmland, scorched to desert by the impact; Luminoth mining stations dot the sand among Sandiggers, Sandbats and Blueroot trees. The Agon Temple, Sentinel I-Sha's seat, was the first domain to fall, defended at the end by the child acolyte D-Chr. The Space Pirates built their Phazon mining facility here after their science teams detected Phazon in the Dasha system, holding Tallon Metroids and forcing rifts into Dark Aether; Samus fought them and Dark Samus in its Main Reactor. The Dark Agon Wastes beyond the portals are Phazon-laced Ing territory, and Amorbis guards the Dark Agon Temple.

Torvus Bog. A forest drowned by the sea, now warm marsh above and flooded Luminoth works below (the Hydrodynamo Station, the submerged Torvus Temple). Sentinel A-Voq died here resisting possession; B-Stz slew the Ing Horde's war chief in the Fourth Battle of Torvus before the domain fell. Grenchlers, Bloggs and the Alpha Blogg infest the waters. Dark Torvus Bog's surface lies under venomous dark water and is patrolled by Hunter Ing; Chykka guards the Dark Torvus Temple.

Sanctuary Fortress. Founded by E-Btr high in the cliffs, the first settlement and the summit of Luminoth engineering, defended by Quads, Rezbits, Ingsmashers and drones built under Sentinel O-Lir. The Ing corrupted the machines one by one; O-Lir's corpse bore the marks of Luminoth weapons, most likely his own rogue drones, and Sanctuary was the last domain to fall. Its hero C-Pul, the Ingslayer, felled over seven hundred Ing before dying in the Battle of the Hive. Pirate Commandos ambushed Samus on her arrival; Dark Samus destroyed the entrance bridge to keep her from the elevator to the Temple Grounds, and fought her a second time from the Aerie Access elevator into the Aerie Transport Station. Its reflection is the Ing Hive, heart of the Horde, never taken by the Luminoth, where the rogue Quadraxis guards the Hive Temple.

Samus crossed all of them by Luminoth transports and portals, returning to U-Mos in the Great Temple between each domain.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Temple_Grounds · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Great_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Agon_Wastes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Torvus_Bog · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctuary_Fortress · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/O-Lir</sub>


### Prime 2: The Aether incident (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes events)

*Keys:* Aether incident, Metroid Prime 2, Prime 2, Metroid Prime 2: Echoes, Dark Echoes, Aether mission, Dasha, Dasha Region, Dasha system, Ing-Luminoth War, Ing Luminoth War


The Aether incident is Samus Aran's mission to the rogue planet Aether in the Dasha Region, where she ended the war between the Luminoth and the Ing (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). It came after the Tallon IV incident and before the Phazon crisis.

The Galactic Federation contracted Samus to locate Squad Bravo of GFMC Task Force Herakles, Federation Marines aboard the G.F.S. Tyr who had chased a crippled Space Pirate frigate to Aether and gone silent eight days earlier. Lightning storms over the planet damaged her gunship and forced it down on the Temple Grounds. In the Splinter hive where the Marines had dug in she found them dead among the creatures; then the corpses rose as Dark Troopers, animated by an unknown biomass with parasitic tendencies. She glimpsed a figure in a black echo of her own armor, Dark Samus, and pursued her through a portal into Dark Aether. There Dark Samus shattered the Light Crystal shielding Samus, and a swarm of Warrior Ing stripped her of most of her equipment before she was flung back to Aether with only the basic functions of her Varia Suit. At the Marines' GFMC Compound she found the rest of the crew dead beside the wreck of the Tyr and read Captain A.C. Exeter's unfinished final report.

In the Great Temple she destroyed a Splinter war pack and the Ing-possessed Alpha Splinter; the Energy Transfer Module it carried bonded to her suit. U-Mos, last Sentinel of the Luminoth, told her how a stellar impact fifty years earlier had torn Aether into a light world and a dark one, how the Ing had stolen the Light of Aether, and that should one world gain control of that energy, the other would perish. He updated her Translator Module to read Violet holograms and marked the next temple on her map; the holograms of the fallen Sentinels I-Sha, A-Voq and O-Lir later taught her Amber, Emerald and Cobalt in turn.

In the Agon Wastes she fought the Space Pirates mining Phazon there and, in their Main Reactor, Dark Samus, who was raiding their Phazon stores; then she crossed to the Dark Agon Temple and killed Amorbis. In Torvus Bog she killed Chykka in the Dark Torvus Temple. In Sanctuary Fortress she battled the Luminoth's own corrupted mechanoids, fought Dark Samus again from the Aerie Access elevator into the Aerie Transport Station, and destroyed the rogue Quadraxis in the Ing Hive. Each victory let her drain a Dark Aether Energy Controller and restore its light to a Luminoth temple. Along the way she hunted down the Ing Guardians that held her stolen abilities and recovered the lost equipment of the Luminoth champion A-Kul.

With three temples restored, U-Mos gave her the Light Suit. She gathered the nine Sky Temple Keys still lying where the Keybearers had fallen across Dark Aether, rode the Sky Temple Gateway's beam into the floating Sky Temple and destroyed the Emperor Ing in its Sanctum. Taking the last of Dark Aether's energy began the dark world's collapse. Dark Samus, bloated with Phazon, barred her escape; Samus broke her apart and dove through a portal as Dark Aether and the whole Ing Horde ceased to exist.

Back in the Great Temple, Samus watched U-Mos wake the Luminoth from stasis. The survivors bowed to her as she left in her repaired gunship. Pirate logs of the period call her "the accursed Hunter"; the Luminoth remember her as the warrior who gave them back their world. The Federation later returned with the G.F.S. Anhur to help the Luminoth dismantle the Pirate mining facility.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aether · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Logs</sub>


### Prime 2: The Ing and the Emperor Ing

*Keys:* Ing, Ing Horde, Emperor Ing, Warrior Ing, Hunter Ing, Inglet, Inglets, Darkling, Darklings, Dark Pirate, Dark Pirates, Dark Splinter


The Ing (the Ing Horde, the Dark Tribe) are the shapeshifting natives of Dark Aether, born when the Leviathan impact split Aether in two. "Ing" is the Luminoth word for terror. In their own dimension they are amorphous dark metamorphs; they cross to Aether as gas but cannot survive long on its surface in their own bodies, so they possess creatures, corpses and even machines, which become Darklings: stronger, tougher, and in some cases gifted with new attacks. How the Ing open their rifts between the worlds is unknown. The Horde lives by fierce competition for rank, rites of passage in which the young fight to prove themselves, and cannibalism; the Keybearer G-Sch watched them feed upon their dead. Their one great weakness is bright, pure light, which they hate and fear: the Luminoth Light Beam burns them, and a Light Crystal energized by it kills any Ing that steps into its glow.

Ing Larva, the first and weakest form, swarm for protection, and Ingstorms gnaw through almost anything given time. Inglets are small laborers that cling to walls and spit dark matter. Warrior Ing are the Horde's soldiers: they melt into puddles, run up walls, slash with razor forelegs and hurl their bodies sheathed in dark energy; a swarm of them stripped Samus of her equipment, and those that absorbed her abilities became the Guardians she later hunted. Hunter Ing, the Horde's high-ranking warriors, phase out of local space to ambush with long tentacles; only they may possess Pirate Commandos, and they compete fiercely for the right. Darklings include Dark Splinters, the Dark Troopers raised from the dead Marines of the G.F.S. Tyr, Dark Pirate Troopers and Commandos, Dark Tallon Metroids, Dark Bloggs, Dark Grenchlers, and the corrupted Quads, Ingsmashers and drones of Sanctuary Fortress. The Luminoth could resist possession only briefly and chose death over serving as Ing weapons; the light energy in the Energy Transfer Module made Samus immune.

The Horde's seat on Dark Aether is the Ing Hive, which no Luminoth assault ever took, and its ruler holds the Sky Temple. The Emperor Ing is the eldest, first and strongest of its kind, swollen and mutated by the Phazon it absorbed. It molded its body around the Sky Temple's Energy Controller in the Sanctum, using itself as shield and weapon while siphoning the last stolen energy. Samus faced it in three shapes: a tentacled head over an armored eye that fires sustained beams; a regenerating chrysalis with eleven weak points that fills the chamber with poison; and a warrior-like final form guarded by Phazon-charged barriers that only beams of the opposite polarity or the Annihilator Beam could break. Its death and the loss of the last planetary energy unmade Dark Aether, and the entire Ing Horde vanished with it. The Ing are extinct.

The Space Pirates, who called them shadowlings, suffered raid after raid and steady attrition at their hands. To Samus they were the force that killed Squad Bravo and nearly killed her in her first minutes on Dark Aether; U-Mos warned her that had Aether fallen, the Ing would have turned to the stars for new worlds to ravage.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Emperor_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Warrior_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hunter_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Darkling · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Pirate_Commando</sub>


### Prime 2: The Ing Guardians and Samus's stolen equipment

*Keys:* Bomb Guardian, Jump Guardian, Boost Guardian, Grapple Guardian, Spider Guardian, Power Bomb Guardian, Ing Guardian, Ing Guardians, stolen equipment, Judgment Pit, Dark Torvus Arena, Alpha Sandigger


Minutes into her first crossing to Dark Aether, Samus Aran was ambushed in the Sky Temple Grounds. Dark Samus shattered the Light Crystal protecting her and a swarm of Warrior Ing tore away most of her equipment before she was thrown back through the portal to Aether. The Ing that carried off her abilities absorbed them, and several possessed Aether's creatures to wield the stolen powers. Samus hunted these Guardians one by one across both worlds.

The Bomb Guardian was the Ing that took her Morph Ball Bombs; it possessed the Alpha Sandigger, dominant male of its species, in the Agon Temple. Its mutated second head dropped and sprayed bombs, and head and tail were its only vulnerable spots: a stunning hit to the tail left the head open. The Jump Guardian, a Warrior Ing holding the Space Jump Boots, lay hidden in the Judgment Pit of the Dark Agon Wastes until she stepped into the Safe Zone, then leapt with shockwaves behind a barrier that fell only when it landed. The Boost Guardian, a Warrior Ing in the Dark Torvus Arena, a chamber with no Light Crystal or Beacon at all, hurled itself at her as a boosting ball and melted into a puddle that chased her across the floor. The Grapple Guardian, a Darkling Grenchler in the Sacrificial Chamber of Dark Torvus Bog, swung the Grapple Beam in place of its lightning; its eye and the flesh under its back shell were its weak spots, and a wound to the eye enraged it. The Spider Guardian, a Darkling Pillbug in the Dynamo Works of Sanctuary Fortress, raced along magnetic tracks; immune to her weapons, it died only when she drove it into the works' electrical nodes. The Power Bomb Guardian, a Darkling Sporb in the Undertemple of Dark Torvus Bog, fired Power Bombs instead of venomous needles and was all but invulnerable; Samus climbed the walls to four Bomb Slots and brought part of the ceiling down on it.

The Luminoth also kept the gear of their champion A-Kul for whoever came after her: her Seeker Missile launcher in a sacred vault, the device that let her move through liquid as if through air, which the Ing had taken, and the visor that pierced Dark Aether's eternal night. Samus retrieved them all. By the time she faced the Emperor Ing she carried every ability she had lost plus the Luminoth's Dark Beam, Light Beam, Annihilator Beam, Dark Suit, Light Suit, Dark Visor and Echo Visor, and a Screw Attack recovered from a Luminoth vault in Sanctuary Fortress. Samus's scans record the Guardians coldly as metamorphs that absorbed the power of each device; she took back what was hers and moved on.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Warrior_Ing · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bomb_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Jump_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Boost_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Grapple_Guardian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Spider_Guardian</sub>


### Prime 2: The Luminoth

*Keys:* Luminoth, Luminoths, Keybearer, Keybearers, A-Kul, Luminoth Lore, X-Qar, E-Btr, Sentinel of Aether, Sentinels of Aether, moth people, Hall of Honored Dead


The Luminoth are a tall, winged, moth-like people who settled the rogue planet Aether. Their lore says they were born not of Aether but of the stars: nomads who roamed for generations and, after meeting other enlightened races such as the N'kren, the Ylla and the Chozo, resolved to bind themselves to a homeworld as those peoples had. The scout X-Qar found Aether and became the first Sentinel of Aether. Q-Cis, the first Sentinel born on Aether, protected it for three centicycles; V-Mos followed, then J-Gnk, who died in battle defending the Great Temple, and then U-Mos.

An adult Luminoth stands over twice the height of a human, with slender limbs, three long digits and working wings. They fly unaided, have heightened reflexes and durability, and shape energy into shields and telekinetic barriers. A constant spiritual link joins them; A-Kul could feel where her dead comrades lay. They write in a three-dimensional holographic script keyed by color (violet, amber, emerald, cobalt) that Samus could read only after U-Mos and then each Temple Sentinel updated her Translator Module. Families are reckoned as houses (the mated Keybearers J-Stl and B-Stl share a house name), they honor their dead with statues in the Hall of Honored Dead, venerate the Light of Aether, and part with blessings such as "May the Light of Aether shine upon you." Samus's logbook rates their command of energy as the equal of the Chozo's, and their works bear it out: the Energy Controllers, Light Crystals and Light Beacons, the Dark, Light and Annihilator Beams, the Dark and Light Suits, and the mechanoids of Sanctuary Fortress such as the Quads, Ingsmashers and Quadraxis.

In the Golden Age they founded Sanctuary in the mountains under E-Btr, then Torvus and Agon, and raised the Great Temple between the three. The Leviathan impact began their Dark Age. Rifts from Dark Aether released swarms that possessed Aether's creatures, and the Luminoth named the enemy Ing, "terror". They learned that light burned the Ing and that the Ing could possess Luminoth too, so they built shielding and vowed to self-terminate rather than become Ing weapons. Ten Keybearers (A-Kul, J-Stl, B-Stl, S-Dly, G-Sch, S-Jrs, C-Rch, D-Isl, M-Dhe and J-Fme) carried the Energy Transfer Module against the Sky Temple; only A-Kul returned her key before dying. With the captured Module the Ing drained Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary, killing the Sentinels I-Sha, A-Voq and O-Lir and heroes such as the child acolyte D-Chr and C-Pul the Ingslayer, who felled over seven hundred Ing before dying in the Battle of the Hive. Other heroes include K-Tch, a farmer of Agon turned warrior, and B-Stz, who slew the Ing Horde's war chief in the Fourth Battle of Torvus and later died of old age in the temple he had defended. The elderly and the children took up arms; an aged Luminoth and an adolescent lie dead beneath the statues of X-Qar and V-Mos. At last the survivors entered life-preserving stasis in the Great Temple, to wake when the Ing were gone or to sleep forever, while U-Mos alone kept watch.

Samus Aran ended the war. The Luminoth woke, bowed to her as one, and she left them to rebuild in peace; they were last reported healing Aether under Federation supervision.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Keybearer · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hall_of_Honored_Dead · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Translator_Module</sub>


### Prime 2: U-Mos

*Keys:* U-Mos, U Mos, Umos, Sentinel of the Luminoth, V-Mos, J-Gnk, last Sentinel, Luminoth Sentinel


U-Mos is the fifth and last Sentinel of Aether, the one Luminoth who stayed awake through his people's long defeat. His mother V-Mos, the third Sentinel, died giving birth to him; he inherited the post when the fourth Sentinel, J-Gnk, died in battle defending the Great Temple. When the Ing had taken Agon, Torvus and Sanctuary and only the Great Temple's Energy Controller remained, the surviving Luminoth entered life-preserving stasis there; U-Mos remained on guard over the sleepers and the last of the Light of Aether, bracing for the Ing's last, terrible assault and praying for deliverance.

Deliverance came as Samus Aran. After she destroyed the Ing-possessed Alpha Splinter in the Temple Sanctuary and the Energy Transfer Module bonded to her suit, U-Mos appeared with the words "Do not be afraid, I am U-Mos, Sentinel of the Luminoth." He explained the stellar impact that created Dark Aether and the Ing's theft of the planetary energy: "Should one world gain control of this energy, the other will perish." He warned that "Should we fall, the Ing will look to the stars for new planets to ravage and conquer," asked her to recover the energy from Agon, then Torvus, then Sanctuary Fortress, and told her plainly, "You're our only hope, Samus." He called the Ing "creatures of shadow and darkness, knowing nothing of peace or mercy." He updated her Translator Module so that she could read Violet holograms and added the next temple's location to her map.

U-Mos guided Samus from the Great Temple throughout the mission, and she returned to him repeatedly for counsel. When the three temples were restored he gave her the Light Suit, armor blessed and energized by the Light of Aether itself, and sent her after the nine missing Sky Temple Keys. After the Emperor Ing died and Dark Aether collapsed, U-Mos woke his people; he and the other Luminoth bowed to Samus in farewell as she departed, with his blessing: "May the Light of Aether shine upon you!"

Like all Luminoth he is winged, psionic and able to shape energy on a par with the Chozo; Samus's logbook dates him at about 2.15 centicycles and records him as the only active member of his species she met on Aether. He is calm and formal in speech, addresses Samus by name, and treats her as an equal and a savior rather than a hired weapon.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/U-Mos · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ing-Luminoth_War · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Great_Temple · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Luminoth · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hall_of_Honored_Dead · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Translator_Module</sub>


## Prime 3


### Prime 3: Bryyo and the Reptilicus

*Keys:* Bryyo, Bryyonian, Bryyonians, Reptilicus, Mogenar, Mogenars, Lords of Science, Lord of Science, Sfimas, Fuel Gel, GFS Theseus, G.F.S. Theseus


Bryyo is a planet of the Kalandor Region, neighbor to Norion and Elysia. Its orbit leaves 48 percent of the surface in permanent sunlight and 48 percent in permanent night; only a 4 percent equatorial jungle band is habitable. It is the largest known natural source of Fuel Gel, which powers Federation capital ships, and Bryyonian gel sales fund the military forces guarding the region. Its scorched half, Bryyo Fire, holds the Temple of Bryyo above a lake of Fuel Gel; its frozen half is Bryyo Ice.

Bryyonian lore records a golden age that celebrated Bryyus the First, the Liberator, and the launch of a first ship to the stars; the Bryyonians then found stellar brethren in the Chozo, the Luminoth and the Ylla. The Lords of Science ignored Chozo warnings to balance old and new ways. When Sfimas, First Lord of Science, denounced the Primals and their Traditions, schism became war, and friends from the stars tried in vain to end it. The Primals turned to ancient dark powers and won, but the land was poisoned and dying. The exiled Science Lords launched purification machines that saved the planet but split its climate forever into scorched and icy halves. The last Lord of Science, hunted by Primal war bands, found a natural energy source he named Fuel Gel, built stone Mogenars to guard his home, and sheltered a young Primal prophetess who dreamed of a starborne death. When his refuge was discovered he stayed behind so she could flee with their records: "Save our world from the starborne plague."

The Reptilicus appear to be the Primals' descendants: tall four-armed reptiles who wield whips and chakrams, tame Warp Hounds and teleport to corner prey. Phazon hardened them against heat and Fuel Gel; their strongest are the Reptilicus Hunters. They attacked Samus on sight.

Dark Samus's Leviathan struck Bryyo during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), and Rundas, sent to halt the corruption, was consumed by it. Samus found the downed frigate G.F.S. Theseus at the Crash Site. At the Temple of Bryyo, where the Pirates had apparently been drawing Fuel Gel from the lake, she killed Rundas. She destroyed the generators shielding the Seed and in its core faced Mogenar, an ancient war golem reanimated with Phazon and fed through orbs of pure Phazon socketed in its body. Overloading the core cleansed Bryyo but deepened Samus's own corruption.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Bryyo_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Reptilicus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Mogenar · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Temple_of_Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Theseus</sub>


### Prime 3: Elysia and SkyTown

*Keys:* Elysia, SkyTown, Sky Town, Aurora Unit 217, AU 217, Helios, Theronian Bomb, Theonian Bomb, Elysian Seed, Spire Dock, Aurora Chamber, Xenoresearch Laboratories


Elysia is a cloud-shrouded planet of the Kalandor Region, neighbor to Norion and Bryyo, uninhabitable by most organisms; its atmosphere is too dangerous for human researchers. Its dominant inhabitants are the Elysians, mechanoids built by the Chozo. SkyTown, the research facility the Chozo built above its clouds, is a network of Research Pods held aloft by rocket-like engines and joined by skybridges and ziplines. Under the Treaty of Elysia the Federation installed Aurora Unit 217, originally built for GF Naval Base Demeter, in a floating pod linked to the station's network, giving it control of the facility and of the Elysians. As administrator, 217 supplied the Federation with tactical intelligence and stellar research.

During the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) 217 was disabled by the Pirate-made virus, and about a week later Dark Samus's Leviathan tore through Elysia's clouds. Most of SkyTown was destroyed. Space Pirates seized the eastern district, destroyed Elysians in great numbers, raised a shield around the Seed and stationed Phazon Metroids in the Xenoresearch Laboratories, while a Phazon virus bound most surviving Elysians to Pirate control. Ghor, sent to cure 217, was corrupted himself. Less than half of SkyTown remained when Samus arrived.

Samus carried the vaccine to 217 in the Aurora Chamber. Ghor cut 217's lines and wrecked her Gunship at the landing site; she killed him and repaired the connection. 217 then guided her to the three components of a Theronian Bomb, the only means of breaking the Seed's shield, which she hauled with her Gunship's grapple; freeing the second required the Seeker Missile from the Xenoresearch Laboratories, where Phazon Metroids pursued her. She fitted the bomb to the Spire Dock while 217 moved all of SkyTown over the Seed. The Spire Dock then descended on its own; Samus defended it alone against Pirate troopers and Assault Skiffs and escaped by pod as the bomb detonated behind her and shattered the shield. In the Seed she destroyed Helios, a corrupted Elysian machine with four tentacle-like arms that led a swarm of Phazon-energized Swarmbots, and overloaded the core. She also faced the Elysian Defense Drone, which with Ghor and Helios was one of Elysia's three great guardians.

Restored, 217 located Phaaze by triangulating the trajectories of the Leviathan attacks and guided Samus through the rest of the crisis. After the crisis, Samus returned to SkyTown to mourn Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysia · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Helios · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Theronian_Bomb</sub>


### Prime 3: Elysians

*Keys:* Elysian, Elysians, Elysian mechanoid, Elysian mechanoids, Treaty of Elysia, First Elysian, Chozo Searcher, SkyTown Data, Elysian lore, Elysian Data Pod


The Elysians are robotic sentients built by the Chozo on Elysia, custodians of SkyTown and keepers of their own history, recorded in data pods. About 1,500 years before the Phazon crisis the Chozo began building SkyTown; some fifty years in, strained by storms and upkeep, they created the First Elysian, gave it self-awareness and let it share that gift with the others. The Elysians count this as Year One. A Chozo Searcher launched from the station found a far-off planet that was itself sentient, but its data was lost before the world could be identified. That world was Phaaze, and the Chozo charged the Elysians with continuing to research it.

In Year 400 the time came for the Chozo to leave, and they entrusted SkyTown to the Elysians, asking them to keep searching for the sentient planet's secrets. Their own verdict: "We failed." By Year 600 supplies and fuel ran critically low, and the smaller mechanoids they built to process fuel from the atmosphere could not produce enough, so they hibernated for 835 years, station data reaching them only in dreams. In Year 1435 armed aliens tripped SkyTown's defenses and woke them. The resulting Treaty of Elysia had the Galactic Federation supply fuel and parts in exchange for the station's scientific systems and databanks. Because the atmosphere was lethal to human researchers, the Federation installed Aurora Unit 217 instead, with control of the facility and the Elysians; the Elysians called what followed a peaceful life.

In their final months the Elysians detected a meteor-like object strike a planet in a nearby galaxy and traced it to the world the Chozo Searcher had sought. Their warning to the Federation failed when 217 was crippled by an unknown virus. A week later a Leviathan struck Elysia, identical to the object they had seen devour their creators' planet, and their sensors identified Phazon. Space Pirates overran SkyTown, and within two weeks a Phazon virus bound most Elysians to Pirate control. Ghor, sent to restore the Aurora, was corrupted and hunted the survivors. "There is no hope left for us." The last of them transferred their memories to a data pod and waited "before the brink of history's end."

Samus fought corrupted Elysian machines, among them Steamlords, Steambots and the Seed's guardian Helios (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Raised by the Chozo herself, she read the records of this other Chozo creation in SkyTown's data pods.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Helios · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Elysia</sub>


### Prime 3: Gandrayda

*Keys:* Gandrayda, Mistress Gandrayda, NZG41, Jovia XII, shapeshifting hunter, shape-shifting hunter


Gandrayda was a shape-shifting humanoid bounty hunter of unknown homeworld and species, hired by the Galactic Federation with Samus, Rundas and Ghor during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). Federation records likened her to the bioforms of Jovia XII: she could assume the form and abilities of most living things, including creatures far larger than herself, though each borrowed form carried that form's weaknesses. Her age could not be determined, though she seemed young. A mistress of stealth, she used a personal cloaking field to set up her attacks. She treated bounty hunting as a sport, regarded Samus Aran as the rival she meant to surpass, and teased her with the nickname "Sammy."

On Norion, disguised as a Space Pirate, she cut down the Pirates guarding Generator C from within and left the generator for Samus to restore. Dark Samus's Phazon blast at the Control Tower left her generating her own Phazon; the Federation fitted her with a PED worn around her neck and sent her to locate the Pirate stronghold. She found the Pirate Homeworld and was corrupted there. Pirate records name "Mistress Gandrayda" the last of their leader's field commanders and note that unfaithful disciples were ended violently when she tested their loyalty.

On the Homeworld a distress call from a Federation Marine designated NZG41 led Samus to a man she rescued from Pirate militia. As they rode an elevator into the Proving Grounds atop a tower, the Marine revealed himself as Gandrayda: "You know, you should never trust strangers, Sammy. This is going to be fun." Phazon had enhanced her shape-shifting. She turned invisible, attacked in her own form with homing energy blades, shockwaves and close-range claws, and took Phazon-charged forms of a Berserker Knight, an Aerotrooper and a Swarmbot. Later she tore open part of the roof to let in the acid rain, then took the shapes of Rundas, Ghor and finally Samus herself in her Varia Suit before entering Hypermode. Samus defeated her. Dying, Gandrayda shifted uncontrollably between the forms of a Pirate, Ghor, Rundas and Samus until Dark Samus assimilated her.

Gandrayda was the last of Samus's three comrades to die. Samus gained the Grapple Voltage from her and later mourned her with the others at SkyTown.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Gandrayda · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Homeworld · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Generator_C · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Prime 3: GFS Olympus and the Valhalla

*Keys:* Olympus, Valhalla, GFS Olympus, G.F.S. Olympus, Olympus-class, GFS Valhalla, G.F.S. Valhalla, Aurora Unit 242, AU 242, Klar Nebula  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Federation, Dane, Admiral, flagship, battleship, Aurora, Norion, Phaaze


The G.F.S. Olympus is the Olympus-class battleship that served as the Galactic Federation flagship during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), commanded by Fleet Admiral Castor Dane. Olympus-class ships were the first Federation capital ships designed around an onboard Aurora Unit, an organic supercomputer that lets them run with smaller crews and devote the saved space to battle systems; they burn scarce Fuel Gel, which makes each one of extreme value to the military, and they are usually led by an officer of flag rank. The Olympus carries Aurora Unit 242, the first Aurora Unit to receive the vaccine against the Pirate-made virus. 242 then directed Samus through the crisis, appearing as a hologram aboard her Gunship with each new objective, while SkyTown's Aurora Unit 217 located Phaaze.

Samus was summoned to the Olympus in orbit of Norion, where Dane briefed her, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda on the virus crippling the Aurora network, then seven days old, and ordered them to deliver the vaccine. Space Pirate boarding pods and troopers stormed the ship mid-briefing, and a Berserker Lord delayed Samus's departure for Norion. After Dark Samus's attack she lay unconscious aboard the Olympus for a month and woke there fitted with a PED Suit. The Olympus later carried the fleet to the Pirate Homeworld, where its assault broke the Pirate forces once Samus dropped the planetary shield, and it followed the captured Leviathan Battleship through the wormhole to Phaaze.

Its sister ship, the G.F.S. Valhalla, had vanished four months before the briefing. Newly crewed with green recruits at Tivus, it was on a training mission in the Klar Nebula when it was lost. Federation intelligence believed a superior Pirate fleet of Phazon-powered starships had ambushed it; Pirate records credit the battleship Colossus with disabling it and claim the crew were jettisoned into space on Dark Samus's orders. Aurora Unit 313 was removed and carried by Dark Samus to Phaaze. Samus found the drifting wreck in the Gaflar region: Marines impaled on Pirate blades, killed by grenades, crushed by elevators or half-eaten by Phazon bioforms, some crumbling to dust when she opened the doors. After restoring its systems with Energy Cells, she heard 313's final warning about Phaaze in the Control Room, ending "...Darkness coming," and recovered the codes the fleet needed to open the wormhole.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Olympus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_242 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data</sub>


### Prime 3: Ghor

*Keys:* Ghor, Hunter Ghor, Ghor's armorsuit, Wotan VII, cyborg hunter, cybernetic hunter


Ghor was a cybernetic bounty hunter hired by the Galactic Federation with Samus, Rundas and Gandrayda during the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). A veteran of the liberation war of Wotan VII, he lost about 94 percent of his original body protecting that world; only 6 percent of his birth-body remained, the rest cyberware. Federation records described him as gentle, logical and empathetic, not the most skilled of fighters, with a sense of humor. A champion of the poor, he often worked for free or gave his bounty money to his targets' victims. He was highly proficient with mechanical systems and computer infiltration and had an unusual mechanical empathy with artificial intelligences.

Ghor could merge his body into larger machines. His giant robotic armorsuit, built for his cyborg body, doubled as his gunship and carried plasma beams, attack claws and a multimissile system he could fire together as an alpha strike. An energy shield repelled fire from the front, leaving his back-mounted generator exposed, and he could charge at high speed, a risky maneuver on slippery ground. Merged with the suit, his calm demeanor gave way to aggression and violence.

On Norion he cleared the way to Generator C by destroying a Berserker Knight, before Dark Samus's Phazon blast struck down all four hunters. Fitted with a Phazon Enhancement Device, he was sent to Elysia to cure Aurora Unit 217 and destroy the Leviathan. He arrived two weeks after the impact, misused the device and was corrupted; the last Elysians recorded him leading the hunt for their survivors. He severed the lines linking 217 to the Federation network, then met Samus at SkyTown's landing site, seized her Gunship, damaged it and hurled it at her. "Don't you feel the power? Soon everything will be corrupted. Including you." Samus broke his generator, sent him skidding across frozen Fuel Gel, and beat him down even after he entered Hypermode. Coming to his senses, he tried to convert the ruined armorsuit into a gunship; it exploded, leaving him hanging helpless in the air, and Dark Samus drained the Phazon from him until his remains decayed and vanished. Samus recovered the Plasma Beam from him.

Ghor was the second of Samus's comrades to die. She grieved for him with the others at SkyTown when the crisis ended.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Ghor · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


### Prime 3: Norion

*Keys:* Norion, Planet Norion, Norion base, battle of Norion, Norion attack, Generator A, Generator B, Generator C, Aurora Unit 486, Kalandor Region, Kalandor, Vanguard offensive


Norion is a human-dominated planet on the fringe of Galactic Federation space in the Kalandor Region, neighbor to Bryyo and Elysia. Once barren and unable to support life, it was transformed into a hardy forest world by a terraforming project designed by Aurora Unit 486. The Federation regards it as strategically vital and keeps a heavy military presence there; its base is often the first line of defense against enemies operating outside Federation territory. A planetary defense cannon protects the base, fed by three generators, A, B and C, and fired from a Control Tower once all three are powered. Federation Marines in the Norion system were the first to test the Phazon Enhancement Device.

During the Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) Dark Samus personally led a Space Pirate assault on Norion, part of the Pirates' Vanguard offensive against Norion, Bryyo and Elysia, while the G.F.S. Olympus lay in orbit and her Leviathan bore down on the planet. The Pirates shut down the generators and Meta Ridley attacked a Marine platoon. Samus, delayed aboard the Olympus by a Berserker Lord, was cornered on the surface until Rundas destroyed four Pirate ATC gunships. She restored Generator A; Rundas restored Generator B; Ghor destroyed a Berserker Knight to open the way to Generator C, where Gandrayda, disguised as a Pirate, cut down the guards and Samus restored the generator. Meta Ridley then dropped on her and dragged her down the Generator C shaft; she defeated him in free fall and he was presumed destroyed, and Rundas caught her on a slide of ice. At the Control Tower Dark Samus blasted the four hunters with Phazon. Samus stayed conscious long enough to fire the cannon, which destroyed the Leviathan moments before impact, and then collapsed. The other hunters recovered within weeks; Samus lay unconscious aboard the Olympus for a month.

Norion's Leviathan is the only one recorded as destroyed before impact, and Pirate records call the attack "a failure...all thanks to the accursed Hunter, Samus Aran." The Phazon that infused the hunters there changed all four: Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda were corrupted and died, and Samus carried self-generating Phazon in her body for the rest of the crisis. For Samus, Norion is where she last fought beside the three hunters as allies.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Norion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Generator_C · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Meta_Ridley · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data</sub>


### Prime 3: Rundas

*Keys:* Rundas, Phrygisian, Phrygisians, Phrygis, Bes III, ice hunter


Rundas was a Phrygisian bounty hunter from Phrygis, a moon of Bes III known chiefly for ice mining. Like all Phrygisians he generated and shaped ice at will: he glided on sheets of ice formed beneath his feet, hurled chunks of ice, froze objects and missiles in flight, and raised ice floes and stalagmites to trap enemies, making him dangerous at any range. Galactic Federation records called him proud, cocky and arrogant, a hunter who considered himself without rivals and kept a trophy from every target he captured or killed. He preferred to work alone and rarely cooperated well, though he was quiet and dutiful on his own and looked after his allies' safety.

Hired by the Federation with Samus, Ghor and Gandrayda to deliver the vaccine for the Aurora virus (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), Rundas met Samus on Norion's surface when Space Pirate ATC gunships had her cornered, and he destroyed four of them. "Between the two of us, this should be a breeze." He restored Generator B, and after Samus defeated Meta Ridley in free fall down the Generator C shaft he caught her on a slide of ice. At the Control Tower, Dark Samus's Phazon blast felled him with the others.

Fitted with a Phazon Enhancement Device, Rundas was sent to Bryyo to halt the Leviathan's corruption of the planet. He overused the device and fell to a Phazon-induced illness; Samus tracked him by scanning his ice, which showed rising concentrations of Phazon. At the Temple of Bryyo he froze a wave of Pirates that was overwhelming her, then sealed her exit with ice and attacked. Corrupted, he entered Hypermode freely to charge his ice attacks with Phazon. His durable ice armor had one weak point, the neck, exposed only when heavy stress broke the armor away. Samus defeated him, and as he fell he was impaled on one of his own ice formations. A silhouette of Dark Samus drew the remaining Phazon from his body, which disintegrated, and Samus recovered the Ice Missile from his shattered ice.

Rundas was the first of Samus's fellow hunters to die and the first she was forced to kill. She mourned him with Ghor and Gandrayda at SkyTown when the crisis ended.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Rundas · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phrygisian · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Temple_of_Bryyo · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Galactic_Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Generator_C</sub>


### Prime 3: The assault on Phaaze and the end of Phazon

*Keys:* assault on Phaaze, Aurora Unit 313, AU 313, end of Phazon, destruction of Phaaze, Phaaze destroyed, Leviathan Battleship, Phaaze wormhole, Sanctum of Phaaze, Genesis Chamber, Darkness coming


The Phazon crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) ended with a Galactic Federation assault on Phaaze, the living planet that is the source of all Phazon. Aurora Unit 217 had located Phaaze by triangulating the trajectories of the Leviathan attacks, and the Federation learned that Dark Samus was using the stolen Aurora Unit 313, implanted on the planet, to spread Phazon and Leviathans across the galaxy. After Samus destroyed the Seed on the Pirate Homeworld, she powered the wreck of the G.F.S. Valhalla with Energy Cells, heard Aurora Unit 313's last recording, which ended "...Darkness coming," and recovered codes that could activate a wormhole. The Space Pirates had fitted a Leviathan, the star-beast Dark Samus brought back from Phaaze, with cybernetic weapon systems as a dreadnought. Samus fed the codes into its launch center, the Leviathan Battleship tore open a wormhole, and the Federation fleet, led by the G.F.S. Olympus, followed it through to fight the Pirates above Phaaze.

The moment Samus entered the planet, its Phazon drove her corruption to the brink. To survive she vented her suit's energy stores and entered permanent Hypermode, wielding maximum firepower at a price: every shot, every wound and every moment on Phaaze raised the Phazon in her body, and had it filled her completely, the overload would most likely have killed her before she fully turned. In the Genesis Chamber, among husks resembling Metroid Prime, she killed an Infant Leviathan that might otherwise have gone on to another world, then descended to the Sanctum at the planet's core, where Dark Samus abided.

There Dark Samus attacked her, generating echoes of herself in battle. Beaten, Dark Samus merged with Aurora Unit 313, which Phazon had mutated into a tentacled organism fused with her bioessence and bound to the planet. Samus tore open its armored hatch and destroyed the core. With the unit gone Phaaze became unstable; Dark Samus perished, and the collapsing planet was wiped from the galaxy as Samus fled. The fleet feared the worst until her Gunship emerged and she reported the mission complete. The Federation celebrated the extinction of Phazon corruption.

The victory cost many Federation troopers and ships and the lives of Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, and left Samus in evident distress. She returned to SkyTown on Elysia to mourn the three hunters, then departed alone. As she entered hyperspace an unidentified ship, later identified as Sylux's, followed her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Genesis_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus</sub>


### Prime 3: The Phazon crisis

*Keys:* Phazon crisis, Metroid Prime 3, Prime 3, Metroid Prime 3: Corruption, Corruption incident, Aurora virus, Phazon virus, Aurora Unit virus, the four hunters, fellow hunters


The Phazon crisis erupted about six months after the Aether incident (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). The Space Pirate battleship Colossus had carried off the last Phazon from Aether, and inside its Phazon stores Dark Samus re-formed herself. She killed a third of the crew, enthralled the rest, flew alone to Phaaze, the living planet that is the source of all Phazon, and returned riding a Leviathan; the Pirates followed her through a wormhole, and those who refused were shot. Her forces captured the G.F.S. Valhalla and took its Aurora Unit 313, which she implanted on Phaaze to direct the Leviathan Seeds. Seeds struck the Pirate Homeworld, Bryyo and Elysia, another bore down on Norion, and a Pirate-made virus crippled the Galactic Federation's Aurora Unit network.

The Federation hired four bounty hunters: Samus Aran, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda. Fleet Admiral Castor Dane was briefing them aboard the G.F.S. Olympus on the vaccine they were to deliver when Pirate boarding pods struck the ship. Dark Samus personally led the Pirate assault on Norion. The hunters restored the base's three generators; Meta Ridley ambushed Samus at Generator C, and she defeated him in free fall down its shaft, leaving him presumed destroyed. At the Control Tower Dark Samus blasted all four hunters with Phazon, but Samus fired the defense cannon that destroyed the incoming Leviathan before she collapsed.

She woke aboard the Olympus a month later; the others had woken weeks earlier. The blast had left the hunters' bodies generating their own Phazon, and the Federation had fitted each with a Phazon Enhancement Device (PED) to harness it. Rundas had gone to Bryyo, Ghor to Elysia and Gandrayda to find the Pirate stronghold, and contact with all three had been lost.

Samus followed. On Bryyo she killed the corrupted Rundas and the war golem Mogenar in the Seed's core. On Elysia she cured Aurora Unit 217 in SkyTown, killed the corrupted Ghor and broke the Seed's shield with a Theronian Bomb before destroying its guardian Helios; 217 then located Phaaze by triangulating the trajectories of the Leviathan attacks. On the Pirate Homeworld she killed Gandrayda, now a Pirate field commander, dropped the planetary shield for a Federation assault, and destroyed the Seed guarded by Omega Ridley, the revived Meta Ridley. Each Seed she overloaded deepened her own corruption, and Dark Samus drained the Phazon from each fallen hunter.

Samus recovered wormhole codes from the wreck of the Valhalla, and a captured Pirate Leviathan carried the fleet to Phaaze. In its Sanctum she defeated Dark Samus, who merged with Aurora Unit 313; when Samus destroyed the unit, Phaaze became unstable and collapsed as she escaped, and Dark Samus perished with it. The Federation celebrated the end of Phazon corruption, won at the cost of many troopers and ships and the lives of the three hunters. For Samus the crisis meant killing three colleagues she had fought beside at Norion while carrying the enemy's own substance in her body. She returned to SkyTown to mourn them, then flew on alone; an unidentified ship, later identified as Sylux's, followed her.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze</sub>


## Dark Samus


### Dark Samus: Aether and the Dark Hunter

*Keys:* Aether, Dark Aether, Aether incident, Agon Wastes, Sanctuary Fortress, Sky Temple Gateway, Torvus Bog, Luminoth, Warrior Ing, Emperor Ing, Echo Visor, Pirate Logs  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, Phazon clone, Phazon double


Dark Samus reached Aether some time after the Tallon IV incident; how she got there is not recorded. She went straight for the Phazon. The Space Pirates mining the Agon Wastes first believed Samus Aran had come for them, then named the intruder "the Dark Hunter" for her dark armor and "strange new weapons." She wanted their Phazon ore, not their deaths, teleported into their base at will, killed only those who stood in her way and wrecked its stealth field generator. Once the real Samus arrived, Pirate command proposed an alliance: Phazon for Samus's head.

Samus first glimpsed her in the Temple Grounds hive where the Federation Marines had died. Dark Samus slipped through a portal into Dark Aether; Samus followed, and Dark Samus shattered a Light Crystal near her so that a swarm of Warrior Ing could strip Samus of most of her equipment. Thereafter Dark Samus shadowed her across the Luminoth world: sealing the direct route through the Agon mining facility, obliterating three Dark Pirate Troopers who had been trying to force Samus onto a detour, and once, in a Torvus Bog training chamber, re-forming before Samus, laughing, and dissipating without a shot.

They fought three times (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes). In the Main Reactor of the Agon mining facility Samus broke her apart; she re-formed. High in Sanctuary Fortress, on the way to the Aerie, she returned with a potent variant of Samus's Charge Beam, an augmented Boost and invisibility, and when beaten staggered and fell from the fortress edge. The scan read "Reborn, enhanced, and vengeful" and warned she could re-form her body "short of total atomic disruption." The last fight came at the Sky Temple Gateway as Dark Aether collapsed after the Emperor Ing's death. She had gorged on Phazon past what she could contain and became a being of pure, unstable Phazon that only her own Phazon could hurt. Samus caught the Phazon Dark Samus expelled in her Charge Beam and fired it back until her double disintegrated, then escaped as Dark Aether ceased to exist.

She was not finished. A cloud of Phazon above Aether gathered into her shape, could not hold it, and drifted as raw Phazon until Space Pirates salvaging the Phazon left on Aether took it aboard the battleship Colossus. See also the Aether incident, Aether and Dark Aether, the Ing and the Emperor Ing, the Luminoth, Colossus.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_1 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_2 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_3 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_4 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Log</sub>


### Dark Samus: Dark Samus (overview)

*Keys:* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, the Dark Hunter, Phazon clone, Phazon double, Samus's Phazon double


Dark Samus is a Phazon-born double of Samus Aran and the architect of the Phazon crisis. She arose on Tallon IV when the dying Metroid Prime tore the Phazon Suit from Samus in the Impact Crater and rebuilt itself from that suit, Samus's genetic material and its own Phazon essence. Scans record her as an entity of pure, highly unstable Phazon energy wearing a Varia Suit "altered and augmented by the Phazon within her": Samus's exact silhouette, dark in color, lit by the Phazon inside her. Her homeworld is listed as Phaaze. The Space Pirates on Aether, who mistook her for Samus until the real one arrived, named her "the Dark Hunter."

Her history falls into three phases.
- On Aether (Metroid Prime 2: Echoes) she raided the Pirates' Phazon stocks in the Agon Wastes, shadowed Samus across the Luminoth world and fought her three times. In the last clash at the Sky Temple Gateway, as Dark Aether collapsed, she gorged herself into a being of pure Phazon; Samus caught the Phazon Dark Samus expelled in her Charge Beam and fired it back until her double disintegrated. She re-formed above Aether as a drifting cloud and was carried off as raw Phazon by salvaging Pirates.
- Revived aboard the Pirate battleship Colossus, she killed a third of its crew and bent the rest into disciples who called her "the great one." She found Phaaze, had her Pirates seize Aurora Unit 313 from the G.F.S. Valhalla, implanted it in Phaaze's core and used it to launch Leviathan Seeds at the Pirate Homeworld, Bryyo, Elysia and Norion.
- She led the Norion attack in person (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption), blasting Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda with Phazon. Samus woke a month later with Phazon in her body and grew steadily more corrupted over the mission that followed. The other three succumbed to corruption and became Dark Samus's field commanders; Samus defeated each in battle, and Dark Samus consumed what was left of them. At Phaaze's core Samus beat her at last. Dark Samus made one last gesture toward Samus, dissolved and merged into Aurora Unit 313; when Samus destroyed the Unit, Phaaze's core detonated, Phazon was purged from Samus's body and Dark Samus was exterminated with no sign of survival.

She has no recorded speech. Her behavior on record is a laugh, that final gesture, and relentless appetite: for Phazon first, for Samus second. Samus's Logbook moved from "Extreme caution recommended" to its highest threat rating; her scans told her the thing wore her shape and carried her genetic material. The crisis cost Samus three comrades, whom she mourned on Elysia before leaving. See also Metroid Prime (the creature), Phazon, Phaaze, Leviathans, Aurora Unit 313, Rundas, Ghor, Gandrayda.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_2:_Echoes · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Log · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data</sub>


### Dark Samus: Origin from Metroid Prime

*Keys:* Dark Samus origin, Dark Samus's origin, where Dark Samus came from, origin, origins, born, reborn, rebirth, Metroid Prime, Impact Crater, Phazon Suit  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, Phazon clone, Phazon double


Dark Samus was born in the Impact Crater of Tallon IV at the close of the Tallon IV incident (Metroid Prime). Her progenitor was Metroid Prime, the Phazon-mutated Metroid that the Chozo prophesied as the Worm and imprisoned beneath the Artifact Temple (see Metroid Prime (the creature)).

Samus entered the crater in the Phazon Suit, the Phazon-saturated form her Power Suit took after the dying Omega Pirate collapsed onto her and Phazon seeped into her armor. It let her fire the Phazon Beam while standing in the crater's Phazon pools, and only a Phazon-fused Arm Cannon could harm Metroid Prime's exposed core essence. She destroyed the exoskeleton and then the core, but in its last moments the creature desperately reached for her. Its grip on the Phazon in her armor was strong enough to rip the Phazon Suit from her body. Samus, left in her Gravity Suit, escaped as the crater was destroyed and left Metroid Prime for dead.

It did not die. Combining its own Phazon essence with Samus's genetic material and the stolen suit, it formed a new body in her shape and emerged as Dark Samus, with all of Samus's abilities and more power than she had. The Phazon that later spread into Federation space traces back to this rebirth.

No Logbook record states the connection outright; per the official Japanese website, it is the rebirth described above. On Aether Samus's scans reported only "the presence of Phazon and your genetic material within this entity." By instrument that is all Samus ever learned: her double carried her genetic material, wore a Phazon-warped Varia Suit, and appeared after she left the Worm for dead. See also Metroid Prime (the creature), Impact Crater, Phazon Suit, Omega Pirate, Tallon IV, Chozo Lore.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(creature) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_1 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Leviathan</sub>


### Dark Samus: Powers, nature and what Samus knows

*Keys:* powers, abilities, weakness, weaknesses, invisible, invisibility, regenerate, regeneration, teleport, teleportation, Phazon shield, personality  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, Phazon clone, Phazon double


Dark Samus is a construct of Phazon shaped by Samus Aran's genetic material and Metroid Prime's essence. Scans record her as "comprised of pure Phazon energy" and "highly unstable," wearing a version of the Varia Suit "altered and augmented by the Phazon within her." She matches Samus's silhouette in darker colors.

Her arsenal mirrors Samus's and exceeds it:
- a scattershot Phazon beam that can be charged into a sustained stream, missile-like attacks, and a beam that encases targets in Phazon
- Boost and Jump abilities "superior to yours," levitation and flight, and teleportation
- invisibility, first to the Combat and Scan Visors, later to the Dark Visor; only the Echo Visor could find her on Dark Aether
- regeneration by absorbing Phazon, and reconstitution of her whole body from a cloud of Phazon, "short of total atomic disruption"
- at Phaaze, echoes of herself that fight while she recharges, the power to draw energy from Phaaze itself, ice pillars and shock waves
- a Phazon blast that seeds living bodies with Phazon, as it did to Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda on Norion

Ordinary weapons can wound her, but in her pure-Phazon form, behind a Phazon shield, only Phazon can: the energy she herself expelled on Aether, Hypermode on Phaaze. Gorging on Phazon past what she can contain destabilizes her. She is Phazon, and when Phaaze died she died with it.

In nature she is hunger first: on Aether she sought the Pirates' ore, not their destruction. Vengeance second: her scan in Sanctuary Fortress reads "Reborn, enhanced, and vengeful," and she laughed in Samus's face in Torvus Bog before vanishing. Cunning grew with each rebirth, from a thief who wrecked a stealth field generator to a strategist who turned a warship's crew into a cult, raided a Federation training ship for its Aurora Unit and used a living planet as a siege engine. She has no recorded speech. Her fixation on Samus is constant: she sought her out at every encounter and marked her with corruption, and her last field commander, Gandrayda, swore to present Samus's helmet to her as tribute.

Samus's Logbook noted "your genetic material within this entity" and advised "Extreme caution." No record states what Samus feels about the thing that carries her genetic material; what she did was destroy it, on Aether and again on Phaaze. See also Phazon, Hypermode, PED Suit, Phazon Suit, Echo Visor, Dark Visor.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_1 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_2 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_3 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus_4 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Log</sub>


### Dark Samus: The Phazon crisis and her end

*Keys:* Phaaze, Sanctum, PED Suit, Rundas, Ghor, Gandrayda, final battle, purged, exterminated, Phazon crisis, death of Dark Samus  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, Phazon clone


The Phazon Dark Samus drove into the four hunters at Norion took root, and their bodies began producing Phazon of their own. Samus woke a month later aboard the G.F.S. Olympus in a PED Suit, a Phazon Enhancement Device that kept the Phazon in her body in a stable flow and let her enter Hypermode. Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda had woken weeks earlier, been fitted with the same, sent after the Leviathan Seeds on Bryyo, Elysia and the Pirate Homeworld, and gone silent.

Each had been overwhelmed by Phazon and fallen under Dark Samus's sway. The Pirates' logs name them her field commanders: Commander Rundas on Bryyo, Commander Ghor on Elysia, and "Mistress Gandrayda," who vowed to present Samus's "bloody helmet" to Dark Samus as tribute. Samus defeated each in turn: Rundas was impaled by his own ice; Ghor's armor exploded and Dark Samus drank his Phazon; Gandrayda, cycling helplessly through borrowed shapes, was assimilated by Dark Samus outright. Every battle drove Samus's own corruption higher, and her visor took on Phazon's blue.

Samus reached Phaaze through a wormhole opened by a captured Pirate Leviathan (see The assault on Phaaze and the end of Phazon), and the planet's Phazon locked her in permanent Hypermode at the edge of total corruption.

In the Sanctum at Phaaze's core Dark Samus rose for their only direct battle of the crisis (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). She split into echoes of herself and drew on Phaaze's energy, but Hypermode could disrupt her. Beaten, she made one last gesture toward Samus, dissolved into Phazon and poured herself into Aurora Unit 313, which scans found "fused with the bioessence of Dark Samus." Samus tore open its armored hatch and destroyed it.

Phaaze's core detonated and the planet was wiped from the galaxy; Dark Samus was exterminated with no sign of survival, the Phazon was purged from Samus's body, and with its source gone the threat of Phazon corruption ended. The fleet escaped but lost contact with Samus; Fleet Admiral Dane was grieving a friend when her Gunship appeared. She returned to Elysia to mourn Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, and as she departed, Sylux's ship fell in behind her. See also Phaaze, Aurora Unit 313, Rundas, Ghor, Gandrayda, PED Suit, Sylux, Castor Dane.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_313 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctum_(Phaaze) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla</sub>


### Dark Samus: The return – Colossus, Phaaze and the Norion attack

*Keys:* Colossus, Valhalla, G.F.S. Valhalla, GFS Valhalla, Norion, disciples, the great one, Leviathan, Leviathans, Leviathan Seed, Leviathan Seeds, Pirate Homeworld  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Dark Samus, Dark-Samus, Dark Hunter, Phazon clone, Aurora Unit 313


Dark Samus returned about six months after the Aether incident, and she returned as a conqueror.
