The crew of the Pirate battleship Colossus, sworn to "take that Phazon or die trying," salvaged the Phazon left on Aether without knowing it held her remains. She revived in the ship's Phazon storage, consumed it, killed a third of the crew and "beguiled" the rest. The survivors renounced their old vows and swore allegiance to her as "our liberator" and "the great one"; the same logs call her "the fiendish shrew."

She went alone to Phaaze, the living source of all Phazon; crewmen who muttered that she had abandoned them were shot. She came back riding a "titanic starborne beast," a Leviathan, and led the Colossus through a wormhole to Phaaze. Her disciples turned the star-beast into a dreadnought with cybernetic weapons built into its skull.

To command the living planet she wanted an Aurora Unit. The Colossus crippled the G.F.S. Valhalla, a Federation battleship "crewed with green recruits on a training mission"; raiders extracted Aurora Unit 313 and jettisoned the crew into space, and the Unit's last log ended "...Darkness coming." Implanted in Phaaze's core, Aurora Unit 313 gave Dark Samus control over the planet's biological functions and the power to launch Leviathan Seeds. The first struck the Pirate Homeworld and corrupted its Pirates into fresh disciples. Around the same time a Phazon-based virus, which the Federation believed to be of Space Pirate origin, began corrupting and disabling Aurora Units, and Fleet Admiral Castor Dane summoned Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda to the G.F.S. Olympus to deliver a vaccine.

Then came three strikes, each a Seed with an armada: Elysia, the Fuel Gel source of Bryyo and the military base at Norion. "Dark Samus herself will lead the attack on Norion." Samus and Rundas restored the generators of the planetary defense cannon, and Rundas caught Samus after her fall with Meta Ridley. At the cannon's control tower Dark Samus descended on the four hunters and knocked them unconscious with a Phazon blast. Samus alone struggled up to fire, destroying the Leviathan before impact, the only Seed ever stopped short of its target, then collapsed into a month-long coma (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption). The Pirates recorded: "Our great leader defeated Aran and her mongrel allies in battle." See also Phaaze, Leviathans, Aurora Unit 313, Norion, G.F.S. Olympus, Castor Dane, Meta Ridley.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Colossus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/G.F.S._Valhalla · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Dark_Samus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_3:_Corruption</sub>


## Phazon


### Phazon: Leviathans (Leviathan Seeds)

*Keys:* Leviathan, Leviathans, Leviathan Seed, Leviathan Seeds, Phazon Seed, Phazon Seeds, Phazon Meteor, Leviathan Infant, Infant Leviathan, Leviathan Core, Leviathan Battleship, Leviathan dreadnought


Leviathans, also called Leviathan Seeds (Phazon Seeds to the Space Pirates, a "Phazon Meteor" in Federation records), are vast living organisms born on Phaaze: sentient asteroid-like bodies of concentrated Phazon inside an armored shell. Their purpose is to spread Phazon and turn target worlds into copies of Phaaze. An infant grows inside a womb organ deep in Phaaze until it develops a Phazon core, "the Leviathan's transition into adolescence and its departure from the womb." Phaaze then launches it; the Leviathan generates its own wormholes to cross between stars and homes in on its target by instinct. "Shortly after impact, the bioform dies, leaving its armored shell to protect the Phazon core." Before dying it enthralls a large local predator, mutates it with Phazon and compels it to guard the core; "The core then begins to seep into the planet, replacing the local ecosystem with one based on Phazon." Destroying the core halts that world's corruption.

Known impacts:
- Tallon IV, roughly fifty years before Samus came: the Chozo sealed the Impact Crater beneath the Artifact Temple, and Metroid Prime gestated at its heart, absorbing Phazon from the core, until Samus destroyed it (see The Chozo of Tallon IV and the Chozo Lore prophecy, and Impact Crater).
- Aether, about fifty years before Samus came there: the impact threw the planet into Transdimensional Flux, creating Dark Aether and the Ing. Dark Aether became a prime source of Phazon until it collapsed after Samus's intervention.
- The Pirate Homeworld: Dark Samus sent a Seed so the Homeworld would become like Phaaze and yield "thousands of new disciples for her to command." Its guardian was Omega Ridley; the Pirates docked their Leviathan Battleship there.
- Bryyo and Elysia: two of the "Three Phazon Seeds" sent with armadas. On Elysia the Pirates built a massive energy shield around the fallen Leviathan and, the Elysians recorded, called it a "Seed." Bryyo's guardian was Mogenar, a Reptilicus War Golem reanimated with Phazon; Elysia's was Helios, a Phazon-enhanced machine commanding Swarmbots. The corrupted hunters Rundas and Ghor defended those worlds. Samus destroyed Bryyo's shield generators and broke Elysia's shield with a Theronian Bomb from SkyTown.
- Norion: the third Seed was aimed at the Federation base. Samus, wounded by Dark Samus, activated the planetary Energy Cannon and destroyed it in flight, the only recorded case of a Leviathan stopped before it reached a planet.

The Leviathan Battleship was a Pirate dreadnought: "We took her great star-beast and fitted it with cybernetic weapon systems... In time, we created a mighty dreadnought, and called it Leviathan," with crew quarters and command consoles in its skull. Samus commandeered it to open the wormhole to Phaaze. In the Genesis Chamber she tore open the Leviathan Infant's womb with the Grapple Lasso and destroyed the exposed infant with the Nova Beam. Every known Leviathan was destroyed by Samus, the last of them along with Phaaze itself.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Leviathan · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Ghost · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data</sub>


### Phazon: Phaaze

*Keys:* Phaaze, Genesis Chamber, living planet, planet of Phazon, Phazon planet, source of all Phazon, Phazon source, Phaaze's core


Phaaze is a sentient planet composed of Phazon, "the source of all Phazon in the cosmos," as the Space Pirates who reached it put it. Its atmosphere is saturated with Phazon radiation, and its native life is embodied in Phazon: Phazon Metroids, Liquid Phazon and Phaz-Ing. Phaaze is the mother of the Leviathans: "Leviathans are the children of Phaaze." Infants grow in the Genesis Chamber inside a serpentine organ that serves as a womb, feeding on Phazon until a Phazon core forms, and are then launched at worlds the planet chooses. The deepest chamber is the Sanctum.

Discovery: a long-lost Chozo satellite returned data on a distant stellar object that was "in many ways, a planet - and yet also sentient," and the Chozo charged the Elysians of SkyTown, on Elysia, to keep searching. Later the Elysians traced a meteor-like object that had struck a planet back through a wormhole to that distant world, but SkyTown's Aurora Unit 217 had been disabled by a Phazon-based virus and every attempt to send the data to the Federation failed. Aurora Unit 313 aboard the G.F.S. Valhalla left a final testament warning of Phaaze and Dark Samus, ending "...Darkness coming."

Dark Samus, revived inside the Pirate warship Colossus, led the Pirates through a wormhole to Phaaze, where they implanted the stolen Aurora Unit 313 into the planet. Through it she gained command of Phaaze: "Dark Samus can now command the living world to send forth Phazon Seeds!" The unit became "symbiotically connected to the planet Phaaze" and "fused with the bioessence of Dark Samus."

Phaaze no longer exists. At the end of the Phazon crisis Samus reached it through a wormhole opened by the Leviathan Battleship captured from the Pirates and destroyed Aurora Unit 313 after Dark Samus merged with it; that "stopped the flow of Phazon throughout the galaxy," and Phaaze was wiped from the galaxy (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption; see The assault on Phaaze and the end of Phazon).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SkyTown_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aurora_Unit_217 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Leviathan · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Genesis_Chamber · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sanctum_(Phaaze)</sub>


### Phazon: Phazon

*Keys:* Phazon, Great Poison, Phazite, Orange Phazon, Blue Phazon, Phazon ore, Phazon sickness, Phazon radiation, Phazon energy


Phazon is a luminous blue substance, intensely radioactive and mutagenic, that originates from the living planet Phaaze and is carried to other worlds by the Leviathans. The Chozo of Tallon IV named it the Great Poison, "an otherworldly pestilence that infests the land." Space Pirate Science Team codified it as "a V-index mutagen" and a new energy source. Galactic Federation medical records call it "a highly radioactive ore with extreme mutagenic properties" of extragalactic origin, a far more potent fuel than Fuel Gel.

Properties:
- Energy. Phazon powers Pirate starships, the Federation's Phazon Enhancement Devices, and the state called Hypermode.
- Mutation. Exposure remakes living tissue. Project Helix found that Phazon infusion "degenerated brain tissue even as it augmented muscle mass," and Pirate simulations of the Phazon-dependent Omega Pirate found that "No subject lived longer than 2 deca-cycles."
- Madness. Phazon "often affects the brains of sentient beings, causing erratic, destructive behavior"; the Federation calls the condition Phazon sickness, and Pirate miners on Tallon IV suffered Phazon-induced madness.
- Will. Phazon is not inert. Phaaze is a sentient planet; Liquid Phazon is a "semisentient" glob; and beings saturated with Phazon, even machines such as Mogenar and Helios, turn to defend the Phazon source and serve Dark Samus, who is herself made of Phazon.
- Ecology. A Leviathan's Phazon core "seeps into the planet, replacing the local ecosystem with one based on Phazon"; left unchecked, a world becomes another Phaaze. Samus's own scans projected that at its current rate of decay Tallon IV would become a barren Class XIII wasteland in about 25 years.

Forms: ordinary blue Phazon; Orange Phazon in the Impact Crater of Tallon IV, "at least 86% greater" in mutagenic potency and fatal to unshielded bioforms within moments; Phazite, a hardened form the Pirates mined on their Leviathan-infected Homeworld for armor plating and door shielding, which only the Nova Beam penetrates; and Liquid Phazon, a living puddle that spawns Phazon Grubs.

History: a Leviathan struck Tallon IV roughly fifty years before Samus's first visit, and the Pirates later mined its Phazon; the Leviathan that struck Aether created Dark Aether, whose rich Phazon drew Pirate extraction raids and fed Dark Samus; Phazon ore recovered by the G.F.S. Anhur from a Pirate mining facility led the Federation to build the Phazon Enhancement Device. Dark Samus later seeded the Pirate Homeworld, Bryyo and Elysia and launched a Leviathan at Norion.

Samus Aran has carried Phazon twice and survived both times: her Power Suit became the Phazon Suit on Tallon IV, and Dark Samus's Phazon blast on Norion corrupted her body, a corruption she fought until she destroyed Phaaze. Destroying Aurora Unit 313 stopped the flow of Phazon through the galaxy and purged the Phazon from her; with Phaaze gone, the threat of Phazon corruption disappeared.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Chozo_Lore · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Orange_Phazon</sub>


### Phazon: Phazon corruption and Hypermode

*Keys:* Hypermode, Hyper-mode, Hyper mode, Corrupt Hypermode, Phazon corruption, Phazon Enhancement Device, Phazon Enhancement Devices, PED, Hyper Missile, Hyper Grapple, Phazon Grenade, Phazon Grenades


Phazon corruption is the progressive takeover of a living body, or a machine, by Phazon. Exposure mutates; the mutated body begins to generate Phazon of its own; the Phazon brings power and dependence; and the host's will bends toward the Phazon source and toward Dark Samus, until nothing is left but a creature of Phazon. The Pirates wrote of the corrupted hunters that "Each of them bears her mark of corruption." A planet's corruption is halted by destroying its Leviathan's core; with Phaaze's destruction the threat of Phazon corruption disappeared.

Hypermode is the state in which a Phazon-saturated being channels that Phazon: "blue, ghost-like tentacles," far greater resilience and "dangerously increased offensive capabilities." Hopping Metroids, corrupted Pirates, the corrupted hunters and Dark Samus all enter it. Samus first entered Hyper-mode on Tallon IV when, wearing the Phazon Suit, she stood in the pools of Phazon that Metroid Prime expelled and fired it back as the Phazon Beam.

After the G.F.S. Anhur recovered Phazon ore, the Galactic Federation developed the Phazon Enhancement Device (PED), which "is designed to harness the energy of Phazon minerals, originally discovered on Aether." Marines carry a Phazon supply in a backpack and siphon it into their armorsuits; a Marine battalion in the Norion system tested it, and "no marines have displayed signs of 'Phazon sickness.'" After Dark Samus's Phazon blast on Norion left Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda producing Phazon in their own bodies, Federation medics, seeing no ill effects, fitted all four with PEDs. Samus's PED Suit draws on her internal Phazon: she commits one Energy Tank to enter Hypermode and fire the Hyper Beam, and later the Hyper Ball, Hyper Missile and Hyper Grapple; Hypermode ends when the Phazon is spent.

The danger: overuse sends Phazon tentacles crawling across the user's vision, and prolonged use can lead to total corruption. When Phazon builds past control, as when a Pirate Phazon Grenade strikes, the user falls into Corrupt Hypermode and must vent the excess through Hyper weapons, or Phazon overtakes them entirely. Rundas and Ghor overused their devices and Gandrayda was corrupted on the Pirate Homeworld; all three fell to Dark Samus. Ghor taunted Samus, "Don't you feel the power? Soon everything will be corrupted. Including you." On Phaaze her own corruption was driven to the brink of totality and she was locked in permanent Hypermode, bleeding off Phazon by forcing it into Phaz-Ing with the Hyper Grapple.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hypermode · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Enhancement_Device · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/PED_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Federation_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Grenade</sub>


### Phazon: Phazon Metroids and Phazon-corrupted creatures

*Keys:* Phazon Metroid, Phazon Metroids, Hopping Metroid, Hopping Metroids, Metroid Hatcher, Metroid Hatchers, Miniroid, Miniroids, Phaz-Ing, Phaz-Ings, Phazon Grubs, Liquid Phazon


Phazon remakes whatever it touches, and the Metroid takes to it best of all: Metroid Prime itself was a Phazon-mutated Metroid, and Phazon exposure on Tallon IV produced the Hunter and Fission Metroids.

Phazon Metroids (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption) are the mature form of this corruption: "Heavy exposure to Phazon energy has mutated this strain of Tallon Metroid." They can "shift into a phased state," passing through walls and slipping attacks; they fire bursts of Phazon and drain life energy; great cold holds them still, and a single Nova Beam shot destroys the core. The Pirates kept them in tanks in Eastern SkyTown on Elysia and bred them in the Metroid Creche of the Pirate Homeworld, but their natural home is Phaaze. Their young are Miniroids, "tiny and weak," which "travel in a flock for protection"; "Those that survive will grow quickly." Related strains:
- Hopping Metroid: "Radical mutation has removed the ability to fly from this strain of Metroid, but its vulnerability to cold remains"; its legs carry blades of Phazite and it can enter Hypermode.
- Metroid Hatcher: a floating head with four energy-draining tentacles and a Phazite-like shell that spawns Phazon Metroids from its mouth. It dies when all its tentacles are ripped away; a Nova Beam shot aimed through the X-Ray Visor also kills it outright.

Life made wholly of Phazon: Liquid Phazon, a "semisentient glob of Phazon" that consumes Phazon in any form and spawns Phazon Grubs, whose bite spreads corruption; Phaz-Ing, "wall-crawling shape-shifters" that stay liquid until they attack and tear apart under the Grapple Lasso; Phaazoids, floating Phazon that only Phazon can harm and that split in two when destroyed.

Corrupted natives and machines: the Turned, Chozo Ghosts of Tallon IV "utterly corrupted by the Great Poison"; Phazon-enhanced fauna such as Phazon Shriekbats; the Reptilicus War Golem Mogenar and the Elysian machine Helios, enthralled as Leviathan guardians; the Elite Pirates and Omega Pirate of Project Helix; Ridley himself, "revived and regenerated through Phazon exposure" after his defeat on Norion and reborn as Omega Ridley to guard the Pirate Homeworld's Leviathan; and the bounty hunters Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, whose Phazon Dark Samus absorbed as each died. Samus, herself Phazon-corrupted after Norion, hunted these creatures while fighting the same change in her own body.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Miniroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Hopping_Metroid · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatcher · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Liquid_Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Grub</sub>


### Phazon: Pirate Phazon operations and the Federation's PED program

*Keys:* Phazon mining, Phazon research, Phazon experiments, Phazon weapons, Phazon-powered, Vertigo strain, Phazon Elite, PED program, Phazon virus, Phazon harvesting


The Pirates (Space Pirate projects and Phazon research): Science Team codified Phazon as "a V-index mutagen" on Tallon IV and began mining near the Impact Crater, where it was most concentrated. The miners went mad. Experiments followed on Metroids, which yielded the Phazon-mutated Hunter and Fission strains, on Project Titan (Thardus, suspended indefinitely after deadly security breaches) and on Project Helix, which infused Pirate DNA with Phazon. Early Helix subjects suffered degenerated brain tissue; the Vertigo strain produced the Elite Pirates, the short-lived Phazon Elite and the Omega Pirate, whose "potential overdependence on Phazon" worried even Command. On Aether, Pirates ran Phazon extraction raids on Dark Aether, and Dark Samus stripped their stores: "She does not seek our destruction, but our Phazon." Aboard the warship Colossus they unknowingly carried Dark Samus's remains in their Phazon stores, and the survivors of her revival became her disciples (see The return – Colossus, Phaaze and the Norion attack). Phazon-powered Pirate starships and superior numbers overwhelmed the G.F.S. Valhalla, and the Pirates carried off its Aurora Unit 313. They created a Phazon-based virus that corrupted Aurora Units, among them SkyTown's Aurora Unit 217, which Samus later cured. They built the Leviathan dreadnought, mined Phazite for armor plating and door shields, bred Phazon Metroids in the Metroid Creche, entered Hypermode in battle and threw Phazon Grenades.

The Galactic Federation: the G.F.S. Anhur recovered Phazon ore while dismantling a Pirate mining facility, and from it the Federation developed the Phazon Enhancement Device (see Phazon corruption and Hypermode). The Federation summoned Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda to clear a virus from its Aurora Units; after Norion it fitted the four Phazon-generating hunters with PEDs and sent them against the Leviathans, and Demolition Troopers helped breach the Pirate Homeworld's Seed. The program ended with Phaaze, whose destruction stopped the flow of Phazon.

Samus accepted the Federation's device on its medics' judgment and used it to the edge of total corruption. Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda, given the same device, did not come back.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Space_Pirate_Data · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Project_Helix · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Omega_Pirate · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Thardus · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon</sub>


### Phazon: Samus and Phazon (corruption and purge)

*Keys:* Samus's corruption, corrupted Samus, Samus corrupted, her corruption, Phazon purge, purged of Phazon, Phazon in her body, Phazon in her veins, Phazon poisoning, permanent Hypermode, near total corruption


Samus Aran's armor and then her body have been corrupted by Phazon, and she survived both times; none of the other corrupted hunters did.

Tallon IV: when the Omega Pirate collapsed onto her and disintegrated, its Phazon soaked into her armor: "The Power Suit has been corrupted by viral exposure, turning it into the Phazon Suit." The jet-black suit shrugged off ordinary blue Phazon but not the Orange Phazon of the Impact Crater. Standing in the pools Metroid Prime expelled, she entered Hyper-mode and fired the Phazon Beam. The dying creature tore the Phazon Suit from her body; she escaped in her Gravity Suit, free of Phazon, while the Phazon merged with the stolen suit and a hand rose from it: Dark Samus.

Norion: Dark Samus's Phazon blast knocked Samus, Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda unconscious; Samus stayed awake long enough to fire the Energy Cannon that destroyed the incoming Leviathan, then lay in a coma for a month. She woke aboard the G.F.S. Olympus to learn her body now generated Phazon on its own. Federation medics, seeing no ill effects, fitted her with the PED Suit.

The crisis: each Leviathan she destroyed deepened her corruption, her armor darkened, and control in Hypermode grew harder to keep. She watched Dark Samus take the Phazon from each fallen comrade: Rundas, impaled on his own ice on Bryyo; Ghor on Elysia; and Gandrayda on the Pirate Homeworld, who in her death throes shifted into a Pirate, Ghor, Rundas and Samus's own form before being absorbed.

Phaaze: on landing, her corruption surged to the brink of totality. Her Gunship refused her; its scanners no longer recognized Samus through "near total corruption." The PED Suit's emergency vent dumped her energy reserves to slow the change and left her in permanent Hypermode. She stayed herself by forcing her own Phazon into Phaz-Ing through the Hyper Grapple. Total saturation would have finished her.

The purge: destroying Aurora Unit 313 purged the Phazon from her, and with Phaaze eliminated the threat of Phazon corruption disappeared. She reported the mission accomplished from her Gunship, then went to Elysia to mourn Rundas, Ghor and Gandrayda (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Samus_Aran · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Suit · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_(game) · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Orange_Phazon · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phazon_Beam · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Phaaze</sub>


## Federation Force


### Federation Force: Bermuda System (Excelcion, Bion, Talvania)

*Keys:* Bermuda System, Bermuda star system, Bermuda planets, Excelcion, Bion, Talvania, Ice Titan, Ice Titans


The Bermuda System is a remote star system, a former Galactic Federation territory of three major planets: Excelcion, Bion and Talvania. The Federation once worked the system and then withdrew; after the Phazon crisis it sent the Federation Force back to restore its bases and survey the ruins, and the Force found the Space Pirates had moved in. During the Bermuda System incident each planet held one of the cloaking generators hiding the Pirate warship Doomseye.

Excelcion is a frozen wasteland rich in natural resources, once mined by the Federation. About twenty years before the incident a giant meteorite impact plunged it into deep cold, and a living nest in the old base that spawned hostile hoppers without end forced the Federation out. Its installations include a biological research institute and an underground lake where a survey team was attacked by aquatic creatures the Pirates had enlarged and brought under control with their beam. Native Ice Titans, huge quadrupeds placid until provoked, were captured by the Force for ecological study. The flagship G.F.S. Aegis held orbit above it.

Bion is a scorching desert world with volcanic lava regions. It was home to an ancient civilization, long extinct and never named, whose people worshipped spherical objects called Orbs and are said to have developed technology for enlarging living things. Their ruins are guarded by golem-like sentinels that curl into spheres and teleport, and by a three-cored artificial life form in a lava shell that causes tremors underground. Here the Force learned that the Pirates had acquired enlarging technology, and a giant creature that a Force squad could not stop fell to a missile barrage from Samus Aran's gunship.

Talvania is a gas-shrouded world of abandoned industry, once home to a highly advanced machine civilization thought to have left for another planet several centuries ago. Its power plant and ancient automated defenses still run. The Pirates reactivated its dormant facilities, built a missile factory, launched ballistic missiles at the Aegis, and guarded a transfer device behind an automated defense system.

Samus knows all three worlds from her intelligence work during the incident, when she destroyed all but one of the Pirate missile bases alone before the Pirates captured her.

### Federation Force: General Alex Miles

*Keys:* Miles, Alex Miles, General Miles, General Alex Miles, Commander Alex  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Alex, General, Federation Force, Aegis, Bermuda, Operation Golem, Doomseye, Excelcion


General Alex Miles is the Galactic Federation officer who commands the Federation Force and directs Operation Golem, the Federation's secret program of piloted Mechs. He is also addressed simply as Commander Alex. His uniform follows the same design as the one worn by Adam Malkovich, Samus Aran's former commanding officer, topped by a helmet reserved for commanders.

During the Bermuda System incident (Metroid Prime: Federation Force) Miles ran the campaign from the flagship G.F.S. Aegis in orbit above Excelcion, issuing the orders and briefings for the Force's operations on Excelcion, Bion and Talvania. Under his command the assignment began as base reconstruction and archaeological survey and became open war once Space Pirates were found entrenched in the system. His pilots destroyed the cloaking generators hiding the Pirate warship Doomseye, boarded it, defeated the brainwashed and enlarged Samus, and confronted the Master Brain that controlled the ship, with Samus returning to assist them. The surviving fleet then destroyed the Doomseye.

Samus took part in the operation as a bounty hunter working under a Federation contract to gather intelligence, not as one of Miles's Marines. She went silent after reporting the Doomseye's construction, was captured by the Pirates and turned against his troops, and after the battle she recovered his Marines from open space and thanked them for what they had done. See also Federation Force, Bermuda System incident and Doomseye.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/index.html · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/multiplay/index.html · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%A1%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%82%A4%E3%83%89%E3%83%97%E3%83%A9%E3%82%A4%E3%83%A0_%E3%83%95%E3%82%A7%E3%83%87%E3%83%AC%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%B3%E3%83%95%E3%82%A9%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B9 · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-the-story-so-far-everything-you-need-to-know-before-metroid-prime-4-beyond</sub>


### Federation Force: The Bermuda System incident

*Keys:* Bermuda System incident, Bermuda incident, Bermuda campaign, giant Samus, enlarged Samus, brainwashed Samus, mind-controlled Samus


The Bermuda System incident was a Galactic Federation campaign against the Space Pirates, fought after the Phazon crisis and the destruction of Phaaze (Metroid Prime 3: Corruption); it opened in Cosmic Year 20X6, per the official Japanese website. With Phazon eradicated and the weakened Pirates still at large, the Federation approved Operation Golem, a top-secret program of piloted Mechs modeled on Samus Aran's Power Suit, and formed an elite Marine unit, the Federation Force, to pilot them under General Alex Miles. Operating from the flagship G.F.S. Aegis, the Force was sent to the Bermuda System's three planets, Excelcion, Bion and Talvania, officially to rebuild abandoned Federation facilities and carry out archaeological surveys.

The Force found the Pirates entrenched on all three worlds: salvaging a weapon from old Federation facilities on Excelcion, digging through Bion's ancient ruins, and reawakening Talvania's dormant industry to build ballistic missiles aimed at the Aegis. The Federation hired Samus to gather intelligence. Working alone from her gunship, she destroyed all but one of the Pirate missile bases, and on Bion her ship's missile barrage brought down a giant creature that a Force squad could not. On Bion the Force learned the Pirates had acquired technology that enlarges living things; their enlarging beam also drives its targets berserk. In one operation, designated Infestation, the Force stole a Metroid egg from the Pirates. Samus then reported that the Pirates were building an enormous warship, the Doomseye, and the Federation lost contact with her.

The Force destroyed the cloaking generators hiding the Doomseye, one on each planet, and boarded the warship after it crippled the fleet and shielded itself. Inside they defeated and freed the brainwashed, enlarged Samus, destroyed the Master Brain controlling the ship with her help, and were recovered by her from open space after the surviving fleet destroyed the Doomseye (see The Doomseye).

The incident is one of the few times Samus was captured, mind-controlled and turned against Federation soldiers, and she owed her release to Marines in Mechs built on her own suit's design. The Metroid egg the Force had taken was later hatched by Sylux.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-the-story-so-far-everything-you-need-to-know-before-metroid-prime-4-beyond · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-who-is-sylux · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/index.html · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/mission/index.html · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%A1%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%82%A4%E3%83%89%E3%83%97%E3%83%A9%E3%82%A4%E3%83%A0_%E3%83%95%E3%82%A7%E3%83%87%E3%83%AC%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%B3%E3%83%95%E3%82%A9%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B9</sub>


### Federation Force: The Doomseye

*Keys:* Doomseye, Doom's Eye, Doomseye warship, Master Brain, cloaking generator, cloaking generators


The Doomseye was an enormous Space Pirate warship built in secret in the Bermuda System after the Phazon crisis and controlled by an entity called the Master Brain. It was discovered by Samus Aran, whom the Galactic Federation had hired to gather intelligence on the Pirates; her report that they were building a huge battleship was her last before the Federation lost contact with her. The ship lay hidden by cloaking technology powered by three generators, one each on Excelcion, Bion and Talvania. The Federation Force destroyed all three, which revealed the Doomseye in orbit, but the warship struck back at once, wiping out most of the Federation fleet, and enclosed itself in an impenetrable shield.

With the fleet crippled, the Force boarded the Doomseye in their Mechs. Inside they found the Pirates' captive, Samus, brainwashed, grown to giant size by the Pirates' enlarging beam and set against them. She fought in Morph Ball form with Bombs, Boost Ball, Spider Ball and Power Bombs. The Marines defeated her, which returned her to normal size, and she was buried under falling debris. Pressing deeper, they confronted the Master Brain; Samus, who had survived, returned to assist, and the Master Brain was destroyed. In the chaos the Marines were sucked out into open space before the surviving Federation ships destroyed the Doomseye, and Samus recovered them.

The Doomseye is where the Pirates held, mind-controlled and weaponized Samus (Metroid Prime: Federation Force). She owed her release to the Federation Force, and the Marines in turn owed their lives to her return. See also Bermuda System incident, General Alex Miles and Federation Force.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-the-story-so-far-everything-you-need-to-know-before-metroid-prime-4-beyond · https://metroid.fandom.com/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://ja.wikipedia.org/wiki/%E3%83%A1%E3%83%88%E3%83%AD%E3%82%A4%E3%83%89%E3%83%97%E3%83%A9%E3%82%A4%E3%83%A0_%E3%83%95%E3%82%A7%E3%83%87%E3%83%AC%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B7%E3%83%A7%E3%83%B3%E3%83%95%E3%82%A9%E3%83%BC%E3%82%B9 · https://www.nintendo.co.jp/3ds/bcaj/mission/index.html</sub>


### Federation Force: The Federation Force and their Mechs

*Keys:* Federation Force, Operation Golem, Golem Mech, Mech suit, Mech suits, Mechsuit, Mechsuits, G.F.S. Aegis, GFS Aegis, Blast Ball, Lone Wolf MOD, AI Combat Drones


The Federation Force is an elite unit of Galactic Federation Marines raised under Operation Golem, the Federation's top-secret program to secure an edge over the Space Pirates after the Phazon crisis. Its specially trained pilots deploy in squads of up to four under General Alex Miles, operating from the flagship G.F.S. Aegis. Its first campaign was the Bermuda System incident, where an assignment to rebuild Federation facilities and survey ruins turned into open war that ended aboard the Pirate warship Doomseye.

The Mech is the Force's prototype battle armor, designed after Samus Aran's Power Suit: a piloted suit about 4.3 meters tall and weighing 3.2 tonnes, with a walking speed of 27.2 km/h, which the pilot enters through the back of the torso (per the official Japanese website). Only Federation Force members are cleared to pilot one. Standard systems are an arm cannon that fires beam and charged shots, target lock-on, a sidestep dodge, and a jump with a short hover.

Before each sortie a pilot fits supplementary equipment within a weight limit:
- homing Missiles and area-blast Super Missiles
- incendiary and freezing shots; frozen targets cannot move or fire and take double damage
- a slowing beam, and a scanning shot that exposes weak points
- the Shock Shot, which shorts out mechanical defenses
- Repair Capsules that restore a Mech's energy, and shield generators that raise a protective dome
- decoys and mines

Performance chips called MODs tune the machine further. The Lone Wolf MOD, barred from squad deployment, doubles beam damage and halves damage taken for a pilot operating alone, who may also bring up to three AI Combat Drones, small autonomous flying units. Squad pilots divide duties among attackers, assisters, healers and all-rounders. Blast Ball is a three-on-three sport played in Mechs, in which teams drive a giant ball into the opposing goal with beam fire.

Samus did not pilot a Mech during the Bermuda campaign; she worked alongside the Force as a hired bounty hunter in her own gunship. The machines are still bound up with her: their design descends from her Power Suit, and Force pilots in Mechs defeated her when the Pirates brainwashed and enlarged her aboard the Doomseye, before she recovered them from open space.

### Federation Force: The Metroid egg and Sylux's break-in

*Keys:* Metroid egg, Metroid eggs, stolen egg, stasis tube, Metroid larva, Sylux hatched  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* Sylux, Federation Force, Bermuda, Doomseye, research station, Delano 7, Tanamaar, Infestation


During the Bermuda System incident the Federation Force stole a Metroid egg from the Space Pirates in an operation designated Infestation. The egg was afterward held in a stasis tube at a Federation research station. Once the incident was over, an armored intruder broke into the station, hacked the stasis tube with an arm-mounted device and fired a beam into the egg, which hatched at once into a Metroid larva. The intruder was never clearly seen, but a shoulder spike glimpsed as he stood over the hatchling marked him as Sylux, the hunter whose ship, the Delano 7, had been seen tailing Samus Aran's gunship after the Phazon crisis.

Neither Samus nor the Force was present for the break-in, which Nintendo's official account dates to Cosmic Year 20X6. Per that account, Sylux, who hates Samus and the Galactic Federation, exploited the hatchling's imprinting to breed a strain of Metroids able to merge with and control a host; leading Space Pirates under their control, he later attacked a Federation facility on Tanamaar, a clash that ended with Samus carried to the planet Viewros (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

For Samus, the galaxy's foremost Metroid exterminator, the break-in matters on two counts. It put a living Metroid, the species she has spent her career destroying, into the hands of Sylux, her rival since the Alimbic Cluster. And the egg came out of the same campaign in which the Pirates had captured her and turned her against the Federation Force. See also Sylux, Federation Force and Bermuda System incident.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime:_Federation_Force · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-who-is-sylux · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-the-story-so-far-everything-you-need-to-know-before-metroid-prime-4-beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Delano_7 · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Metroid_characters</sub>


## Prime 4


### Prime 4: Aberax and Sylux's Metroids

*Keys:* Aberax, Sylux Metroid, Sylux Metroids, Sylux's Metroids, fused Metroid, Metroid-fused, fused with a Metroid, possessed by a Metroid, mind-controlling Metroids, mind-controlled Pirates, Metroid strain


Sylux's Metroids are a strain he bred from the Metroid hatchling he took from a Federation research station after the Bermuda System incident; his theft of a Metroid from a Federation base is dated to Cosmic Year 20X6. Exploiting the creature's imprinting, he cloned it and cultivated copies with a new ability: they fuse with a host organism and take control of its mind. With them he possessed Space Pirate officers, seized command of their army, and turned the Pirates against Galactic Federation research facilities in Cosmic Year 20X9 (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Federation intelligence already suspected that the raiders were being controlled by Metroids able to merge with other life-forms.

The Aberax are unusually large Space Pirates with tough, beetle-like exoskeletons, often used in command roles; they are nearly immune to beams and explosives and can deploy a barrier. An Aberax was the first such host Samus Aran met. The fused Metroid took over its mind and grew up to four beam-projecting lenses in its chest; only destroying every lens forced it to vent its core energy. It stormed the hangar of the UTO Research Center on Tanamaar as the artifact was being loaded for evacuation, and Samus destroyed it moments before Sylux himself arrived with more Pirates and Metroids.

On Viewros Sylux pushed the technique further, infusing his Metroids into the Lamorn Guardians of the Master Teleporter Keys. Carvex, Xelios, Keratos and Phenoros each lost the ability to communicate and attacked everything near them; the fusion left exposed, vulnerable tissue, and in Phenoros's case Samus had to pass down the creature's throat to reach the Metroid lodged deep inside it. For Samus, who has fought Metroids since Zebes and SR388 and once carried the baby Metroid that saved her life, the strain was a new perversion of a species she had already seen the Space Pirates and the Galactic Federation try to turn into weapons.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aberax · http://www.nintendoworldreport.com/feature/73864/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-timeline-placement-analysis · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/</sub>


### Prime 4: Grievers and the Guardians of the Keys

*Keys:* Griever, Grievers, Carvex, Xelios, Keratos, Phenoros, Gravinax, Key Guardian, Key Guardians, Sanctum of the Guardians, Snow Wolf, Snow Wolves


Grievers are the feral monsters that overran Viewros. They are not native beasts but transformed Lamorn: when the Lamorn showered their world with Green Energy to revive it, the energy reacted with the psychic crystals implanted in their people and twisted them into savage, mindless hunters. The Lamorn called this the Great Tragedy. Griever packs hunted the uncorrupted survivors, and every attempt to cure them in the Ice Belt laboratories failed (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

Varieties:
- Jungle Griever (Fury Green): thin-skinned pack hunters that leap through the trees.
- Sand Griever (Sol Valley): ambushers that bury themselves in the dunes.
- Frostburn Griever (Ice Belt): pale specimens revived from the Lamorn cryopods, cold-resistant but weak to heat.
- Maul Griever and Maul Griever Brute (Great Mines): burrowers that hunt by sound and attack in waves; loud noises draw hordes. Brutes, swollen by excess Green Energy, spit mucus that hardens into barriers.
- Omega Griever: the largest and strongest Griever, apex predator of the Great Mines. Green Energy keratinized much of its hide into armor that turns weapons aside everywhere but its unhardened patches. By the time Samus arrived, Gravinax, the Guardian of the mines' Master Teleporter Key, was gone, and the Omega Griever held the key in its place.

The Guardians of the keys were creatures and minds the Lamorn trusted; Sylux infused four of them with his Metroids:
- Carvex (Fury Green): a giant carnivorous plant that once communed telepathically with the priests, now driven to attack everything.
- Xelios (Volt Forge): the organic-cell artificial intelligence that managed vehicle manufacturing until the fusion turned it into a battle AI.
- Keratos (Ice Belt): a one-of-a-kind armored giant with an iceberg-like shell, once intelligent enough to communicate its will to the priests, its mind and body irreversibly altered.
- Phenoros (Flare Pool): a huge mineral life-form in the lava lake of Magdrahda that feeds on Green Energy.

Other dangers include Varmis, a giant sand-swimming crustacean of Sol Valley grown enormous on Green Energy, Snow Wolves that hunt in ranked packs in the Ice Belt, and the Behemoth of Flare Pool, an armored arthropod whose four eyes are its only weakness. Samus Aran destroyed each fused Guardian and the Omega Griever to claim the five keys. The Sanctum of the Guardians in Fury Green preserves holographic records of the Guardians, among them Gravinax, the one Guardian she never faced.


<sub>Sources: https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/boss-guide/ · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


### Prime 4: Myles MacKenzie and the stranded Federation squad

*Keys:* Myles MacKenzie, MacKenzie, Reger Tokabi, Tokabi, Ezra Duke, Sergeant Duke, Nora Armstrong, Private Armstrong, VUE-995, Betsy mech, MK-99


When the Lamorn artifact on Tanamaar activated, five members of the Galactic Federation contingent at the UTO Research Center were hurled to Viewros alongside Samus Aran (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Samus found them one by one.

- Myles MacKenzie: a Federation engineering specialist who repairs vehicles and writes weapons software. Timid, talkative even when alone and restlessly curious, he has a strong moral compass. Samus freed him from his wrecked transport in Fury Green; he handed her the Missile Launcher stored aboard, restored comms and asked, "What do you say? Teammates?" He set up the Base Camp in Fury Green and rebuilt the shattered MK-99 mech Betsy, a model he knew well, from salvaged parts to batter down the Chrono Tower's barrier.
- Corporal Reger Tokabi: a Federation sniper, a solitary hunter from a remote world who enlisted in search of a stable life. He is never without an arrowhead-shaped talisman he credits with his survival, and his family's generations-old worship of the god Sollan sits uneasily with his missions. He camped in Sol Valley and spoke with Samus about his upbringing and the sacred feel of the planet.
- Sergeant Ezra Duke: a veteran of several Space Pirate engagements, stubborn and prone to clashing with superiors, which cost him promotion and left him leading a single squad at the UTO Research Center. He is sincere, compassionate and dependable in a fight. Samus freed him from a generator in Flare Pool.
- Private Nora Armstrong: bright and proactive, with an intuitive command of structures and vehicles. She idolizes Samus for her famously unshakable demeanor, and her enthusiastic outbursts about Samus annoy her superiors. Samus met her on a tram in Flare Pool; "Finally, we get to go home," she said when the way out seemed open.
- VUE-995: a Federation combat android built to replace human pilots of fighters, armored vehicles and battle mechs. It has no emotions but produces pseudo-emotions at times, and apologized for wrecking Betsy when the mech punched through the barrier.

All five fought beside Samus against Sylux at the Chrono Tower's Pinnacle. When he returned and crippled the Master Teleporter they held him down so that Samus could escape; Tokabi threw her his tag as she went. Samus left them on Viewros and hung Tokabi's tag on the tree she grew from the Memory Fruit in their memory. Whether any of them survived is unknown.


<sub>Sources: https://www.nintendolife.com/news/2025/11/metroid-prime-4-beyond-meet-the-team · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://shinesparkers.net/metroid-prime-4-beyond-transcript/ · https://tech.yahoo.com/gaming/articles/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-ending-181400714.html · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


### Prime 4: Sylux

*Keys:* Sylux, Delano 7, Shock Coil, Lockjaw, Psy-bot Mimic, Psy-bot Mimics, Sylux decoy, Sylux decoys, Cylosis, Sylux's past, Sylux backstory


Sylux is a bounty hunter from the planet Cylosis who wears a blue armored suit and excels at stealth, tracking and marksmanship; he is almost certainly human, though early records listed his species as unknown. He harbors an intense hatred for the Galactic Federation and for Samus Aran, whom he despises for serving it (per the Metroid Prime Hunters manual).

Equipment:
- Shock Coil: banned technology that fires charged blasts of high-density neutrinos as a continuous beam of blue lightning, draining an opponent's energy into Sylux.
- Lockjaw: his alternate form, two tetracarbon ceramic-alloy blades joined by an energy thread that lays bombs linked by explosive trip wires; on Viewros it also rammed Samus to tear an Energy Tank from her.
- Delano 7: his blue Delta-class gunship, armed with an automatic cannon.

Career:
- Alimbic Cluster incident (Metroid Prime Hunters): he followed Samus to the Alimbic Cluster seeking the ultimate power and first crossed her path at the Vesper Defense Outpost. She freed him from Gorea, yet he fled with his hatred intact.
- After the Phazon crisis, Delano 7 was seen shadowing Samus's gunship as she departed.
- After the Bermuda System incident, in Cosmic Year 20X6, he broke into a Federation research station and hatched the Metroid egg the Federation Force had recovered. He has long run covert operations that include spying on Samus.

The Viewros incident: exploiting the hatchling's imprinting, Sylux cloned it and bred a strain that merges with and controls other organisms. With these he seized the minds of Space Pirate officers, took command of their army, enhanced his battlesuit with the latest nanosuit and raided Federation research centers, ending with the attack on Tanamaar, where his duel with Samus set off the Lamorn artifact that hurled both of them to Viewros. Gravely injured, he arrived by chance inside the Chrono Tower, slipped into a Lamorn healing pod and synchronized with the tower, learning of the Master Teleporter and its five keys. From there he sent Psy-bots disguised in his likeness, indistinguishable from him until destroyed, to attack Samus and steal her keys, infused his Metroids into the keys' Guardians and sealed the tower behind a barrier. His consciousness briefly linked with Samus's, and she relived his memories as flashbacks. At the Pinnacle he rose from the pod with tentacle-like nano-arms and Lamorn energy, dragged her through a dimensional rift and lost to her in the void beyond, where he could even conjure a black hole. He returned as she activated the Master Teleporter and damaged it; her five Federation companions held him down while she escaped. He was left on Viewros, his fate unknown.

His past: Sylux was a Federation officer in command of a platoon fighting the Space Pirates. Ordered to hold position until Samus arrived, he disobeyed ("Get me that weapon") to seize a Pirate superweapon. It annihilated his platoon and the Federation mechs with it; he alone survived, barely. Samus arrived in her gunship, destroyed the weapon with ease and offered him her hand, which he slapped away. Blaming her and the Federation, he deserted with stolen prototype gear and became a bounty hunter bent on revenge. To Samus he has become an unavoidable enemy.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Sylux · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Delano_7 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Shock_Coil · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Lockjaw · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylux · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond</sub>


### Prime 4: Tanamaar and the UTO Research Center

*Keys:* Tanamaar, planet Tanamaar, Desolan, Desolan System, UTO Research Center, UTO center, UTO facility, research center attack


Tanamaar is a planet in the Desolan System occupied by the Galactic Federation and the site of the UTO Research Center. In Cosmic Year 20X9 Federation researchers there unearthed an alien artifact that their analysis could identify only as some kind of device. It was in truth a Lamorn return teleporter: one of the twelve Lamorn who left Viewros to search for a prophesied Chosen One had died on Tanamaar, and the Federation had found what it carried.

Sylux and his Space Pirates, who had been raiding Federation research facilities with mind-controlling Metroids, struck the UTO Research Center while Samus Aran was on reconnaissance nearby, and Federation HQ sent her to secure the artifact (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Her security ID gave her full access, and a trooper sent her off with "Go get 'em, Samus." Command ordered the artifact moved to the hangar and loaded onto a transport while Pirates fought the garrison through the facility. In the hangar Samus destroyed an Aberax, a giant command-rank Space Pirate fused with a Metroid, and then faced Sylux himself. His fire damaged the artifact; it activated and enveloped the whole center in an energy burst. Samus, Sylux, the engineer Myles MacKenzie, the soldiers Reger Tokabi, Ezra Duke and Nora Armstrong, the android VUE-995, the Federation war mech Betsy and chunks of the facility were all carried off to Viewros. Betsy is a Galactic Federation Heavyweight-Class Golem, an MK-99 bipedal manned war mech.

Debris from the UTO Research Center lies scattered across Viewros, and its salvaged mech parts and power cores became the raw material of the group's escape. The barren world where the Master Teleporter finally delivered Samus has not been identified.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Planet_Tanamaar · https://shinesparkers.net/metroid-prime-4-beyond-transcript/ · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://www.nintendolife.com/guides/metroid-prime-4-beyond-galactic-federation-uto-research-center-guide · https://www.noobfeed.com/news/metroid-prime-4-beyonds-ending-sparks-new-mysteries</sub>


### Prime 4: The Chrono Tower and the Master Teleporter

*Keys:* Chrono Tower, Master Teleporter, Master Teleporter Key, Master Teleporter Keys, Teleporter Keys, Nexus of Hope, Psy-bot, Psy-bots, Psy-bot Soldier, Psy-bot Soldiers, Lamorn teleporter, return teleporter


The Chrono Tower is a vast tooth-shaped Lamorn structure floating above the desert of Sol Valley on Viewros. It served as the trial ground where a Chosen One would be certified, and its summit, the Pinnacle, houses the Master Teleporter together with Lamorn sleeping and healing pods. After the Great Tragedy the surviving Lamorn converted their Psy-bot servitor machines into anti-Griever soldiers that defend the tower; they travel by shuttle or are launched through the air from the tower itself. Its halls include the Statuary Hall and the Nexus of Hope.

The Master Teleporter grew from the priest Shei's insight into the structure of space and time and was meant to carry the Lamorn legacy to a new world. Lekhash devised its sealing mechanism and the five Master Teleporter Keys, and it stays sealed until all five are set. The Lamorn entrusted the keys to Guardians in the regions of Viewros and hid them in a pocket dimension. The last priest, Chatoya, prepared the Sacred Shrines, single-use teleporters each bound to one Guardian, which lead to its key; a shrine appears when its Guardian recognizes the Chosen One or dies. The twelve Lamorn who went searching for the Chosen One left through the Master Teleporter, each carrying a smaller return teleporter such as the artifact unearthed on Tanamaar.

Samus Aran woke inside the tower after the Tanamaar explosion (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). A Guardian mechanoid guided her to the Psychic Crystal, and at the Pinnacle a statue played the recording of Chatoyant Vooloon that named her the Chosen One and told her of the keys; a teleportation orb then carried her to Fury Green. Sylux, healing in one of the pods and synchronized with the tower, raised a barrier around it and sent Psy-bots disguised in his likeness against her. With the keys gathered and the barrier broken by the Federation mech Betsy, Samus and her companions returned; at the Nexus of Hope a statue delivered Chatoya's final message, and at the Pinnacle Sylux attacked. After his defeat Samus set the keys and activated the Master Teleporter. Sylux returned and damaged it; it could hold open only briefly and worked only for her. While her five Federation companions pinned Sylux, Samus stepped through, the only one of the castaways to leave Viewros.


<sub>Sources: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-technology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/ · https://www.ludo.guide/guide/metroid-prime-4-beyond/finale-a-battle-of-wills-chrono-tower</sub>


### Prime 4: The Lamorn

*Keys:* Lamorn, Lamorns, Chatoyant Vooloon, Chatoya, Vooloon, Great Tragedy, Green Energy, Memory Fruit, Legacy Suit, Viewros Crystal, Lamorn priest, Lamorn priests


The Lamorn were a peaceful, graceful and deeply spiritual species of high intelligence that inhabited Viewros and are now extinct. They communicated telepathically, and Samus Aran could understand their recorded messages. A priesthood stood at the center of their culture. When they first arrived on Viewros they planted the Sacred Tree, which holds the memories and knowledge of their whole people; Lamorn prayed at the altar of the Temple of Memory in Fury Green and their memories passed into the tree, which bears them as Memory Fruit.

History: in their Machine Era the Lamorn built the Great Mines, the Volt Forge factory, hover shuttles, Scout Bots and the Vi-O-La, until resources ran short. The discovery of the Viewros Crystal in the mines began the Psychic Era: higher-ranking Lamorn bore a crystal in the forehead that granted psychic and telepathic power, the rest could receive artificial crystals, and the priests were the masters of these powers. Revered priests included Thesarh, the first priest, who communed with the Sacred Tree and founded the Temple of Memory; Shei, the only female priest, whose grasp of space and time led to the teleporter; Lekhash, the sculptor-mathematician whose equations became psychic objects, including the sealing mechanism of the Master Teleporter and its keys; and Vahkuun, inventor of the Mote-operated Psychic Lock and builder of the shrines of Sol Valley.

The Great Tragedy: seeking to restore a planet damaged by their industry, the Lamorn extracted Green Energy, a seemingly limitless fuel, from the magma of Flare Pool and fired it over Sol Valley in showers that revived flora and fauna. Overexposure reacted with the implanted crystals and twisted those Lamorn into the feral Grievers, who hunted the rest. Even then the survivors could not bring themselves to turn their war machines on the Grievers, who had been their own people. Under the priest Chatoya they ran laboratories in the Ice Belt trying to reverse the change; every attempt failed, and the researcher Tahrgun's last log reads, "I am Tahrgun. And I am utterly defeated."

The final thirteen Lamorn withdrew to the Chrono Tower. Twelve left through the Master Teleporter, each carrying a small return teleporter, to search other worlds for a prophesied Chosen One, and each died where they landed; one came to rest on Tanamaar, where the Galactic Federation later dug up its teleporter, and eleven others still lie on distant worlds. Chatoyant Vooloon, called Chatoya, stayed behind, prepared the Sacred Shrines that lead to the keys, and left recordings to set the Chosen One on the path: gather the five Master Teleporter Keys, feed Green Energy to the Memory Fruit, and carry it to a new world. The Lamorn's last gift to the Chosen One was the Legacy Suit, a white suit that wraps its wearer in a defensive barrier.

Samus fulfilled that charge. The Lamorn recordings addressed her as the Chosen One, and after escaping Viewros she planted the Memory Fruit on another world, giving up the Legacy Suit as it took root.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Lamorn · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-technology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/</sub>


### Prime 4: The Viewros incident (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond events)

*Keys:* Viewros incident, Metroid Prime 4, Prime 4, Cosmic Year 20X9, 20X9, Lamorn Chosen One, Tanamaar artifact, Lamorn artifact, stranded on Viewros, escape from Viewros


In Cosmic Year 20X9 the bounty hunter Sylux and a Space Pirate army under his command were raiding Galactic Federation research facilities. Federation intelligence believed the Pirates were controlled by a new strain of Metroid able to fuse with other life-forms and seize their minds; Sylux had bred it from a Metroid stolen from a Federation base in Cosmic Year 20X6. When the UTO Research Center on Tanamaar, in the Desolan System, came under attack, Federation HQ dispatched Samus Aran, who was nearby on reconnaissance, to secure a recently unearthed alien artifact that analysis could identify only as some kind of device (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond).

Samus fought through the facility while its staff rushed the artifact to the hangar. There a Metroid fused with an Aberax, one of the giant command-rank Space Pirates, and Samus destroyed it before Sylux broke in with more Pirates and Metroids. During their duel Sylux damaged the artifact; it activated and engulfed the center in an energy burst that flung Samus, Sylux, five Federation personnel and parts of the facility to Viewros, a planet on no known star chart, lying beyond time and space.

Samus woke inside the Chrono Tower with most of her suit's capabilities gone. A Lamorn Guardian mechanoid led her to the Psychic Crystal, which awakened psychic abilities in her, and at the tower's Pinnacle a recording of Chatoyant Vooloon, last priest of the extinct Lamorn, named her the Chosen One foretold to carry the Lamorn legacy to a new world. The Master Teleporter there could carry her off Viewros, but only once the five Master Teleporter Keys entrusted to Guardians across the planet were recovered. The Lamorn also asked her to ripen the Memory Fruit of the Sacred Tree with Green Energy gathered from crystals scattered over Viewros.

In the jungle of Fury Green Samus rescued the engineer Myles MacKenzie, who guided her over comms from a Base Camp. Riding the Lamorn vehicle Vi-O-La across the desert of Sol Valley, she took the keys of Fury Green, Volt Forge, the Ice Belt, Flare Pool and the Great Mines and found the other castaways: Corporal Reger Tokabi, Sergeant Ezra Duke, Private Nora Armstrong and the combat android VUE-995. Lamorn records revealed that Green Energy, meant to heal the planet, had turned the Lamorn themselves into the feral Grievers. Sylux, gravely hurt on arrival, had healed in a pod inside the Chrono Tower, sealed it behind a barrier, sent Psy-bots disguised in his likeness against Samus and infused his Metroids into the key Guardians.

MacKenzie's rebuilt Federation mech Betsy breached the barrier. At the Pinnacle Sylux, changed by his long synchronization with the tower, ambushed the group, dragged Samus through a dimensional rift and was beaten by her in the void beyond. As she activated the Master Teleporter he returned and damaged it, so that it could hold only moments and answered only to Samus. Her five companions seized Sylux and held him down, and she teleported away alone. On a barren world she planted the Memory Fruit together with her Psychic Crystal; a tree sprang up, her white Lamorn Legacy Suit faded back to the Varia Suit, and she hung Tokabi's tag on its branches for the companions she had left behind. Their fate, and Sylux's, remain unknown.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://shinesparkers.net/metroid-prime-4-beyond-transcript/ · https://shinesparkers.net/features/the-lamorn/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/feature-metroid-prime-series-producer-kensuke-tanabe-speaks-to-japanese-magazine-nintendo-dream-confirms-retirement-successor-metroid-prime-4-story-inspiration-more/</sub>


### Prime 4: Vi-O-La and Sol Valley

*Keys:* Vi-O-La, Vi-O-La Suit, IC Suit, IC Mode, Sol Valley, Sacred Shrine, Sacred Shrines, Vahkuun, Varmis, Lamorn bike, Lamorn vehicle, Green Energy Crystals


The Vi-O-La is a Lamorn motorcycle that Samus Aran rode across Viewros (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). Built in the Lamorn Machine Era as a two-wheeled single-seat vehicle and redesigned in the Psychic Era, it recognizes the brain waves of a registered rider, who alone can use it and can summon it from anywhere unless impassable terrain blocks its path. It was prototyped and tested at the Volt Forge factory, where Samus acquired it together with the Vi-O-La Suit that registers her as its rider, and she first rode it on a Volt Forge test track before taking it onto the sands. The machine boosts to ram through enemies and ore deposits, power-slides through turns and crowds, and hurls an energy orb boomerang-fashion at up to five locked targets. Samus used it to gather the Green Energy Crystals strewn across the desert; the crystals are fragile and break under impact or gunfire, and their energy can be extracted only with the device on the altar of the Temple of Memory. Fitted with the Vi-O-La IC Suit it enters IC mode, triggered automatically by a surface sensor, and hovers over magma and lava, which let Samus cross the molten pools of Flare Pool. The Lamorn planned further transformations they never completed.

Sol Valley is the sun-scorched desert at the heart of Viewros, from which Fury Green, Volt Forge, the Ice Belt, Flare Pool and the Great Mines are all reached; the Chrono Tower floats above it. A Lamorn cargo launcher in Fury Green delivers travelers into the valley. The priest Vahkuun built shrines across it filled with Mote-driven devices, and the Sacred Shrines open onto the pocket dimension where the Master Teleporter Keys were hidden; Lamorn statues conceal underground chambers. The Green Energy showers the Lamorn fired over the valley from Flare Pool left its crystals unusually common and its wildlife swollen: Sand Grievers ambush from the dunes, and Varmis, a giant crustacean that swims through sand, swallowed a power core from the wreckage of the UTO Research Center. Reger Tokabi made his camp here, marked by the smoke of his fire, and Samus hauled Federation debris and mech parts from the sands to rebuild the mech Betsy.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Vi-O-La · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-technology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/boss-guide/</sub>


### Prime 4: Viewros

*Keys:* Viewros, planet Viewros, regions of Viewros, Fury Green, Volt Forge, Ice Belt, Flare Pool, Great Mines, Temple of Memory, Sacred Tree, Lamorn homeworld


Viewros is an uncharted planet on no known Federation star chart, lying beyond time and space in another dimension, to which Samus Aran, Sylux and five Federation personnel were hurled when a Lamorn artifact on Tanamaar activated (Metroid Prime 4: Beyond). It was the home of the Lamorn, a psychic civilization now extinct; their ruins, statues and machines cover its surface, and the Grievers, the mutated remnant of their people, roam it. Green Energy, once showered over the land by the Lamorn, has left many creatures swollen and aggressive.

Regions:
- Sol Valley: a sun-scorched desert at the planet's heart from which every other region is reached. The Chrono Tower floats above it; Lamorn shrines and statues conceal underground chambers. Anti-Griever war machines, the Aeronaut and the Marauder, still patrol it; the last Lamorn built them but detested turning weapons on Grievers, their own transformed kin.
- Fury Green: an ancient jungle holding the Temple of Memory and the Sacred Tree, where the Lamorn stored their memories. Myles MacKenzie set up his Base Camp here. The Guardian Carvex held its key.
- Volt Forge: a vehicle factory powered by the planet's lightning storms and run by Xelios, an artificial intelligence grown from organic cells. The Vi-O-La was prototyped and tested here.
- Ice Belt: a frozen research facility built after the Great Tragedy, where Lamorn scientists tried and failed to cure the Grievers; cryopods of frozen Grievers still line its halls. Snow Wolves hunt outside, and its key was held by Keratos, a giant once intelligent enough to communicate its will to the Lamorn priests.
- Flare Pool: a volcano facility that extracted Green Energy from magma and loaded it into shells fired over Sol Valley. The Guardian Phenoros dwells in its lava lake.
- Great Mines: the Lamorn mineral network where the Viewros Crystal, source of their psychic power, was discovered; now the hunting ground of the Maul Grievers and the Omega Griever.
- Chrono Tower: the floating sanctuary above Sol Valley housing the Master Teleporter.

Samus woke on Viewros stripped of most of her suit's capabilities and left it alone through the Master Teleporter; the five Federation companions who held Sylux back were left there, as was Sylux himself. She knows the planet as the place where she gained psychic abilities, lost her companions, and inherited the legacy of a dead people.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metroid_Prime_4:_Beyond · https://omegametroid.com/new-metroid-prime-4-beyond-details-revealed-on-the-games-japanese-website-spoiler-warning/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-biology/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-lamorn-legacy/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-prime-4-beyond-walkthrough/logbook-guide-machines/</sub>


## SR388


### SR388: Areas of SR388

*Keys:* Area 1, Area 2, Area 3, Area 4, Area 5, Area 6, Area 7, Area 8, Nest Chamber, caverns of SR388, SR388 caverns, purple liquid  
*Secondary (AND ANY):* SR388, SR-388, Metroid, Metroids, Chozo, Samus Returns, Diggernaut, nest


SR388's underworld is a descent through layered caverns, each area cut off from the next by corrosive purple liquid until its Chozo Seal receives enough Metroid DNA to drain it. Elevators join the areas, and teleporters let Samus return to any she has opened. This is the layout of the later retelling (Metroid: Samus Returns); in the original account the same descent opened through earthquakes as each area's Metroids died.

- Surface: Samus's landing site, a stormy waste of ledges and ruined passages where a dead Federation soldier lies near a door. One Alpha Metroid.
- Area 1: acid caverns giving way to a lush cave of water, waterfalls and plants around a Chozo settlement. Four Alpha Metroids.
- Area 2: watery caverns and Chozo ruins around a great machine installation, with a superheated zone and the lair of Arachnus. Eight Metroids, among them the first Gamma.
- Area 3: one of the largest areas, a maze of Chozo robotics and mining equipment where the Diggernaut lay buried. Ten Metroids, Alphas and Gammas.
- Area 4: crystal caverns lit by a crystalline glow, with a water pool, a superheated zone and the Diggernaut's tunnels. Four Metroids, including the first Zeta.
- Area 5: another of the largest areas, mostly water with fully submerged stretches, jungle growth and superheated zones. Seven Metroids.
- Area 6: purple acid caverns and orange-lit chambers lined with the skeletons of giants, with two seals; the first flooded the lower half as it opened a new path. A Zeta and an Omega Metroid, and the Diggernaut's final chamber.
- Area 7: the Chozo Laboratory, dark shafts and vat-filled chambers beneath a planetarium. Three Omega Metroids.
- Area 8: the Metroid nest, the only area without a seal. Passages wind through red weeds, crystals no weapon could break and pools of glowing liquid to the Nest Chamber, a vast tiered cavern above lakes of the same liquid. Ten newly hatched larval Metroids guard it, and beyond lies the Queen Metroid's chamber, strewn with hatched eggs.

Samus cleared every area of Metroids, forty with the Queen. Years later, escorting a BSL research team, she was infected by an X Parasite on SR388, and the caverns perished with the planet when the BSL station struck it (see The destruction of the BSL and SR388).


<sub>Sources: https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/ · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/return-to-sr388 · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-1-ancient-chozo-ruins · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-2-hydroelectric-station · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-3-aeion-mines · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-4-crystal-caverns</sub>


### SR388: Diggernaut

*Keys:* Diggernaut, Diggernauts, Chozo mining robot, Chozo mining machine, Chozo excavation machine, Chozo drill robot, Chozo digging machine


The Diggernaut is a colossal Chozo excavation machine that the colonists of SR388 left derelict in the planet's mining tunnels: a hulking body with a single red eye and two enormous drill arms. It is the most dangerous survivor of the colony's machine works. It belongs to the later retelling of Samus Aran's Metroid extermination mission (Metroid: Samus Returns); the original account does not mention it.

Samus woke it herself. In the mining complex of Area 3, soon after acquiring the Grapple Beam, she found it deactivated and blocking her path under fallen rock; pulling the rubble away brought it to life, and it withdrew into the depths. In Area 4 she found a mutilated Chozo Statue with empty hands, then dropped into a cave where the Diggernaut was tunneling. It came after her along its mining route, its drills fatal at a touch, until she pressed into an alcove and it passed; as it wandered away it ejected the component it had torn from the statue, the Space Jump. In Area 6, in an orange cavern lined with the skeletons of giants, a second Chozo Statue was revealed and the machine destroyed it at once, carrying off the Power Bomb it held.

Once Samus had cleared Area 6 of Metroids, she faced it in a great chamber there. It emerged from the rock and attacked with sweeping drills, a rotating eye laser that later split into three beams, ceiling smashes that rained purple acid, a lock-on missile, a suction vortex that dragged her toward it, triple ground slams with shockwaves and an eight-beam laser pattern. The vortex was also its weakness: it swallowed the Bombs she laid and was stunned. She bombed the exposed slots in its drill arms, and at the end clung to its faceplate with the Spider Ball and bombed the three pink nodes beneath; a last shot to the face brought it down. She recovered the Power Bomb from the wreck and used one to blast the carcass apart, clearing the way onward to Area 7.

It is the one Chozo machine on SR388 that stole from Samus: twice it destroyed a Chozo Statue before she could claim what it held (see The Chozo laboratories of SR388).


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid:_Samus_Returns · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-3-aeion-mines · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-4-crystal-caverns · https://omegametroid.com/metroid-samus-returns-walkthrough/area-6-diggernauts-domain</sub>


### SR388: SR388 (planet overview)

*Keys:* SR388, SR-388, SR 388, planet SR388, Metroid homeworld, Metroid home world, Metroid home planet, homeworld of the Metroids, Metroid planet


SR388 is a rocky frontier world in an unidentified star system near FS-176. It is the homeworld of the Metroids and of the X Parasites, and the site of a lost Chozo colony. Its surface is a stormy waste of rock under dark green cloud and lightning; the living planet lies beneath. Per the original manual, the underworld is "a complicated structure of multi-layered domes and spaces," some holding ruins of an unknown civilization and many native life forms, with a dangerous liquid filling the tunnels between areas. The depths descend through flooded grottos, crystal caverns, superheated chambers and Chozo mining tunnels to the Metroid nest at the bottom, and corrosive purple liquid seals the lower passages until the Chozo Seals drain it. The planet emits Aeion, an energy the Chozo first extracted from its creatures and learned to harness. Chozo ruins and laboratories lie deep underground, still patrolled by the machines the colonists left behind.

Long ago the Chozo colonized SR388. Its colonists, scientists of the Thoha tribe, found the X Parasites there, organisms that killed and copied the planet's creatures like a plague, and engineered the Metroids to hunt them. The Metroids nearly wiped out the X, then grew out of control and rose to the top of SR388's food chain. The Thoha and the warrior Mawkin tribe of Raven Beak joined forces to contain them on the planet. When the Thoha resolved to destroy SR388 to be rid of the Metroids, Raven Beak, who coveted them as weapons, massacred the Thoha and spared only the scientist Quiet Robe, intending to return once the Metroids had finished off the X. The colony was left in ruins, its story preserved in the Chozo Memories. Samus learned the tribes' names and the massacre only on ZDR, from Quiet Robe.

In cosmic year 20X5 a Galactic Federation deep-space research ship found a Metroid on SR388 and sealed a specimen in a stasis capsule; Space Pirates seized it in transit, which drew Samus Aran to Zebes. When the Federation's later expeditions to SR388 vanished, it ordered the species exterminated and gave the task to Samus. She hunted the Metroids through the caverns, killed the Queen Metroid, spared the newborn hatchling that took her for its mother, and fought off Proteus Ridley at her Gunship before leaving with it. Hornoads and Arachnus are among the native life (see Fauna of SR388).

With the Metroids gone, the X recovered; per the Fusion manual, they overran and infected most of the life-forms on the planet. Years later Samus returned as bodyguard to a Biologic Space Laboratories research team, and on the planet an X Parasite mimicking a Hornoad infected her. To end the X, she later altered the orbit of the BSL station, on the Adam AI's plan, so that it struck SR388, and the impact destroyed the planet (see The destruction of the BSL and SR388; Metroid Fusion). SR388 is the world where Samus spared the last Metroid, and the world she destroyed.


<sub>Sources: https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/SR388 · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Aeion · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Raven_Beak · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Quiet_Robe · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/X_Parasite · https://www.metroidwiki.org/wiki/Metroid_Hatchling</sub>


### SR388: The Chozo laboratories of SR388

*Keys:* Chozo Laboratory, Chozo Laboratories, Chozo Seal, Chozo Seals, Thoha colony, Thoha research colony, Chozo colony on SR388, Chozo settlement on SR388, SR388 ruins, ruins of SR388, Aeion Ability Artifact, Aeion Ability Artifacts


The Chozo who colonized SR388, scientists of the Thoha tribe, built downward. Their works riddle the planet from just beneath the surface to the Metroid nest, and when Samus Aran came to exterminate the Metroids she found them abandoned but still running.
